# Tips notebook

Everything learned from 82 games, with evidence. Built by `uv run tips.py build`; the players' sheet is distilled from the active entries.

## Active (375)

- [t5] (Everyone) Keep your votes consistent with your stated reads. If you change your mind, say why before the vote; an unexplained switch gets cited against you. Ballots are secret, so the record is your stated vote plus the announced totals. Village: a read/vote mismatch is a weak lead, not a verdict. Confused townies mismatch often, and a Fixer can flip a player's trial votes without telling them (nomination votes can't be fixed). Check a mismatch against claims, mechanical results and PM links. Never shoot or hang on a mismatch alone. Never hang a credible power role (e.g. a Veteran whose alerts killed visitors) over an old stated Yes on a nomination: a Yes only opens a trial. When a group pushes a mismatch case, count whether its Guilty votes alone could decide the trial, and ask who benefits. | support: 38 games, total weight 14.45 | against: 8 reports, total weight 1.97: Moderator: the read/vote mismatch scan led Dave to shoot Cardinal Grace, and her curse killed Alice and Frank.; Grace: Bob's misstated vote on his own nomination was a real slip, but the Yakuza turned it into the case that hanged a Vanilla. A mismatch is a lead, not a verdict.; Moderator: Dave and Grace hanged Vanilla Mallory on D6 at 5 alive mainly for two ballot/stance mismatches. That mis-hang handed the Yakuza 2 of 3. (The game had public ballots.); Ivan: the town treated Mallory's mismatch as near-proof and hanged her, despite the tip's warning.; Carol (Yakuza) filed 'hurt': the team's consistent, predictable reads made it look coordinated. The real problem was the D2 bloc vote, not consistency.; Bob filed 'hurt': his silent no-claim posture made nominations of Ivan and him predictable. Misapplication: the problem was refusing to claim, not vote consistency.; Grace and Heidi: the Village spent D3-D5 renominating on Carol's explained mismatch, and the Yakuza bloc turned the same mismatch argument against Vanilla Ivan and Grace.; Bob filed 'hurt': his self-nomination after saying 'no nomination' was used to dismiss his slot math. Misapplication: the tip predicts exactly that cost.; Judy filed 'hurt' because her stated-lean/vote mismatch got her nominated. Misapplication: she broke the tip, and the 'only a lead' clause got her acquitted. | recent evidence: Moderator, Alice, Grace, Ivan, Dave: the 6-2 total showed that Judy voted Guilty on Heidi after saying she leaned Innocent. The town treated it as a lead only, and Vanilla Judy was rightly acquitted 1-6 after admitting it.; Bob, Dave: stated reads tied to votes kept the record auditable.; Frank: keeping his own vote consistent and checking the totals (3 Yes, 5-1 Guilty) confirmed the counts were honest.; Dave and Frank: tracking stated votes against totals kept mismatches as leads. Frank's flip-flop Yes-then-Innocent drew a nomination until he explained it.; Ivan: the Yakuza kept stated and cast votes matched, so vote checks only confirmed them. Bob's stated No against 9 Yes of 9 was cited against him at once.; Grace: she kept stated and cast votes matched all game and was never treated as a swing vote or nominated.; Alice, Erin: Bob's Juror check found Erin's unstated D2 Yes, and the unexplained mismatch became a lead that fit the later SK hang.; Alice, Bob: Grace's stated Guilty against her cast Innocent was a real mismatch but an honest miscast. The table hanged on it alone, against the tip, and lost a Villager at 4 alive. Alice's 'hurt' is non-application.; Moderator: Alice announced Guilty on Frank and later claimed she had voted Innocent. Frank's Juror check (Alice and Dave voted the same way, and Dave voted Guilty) exposed it, the second pillar of her conviction.; Mallory (1.0) filed 'helped': Dave's No on Frank's nomination followed by Guilty in the trial, with no explanation, became the case against him.
- [t9] (Everyone) A visit shows a destination, not an action or alignment. A track or watch cannot tell protection from framing or a Katana attack. A Lookout sees only visitors to their target, so watching the Katana holder shows nothing about the holder's attack. Killing an attacker at night does not cancel their attack that same night. A Lookout never sees their own visit, but that only matters if the Lookout could be the holder. If the reporter has independent support (a Tracker confirms their visits, earlier reports checked out, the named visitors confirm themselves) and X is the only other visitor to a Katana victim, treat it as near-proof that X made the attack. The same applies when several visitors are named and all but X have confirmed harmless explanations: X is then the lone unexplained visitor. This holds even more when X first denied the visit, claims a role that doesn't fit, or was the sole visitor to victims on several nights. Don't let 'a visit isn't an attack' or the self-visit caveat acquit such a visitor. One Village explanation exists: a Villager with the Stolen Blade trait can make one attack a game whose kill reads 'Katana'. No investigation sees traits, so 'that was my Stolen Blade' is only a claim, and a Yakuza can make it too. It covers one night and never a second victim, so test the role claimed beside it and ask where the Yakuza's own Katana went that night. 'Corroborated' is the condition that matters. A lone-visitor report (watch, track or map) from one unverified claimant with no second source is only that claimant's word, and a Jester or Yakuza can invent exactly this pattern, 'denied visit' included. Give it no weight if it was held back, names the claimant's own accuser, or is contradicted by claimants who confirm each other (t296). | support: 43 games, total weight 18.11 | against: 3 reports, total weight 1.59: Moderator: the Lookout-can't-see-himself caveat was used to acquit Erin, the real Katana holder, on D3.; Ivan (Grunt, won): the Yakuza relied on the caveat as their core defense, and Erin was acquitted while she killed Peggy, Dave and Frank.; Heidi, Ivan, Niaj filed 'hurt': the Jester fabricated exactly the 'lone visitor who denied it' pattern with a fake map, and nine voters took it as near-proof with no second source.; Frank filed 'hurt': he says the visit line was used to attack Alice's Veteran claim. Weak: only Bob (Yakuza) made that argument, and the moderator says the table rejected it. The gap was that the tip didn't say a visitor can have a second destination; t357 now does. | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Dave, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia: Erin's watch plus the FT/Cartographer graph narrowed N1 to Erin or Heidi. Heidi's 'my visit was a check' didn't acquit her.; Moderator, Grace: the Diviner visitor was the only unexplained visitor to Katana victim Frank, corroborated by Bob's Insomniac list and Erin's map. The Village rejected Dave's 'the link could be Frank visiting me' defense and hanged him 4-1.; Alice, Frank: Alice's silent trap reports were treated as her word, not as a clearance of Heidi.; Alice, Dave, Frank, Carol (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Erin's never-fully-posted N3 'no visitors' and Ivan's late PM quote were treated as word only, and nobody chased Alice's and Bob's visits to Carol beyond the count.; Dave filed 'helped': Erin's track of Carol visiting Dave corroborated the Sheriff check and was the reason he trusted the read.; Erin, Grace (0.16 each): Erin treated Heidi's uncorroborated N4 'no visitors' watch as a check and leaned Innocent on it. Heidi was the Arsonist; the claim was only her word.; Grace filed 'helped': Alice's late Tracker claim, made after she had denied any action, was treated as her word only until the audit tested it.; Alice, Carol (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Dave's watch showed that Grace visited Erin, not that Erin was her only destination. The table read it that way and kept Alice's Veteran account.; Frank, Grace, Erin (0.16 each): a 'visited nobody' track rules out seen visits only; a blocked or no-action player shows the same, so it cleared nobody and couldn't tell the two Tracker claims apart.; Dave, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Frank's track showed only that Dave visited Grace, which fit his Trapper claim, and the table acquitted him 5-2. Frank adds that the town still spent two Days on it.
- [t24] (Everyone) Settle duplicate claims with independent corroboration (a Lookout or Tracker result confirming the same destination, an audit, an exact-role check), not repetition or speaking style. Build a positive case for each claimant early. Roles stay hidden on death, so no flip will settle it for you. | support: 7 games, total weight 3.59 | recent evidence: Judy, Niaj and Heidi all noted this. Mallory's and Dave's results were what resolved Grace vs Frank.; Alice and Grace: settle duplicate Medic claims with a watch or track on each claimant's claimed nights, not with vote history.; Olivia, Ivan, moderator: the Frank/Ivan Scrutineer duplicate was never settled with an independent check or a role-only detail, and the real holder was hanged D4.; Alice, Frank (0.16 each): the Alice/Bob Cartographer duplicate sat for a Day waiting on the next night's sizes. Erin's profile of Bob was the independent check that told them apart. Frank's 'wrong' is non-application.; Alice: the Trapper duplicate was settled on pushes and a dead player's will, with no independent check on either claim. Both claimants were real.; Judy, Niaj, Heidi: the Judy/Carol Party Animal duplicate was settled by independent checks (Ivan confirming Judy's block, Ivan's WRONG on Carol, the Enforcer kill), not by who argued better.; Carol, Dave, Erin (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the Bob/Frank Tracker duplicate was tested, not settled on style. The PM-coaching admission and Bob's defense of Dave broke Bob's side, and the shot confirmed it. Erin and Grace note that two 'visited nobody' tracks can't separate the claimants.
- [t26] (Everyone) Scrutinize a player who stays silent for days and then suddenly produces a concrete accusation right as the board changes. The timing of the pivot is the signal. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.19 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.16: Frank: Ivan's late Oracle claim was true, and suspicion over its timing helped get him hanged. Vote patterns, not timing, found the Yakuza. | recent evidence: Bob went from four days of 'no nomination' to pushing Heidi right after Frank flipped.; Ivan and Mallory: the timing of Carol's late Archivist claim and Mallory's will drew useful scrutiny.; Heidi went quiet, then made an aggressive endgame nomination of Erin, and was hanged.
- [t29] (Everyone) Since private messages are publicly visible, briefly summarize their content in public. Unexplained PM clusters right after claims or votes became a main source of suspicion. | support: 6 games, total weight 1.43 | recent evidence: Erin, Grace and Dave all noted the PM graph being used as evidence.; Erin and Ivan: Erin's audit-request PM to Heidi became a public point of suspicion.; Grace: PMs sent to voters on a suspect's Day read as coordination unless explained at once.; Frank, Heidi (0.16 each): the PM cluster around Heidi, Ivan and Dave was the town's fixation. Ivan's late quote of Heidi's PM read as evasion; a prompt public summary was needed.; Frank, Bob, Erin (0.16 each): the unexplained Bob-Dave PMs on D5, both Village, became Heidi's trial defense. - [t30] (Everyone) Before accusing anyone from public data, re-check the ledger: who sent each private message (not just who received it), each player's stated vote against the announced totals, the exact wording of role results, and each death's announced cause against the dead player's last will. Roles are never revealed on death, so a will is only the dead player's claim, not proof: a Fabricator can forge it and a Mortician can hide it. One reversed fact can discredit an otherwise true claimant. | support: 8 games, total weight 2.57 | recent evidence: Six players and the moderator flagged this. Erin, the real Lookout, reversed who messaged Carol, lost credibility and was hanged 3-1.; Moderator, Grace and Ivan: Dave's transcript check corrected a reversed timeline about when Grace named her visitors, which saved the real Lookout on D2.; Bob and Erin: re-verifying the Oracle reports and the ledger kept Grace's and Bob's cases honest, and Rupert corrected a false 'Erin never explained her vote' claim.; Judy: Mallory's claim that Judy never answered Carol was checked against the transcript and corrected, which stopped a D1 mislynch pile-on.; Alice, Frank, Grace, Peggy, Ivan, Dave: Ivan's misread claim that Alice voted Innocent on Frank was corrected from the ledger, and she survived.; Frank and Ivan: keep nomination and trial ballots in separate, accurate records. A misstated vote history nearly drove endgame accusations.; Dave and Judy: Dave's forged Sheriff will contradicted his Tracker reveal. The town treated the reveal as authoritative and the will as forged.; Mallory and the moderator: Mallory nominated Bob citing a Judy visit report that was never posted. The false detail collapsed the D4 trial on the real holder.
- [t47] (Everyone) Get the mechanics right before calling something a contradiction. Night actions are optional unless a role says 'must'. Two players can claim the same role unless the rules make it unique, or unless a role list plus confirmed roles (self-proving effects, exact-role checks) accounts for every player. Once every unshown slot is filled that way, a role listed once exists exactly once, and a second claimant is a provable liar. Roles stay hidden on death, so a dead player's slot stays open unless their role was confirmed. But 'roles can repeat' only means a claim isn't disproved. It is not evidence for either claimant, and both can be fake: a tokenless visiting role such as Tracker is the cheapest cover for a Yakuza and for a Neutral killer alike. 'Test both' means running a test that Day or night, not accepting both and moving on: ask each to target a player whose visits are known, line their claimed visits up against the visit counts, and aim a watch, an exact-role check or a mark at one of them. A duplicate where neither role is on a credible list and neither claimant has produced a checkable result is a suspect pool of two (t373). A claim of the exact role a dead victim had claimed, made by a player seen visiting that victim the night they died, is still close to a confession: weigh it with the other evidence and don't acquit on the bare possibility. Count living players and elapsed nights as of the right moment: on Day 2 only Night 1 has passed, so 'name the night of your result' is not a contradiction, and demanding it repeatedly only creates false certainty. A false 'contradiction' costs the accuser credibility, and a real one dismissed as 'roles aren't unique' frees a Yakuza. | support: 43 games, total weight 17.82 | against: 6 reports, total weight 3.17: Moderator: the 'roles aren't unique' caution was misapplied to a full census and nearly acquitted the fake Sheriff Heidi.; Moderator (0.4), Ivan and Alice (0.16 each): the 'roles can repeat' caution was the stated reason two Good voters acquitted Pharmacist Erin's fake Sheriff claim on D2, which cost Heidi's life.; Alice filed 'hurt': quoting 'duplicates are legal' did not save her late claim, whose result repeated public information. Weak: she says the tip is correct, and the cost came from claiming a Day late (t1) and from the table ignoring it.; Heidi filed 'hurt': the 'check the rules before calling a contradiction' line made her hesitate over Veteran Alice's true account. A misreading: Bob was the one calling a false contradiction, and the hidden-trait explanation is what the tip asks for. t357 now covers multiple visits.; Alice filed 'wrong' on the 'a duplicate claimant is often real' line: here the second Tracker (Bob) was a Pharmacist's cover, and she says the framing kept both claims level until the shot. Weak: Carol, Dave and Erin filed 'helped' on the same line, and testing both found the liar.; Alice, Carol, Erin, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj filed 'hurt' and Dave 'wrong' (1.0 each) on the sheet's 'a duplicate claimant is often real' line: Frank (Arsonist) and Grace (Diviner) both claimed Tracker, the town accepted both because duplicates are legal, tested neither, and both lived to Day 4. Frank and Grace filed 'helped' from the hostile side: the line acquitted Frank 3-9 on D2. Partly non-application (the entry says 'not evidence for it' and 'test both'), but the sheet line dropped that. | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Bob, Erin (0.16 each): Grace and Bob, both real Snipers, each called the other fake on D2 and fired. The Village lost a Sniper and 2 tokens with no Yakuza involved.; Moderator filed 'helped': Erin and Heidi were both real Kitsunes. Heidi called Erin fake because 'I know my own role' and released her held SK attack on her on N3, killing a Village Kitsune. Alice, Frank, Grace and Heidi filed the same lesson.; Moderator: on D2 the table kept asking for a 'Night 2 result' when only Night 1 had passed. Erin and Heidi corrected it twice and the Day was lost to the confusion.; Moderator filed 'helped' as decisive when ignored: Alice and Bob were both real Trappers. The table repeated 'only one can be the Trapper', Grace released her held SK attack on Bob and killed a Villager, and Alice was hanged D3 on Bob's will.; Bob, Erin, Judy, moderator (1.0 each) filed 'helped': the table accepted two Tracker and two Party Animal claims as possible and checked R9 on blocker feedback, which exposed Carol's 'a PA isn't told' slip. When it called Erin's protect-then-suspect a 'contradiction' without checking, it hanged the real Medic.; Alice ('helped'), Erin (0.16 each): Erin nominated Grace believing a Talent Agent has two uses; it has three, so the 0-token audit fit the claim. Checking the role text removed a false contradiction, while the real one (the role's faction) went unnoticed.; Bob, Dave, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Dave pointed out that a Juror does have a night action, which stopped Grace calling Frank's claim impossible. Checking the role text separated the real contradiction (Alice's tokens) from noise.; Bob, Carol, Erin, Grace, Heidi, Mallory, moderator (1.0 each) filed 'helped' as not applied: Alice cited 'only Smuggler and Vulture are Unique' for her second Auditor claim and was right; the table treated the duplicate as fake and she was shot.; Every player and the moderator filed 'helped': Bob and Mallory were both real Auditors. Treating the clash as proof got Bob killed and fed the hang of real Profiler Dave; only Carol said two Auditors were possible.; Moderator (0.4), Erin (0.16) filed 'helped': Carol, Dave and Erin corrected Frank's claim that a Sheriff Not Evil and a Tracker 'no visit' on Bob contradicted each other, and on D3 the table rejected Bob's 'the visits make Alice's alert impossible' by citing possible extra visits.
- [t69] (Everyone) Fortune Teller numbers are labeled: players visited (distinct) and players who visited anyone (distinct). Every visiting action counts, including Lookout watches, Tracker tracks, Accountant audits, Medic protections, Fabricator frames and the Katana. Self-visits count in both. A Veteran's alert isn't a visit, but anyone who visits the Veteran is. Blocked and Astral actions (Oracle, Ritualist, ignite, map, Coroner exam, Jester or Executioner revenge) count in neither. Players who died that night still count. One visitor can add several destinations: a Katana holder who also uses their own visiting action, a Smuggler (a swap visits both players), a Seer's chosen pair (visits both) or a Villager who adds a Stolen Blade attack to their role's action. A player with the Sleepwalker trait visits one random other player every night on top of any action of their own, so a no-action role can still show as a visitor. Read small counts literally. On a night with a Katana death, a visitor total of 1 means one player made every visit that night, the attack included: the holder, or once a game a Villager using the Stolen Blade trait, whose kill also reads 'Katana'. Anyone who claims a visit that night is then that attacker or is lying, and a player who refuses to state their night action, or gives only a bare denial, is the hang. A totals-only role can't name visitors, so 'the FT's visitors are unnamed' is not a flaw, and evil uses that line to hang a real FT. FT: line your totals up publicly against every claimed visit, then ask each player with no claimed action for a plain yes/no on the leftover visit. A total that doesn't fit shows that some set of claims is false, not which claim. List every account that repairs it (holder acting twice, a swap, a chosen Seer pair, a Sleepwalker's random visit, a Stolen Blade attack, douses, switched plans). Outside the one-visitor case, never hang on the count alone, least of all against an exact-role check. Ask for the totals per night and the derivation before calling a count a lie. | support: 11 games, total weight 5.49 | recent evidence: Alice and Carol: keeping the counts as unordered totals avoided false certainty and avoided a hang on counts alone.; Dave, Erin, Judy: listing every visitor (Katana holder, audits, tracks) was needed to reconcile Ivan's 2/2 and 2/3 with Alice's group.; Ivan and Alice: Dave wrongly claimed the Fortune Teller had a night action, and Ivan's 2,2 was dismissed rather than reconciled against visits.; Bob, Alice, Judy: reconciling Heidi's 3/3 and 4/4 required counting the Lookout's own visit correctly. Most of the 'open' visits came from counting errors.; Mallory: FT totals are a consistency check that rejects claims needing too many visits, not proof of a named visit.; Moderator, Mallory, Heidi: the N1 2/2 fit Bob's watch plus Grace's divine and left no room for Judy's claimed check. The N4 and N5 1/1 counts on Katana nights made the lone visitor the holder, and Heidi's N3-N4 denials and skipped N5 answer hanged her.; Moderator, Alice: Alice's N3 4/4 minus the claimed visits (Bob, Grace, Carol) left Frank or Heidi. Her N2 counts plus Ivan's trio left Dave, Heidi or Judy. Heidi, on both lists, was the holder. Alice adds: test every split of the unlabeled numbers before committing.; Carol's counts (e.g. 3/6) lined up against claimed visits corroborated Erin's lone visitor and left no room for hidden Yakuza visits.; Moderator (0.4), Heidi (0.16): Ivan, Heidi, Dave and at first Grace counted Erin's alert as a visit and argued Alice or Bob lied about visiting Carol. Day 2 passed with no nomination until Bob cited R6. List visitors and visited by name before arguing a count.; Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj and the moderator: the N1 count (7 visitors, 4 visited) was first read as naming Frank alone. Frank and Dave showed that a holder acting twice and a Sleepwalker left three fits, and he was acquitted 3-9; the real break was Bob's fake visit. Niaj adds that a count matching the claims shows only that the lies balance, not that each claim is true.
- [t79] (Everyone) Before each night, write your planned target into your will (e.g. 'N2 plan: audit X') and, beside it, what each possible result would mean ('pair Bob+Carol: if DIFFERENT, Bob is not Good'), so your death still yields info and can set the town's next hang. The result of the night you die never reaches your will: results and deaths resolve together, and you can't edit at night. So post high-value results publicly while you're alive, and keep the will to past results plus tonight's plan and its readings. Label plans as plans and hedge accusations: a will can't be updated after death, and a confident line can hang a villager. Don't leave in it a suspicion that a hang has since settled (the other half of a Seer pair whose non-Good half was hanged): it can get a Good player shot. Town: a will's 'plan' is not proof the action happened, since players switch targets. But a claimed investigator killed on the night their will names a check on X was probably killed to stop it: treat X as a strong lead and bring a second pointer to the vote. | support: 48 games, total weight 24.48 | against: 5 reports, total weight 2.48: Olivia: against a Mortician the N1 and N2 victims' wills were cleaned, so posting results publicly by day mattered more than the will.; Dave filed 'wrong', saying will-planned targets made his reads predictable. Weak: the moderator notes the opposite, since his N3 map on Judy was lost because his will didn't name it. The revisions he cites were public posts, not will plans.; Niaj filed 'wrong': his will held results but no plan, and he saw no benefit. Weak: he didn't follow the plan clause.; Frank filed 'wrong': his will couldn't carry his N2 track because he died that night. Misreading: the tip already says the result of the night you die never reaches the will.; Olivia (1.0) filed 'hurt': her current will was replaced by a Fabricator forgery the night she died, so only her public statements survived. A real limit the tip doesn't mention; t52 now carries the forgery check and the 'say leans in public' line. | recent evidence: Heidi and Niaj (1.0 each): Heidi died N2 with no target in her will; 'N2: tracking Dave' would have pointed at the fake Tracker. Niaj died N1 with a 'no results yet' will that gave the Village nothing. Ivan's matching will entries backed each posted result.; Bob, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Bob's will carried his protections and his suspicion of Ivan, and Frank's carried his targets and results, which the table read after his death.; Olivia, Grace, moderator: Grace's will set out her remaining scent readings, including one naming Olivia, and the town built the D5 case on it. Alice adds a limit: a will that only points elsewhere doesn't replace posting your evidence while alive.; Bob and Erin: speculative accusations in wills (Bob naming the Katana target and her protector as Yakuza) misled the town after death. Keep wills to facts and label leads as weak.; Dave, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Heidi's will carried her N1-N3 targets and blocks to the table. Dave's N2 Evil read on Erin came on the night he died and never reached his will or the table.; Carol, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Carol's will carried her N1 track of Bob and her N2 target, with the N2 result marked as unavailable, so the table had the context after her N2 death.; Erin, Mallory, Niaj filed 'helped': Erin's will explained why her scent went on Dave, which kept the pressure on him. Her N2 count (3 attacks among Dave/Judy/Olivia) came the night she died and never reached the town. Her fix: write in the will what each possible result would mean.; Moderator (0.4), Frank (0.16): Frank's N2 track of Bob came on the night he died and never reached his will or the table.; Bob, Dave, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Bob's will gave his Surveyor role and target, Frank's carried his tracks, and Dave's carried his trap placement and the Frank-Erin track. Bob's N1 survey result came the night he died and never reached the table.; Alice, Carol, Dave, Ivan, Judy, Niaj, Olivia and the moderator filed 'helped': Ivan was killed on N2 before his result, but his will named the Bob+Carol pair and how to read each result, and with Olivia's census it hanged Bob. Olivia's N3 census (would have named Grace) died with her.
- [t85] (Everyone) On trial, lead with one concrete, checkable alternative explanation of the evidence, e.g. the exact visit assignment that fits the numbers. A counter-accusation reads as deflection; a worked example that collapses the case can move votes. | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Ivan's reflection on his hanging over FT counts.; Niaj: on trial he attacked Olivia's FT arithmetic. It didn't save him, but it became the case that hanged the Arsonist the next Day.
- [t88] (Everyone) Anyone fabricating a night account (Yakuza, Neutral killers) must test it against the global visit arithmetic, not just individual witnesses. Keep a ledger of claimed visitors, distinct destinations and token counts. Claiming protection on an already crowded target erases the extra destination your real kill created. Never report a blank or 'held' action on a night your side killed: an empty night is exactly the slot a holder needs, and the town pins the count's gap on it. Claim a concrete action for that night, posted before anyone asks, on a target whose visits fit the counts. Yakuza: choose the Katana holder so the night's visits match the team's public claims. A holder whose own action hits the kill target counts as one visitor. | support: 3 games, total weight 3.00 | recent evidence: Alice, Olivia and the moderator: the holders were chosen deliberately, and Judy's counts fit the Yakuza stories.; Bob and Frank: with an FT and a Cartographer posting, every unexplained Yakuza visit (Katana or own action) became a counted slot. A Diviner claiming no visits must skip, or pick a target already explained.; Bob and the moderator: the holder should be the member whose claimed visit lands on the victim. A no-visit holder added an exposed edge.
- [t120] (Everyone) Argue comparative likelihood, not mere mechanical possibility. A legal alternative explanation is not evidence that it happened, and 'some other claim could be false' rarely beats several converging clues. Don't count several consequences of one disputed claim as independent confirmations. On defense, name the specific weak premise, explain why your account fits better, and offer a concrete test that tells them apart. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.16 | recent evidence: Carol, Ivan, Frank, Rupert, Niaj, Dave and Grace all made this point. Bob, Judy and Heidi's 'mechanically possible' defenses all failed.; Rupert's 'that claim is uncheckable, test it first' failed against a self-testing Oracle hit; Heidi's 'mechanically possible' second-Pharmacist defense failed too.; Alice and Bob: repeating 'unverified' changed no votes. Name the exact conditional link and ask the accused to disprove it, or offer a concrete alternative explanation.
- [t132] (Everyone) Keep exact-role results separate from alignment. An Oracle WRONG rules out only the guessed role, not a faction, and a Roleplayer shows as 'Roleplayer' to exact-role checks. A RIGHT clears alignment only if the guessed role is a Village role. Use the miss to test the target's claim: it convicts when it disproves their claimed role and they then change their story. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.62 | recent evidence: Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Rupert, Judy and Bob: Dave's wrong guess 'Bob = Pharmacist' was correctly not read as Evil, and Bob (Cardinal) was spared. His wrong guess 'Erin = Vanilla' disproved Erin's claim, and her pivots then convicted her.; Judy: a correct guess confirms the role only. Her Jester RIGHT on Carol voided Carol's fake Sheriff result.
- [t137] (Everyone) Know which claimed results are automatic (a Seer's nightly pair) and which need tokens or actions. Ask promptly about an unreported automatic result: it is a concrete, testable question. But treat the gap as something to be explained, not proof of a lie, and remember random pairs can repeat. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.17 | recent evidence: Erin, Ivan, Frank, Heidi, Dave: Heidi's missing N2 pair was pressed, and she was saved once she posted it. Frank and Heidi noted that repeated pairs are legal.
- [t141] (Everyone) Get the reason for a kill or a missing kill right before arguing from it. A role-block on the victim doesn't stop the attack on them; only blocking the Katana holder does. A kill can also fail to protection, a trap, a charm, innate Defense, the hidden Narrow Escape trait (once per player) or a Shaky Katana holder. List every explanation before building a case or a cover story on one. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.18 | recent evidence: Niaj wrongly argued that Olivia's block on Alice cancelled the Katana, which hurt his credibility. Olivia, Ivan and Heidi warned against overconfident causal stories.
- [t142] (Everyone) A Philanthropist's gift notice tells the recipient only that some Philanthropist acted, not who. A claimed recipient's 'confirmation' doesn't verify a particular Philanthropist claimant, and a token count or will written before the gift may be out of date. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.34 | recent evidence: Dave, Mallory and Olivia all stressed reading gift feedback literally.; Erin: track gifts as submitted actions, and record separately what the recipient's later account or will actually confirms.
- [t146] (Everyone) Before accepting a chain of claims that corroborate each other, list which links are independently verified. Verified links include a self-proving action, a token audit, a tracked visit, an Oracle check, a survived attack that matches a Medic's claimed protection, and a block notice confirmed by its target. A chain with no verified link is a story, not evidence. Name which single link could be tested tonight and what would still follow if it were false. Don't swing to ignoring the map, though. If its links hold up and it leaves one living candidate, that candidate stays the top case. Don't acquit them and then spend the hang on someone whose checkable record is clean but whose nominations misfired. If you won't hang the candidate today, block or track them that night. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.36 | recent evidence: Olivia, Judy, Grace and Erin: Niaj's claimed Strong protection on Erin went untested, and the Erin/Niaj chain stood on one unverified claim for four days.; Carol: the D3 hang of Vanilla Carol rested on a chain of a survey pool plus an unverified Sheriff claim. Test one link directly first.; Heidi, Niaj: Dave's fake Tracker result fit Carol's fake block claim, a chain with no independent link. The links that held were those confirmed by a third party (Ivan and Bob confirming Judy's and Carol's blocks, Frank confirming Olivia's charm).
- [t152] (Everyone) A Veteran on alert Strong-attacks every non-Astral visitor and is told how many visited and whether the attack on each succeeded. So a living player who visited a claimed Veteran on a claimed alert night is a direct test. Ask the claimed Veteran for each alert night's notice (visitor count, whether each attack succeeded): a living visitor on a night they reported no visitors proves the claim false. A report of a visitor whose attack failed fits a visitor with Strong Defense that night (a Medic's Strong token, a Diviner's pick) or the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which saves any player once (R24). Investigators who visited a claimed Veteran should say so publicly as a test of the alert story. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.45 | recent evidence: Ivan audited Erin on N4 and survived, while Erin said she alerted that night; with the 0-token audit this hanged the Serial Killer D5. Bob, Erin, Ivan and the moderator reported this.
- [t159] (Everyone) If one player is killed in one night by two attacks with different killers' causes (e.g. Serial Killer and Katana), two independent attackers acted that night. Keep both in the suspect pool and don't assume one killer explains every death. Exception: a 'hidden gun' cause beside a Katana cause shows no second killing side. The gun is the Armed trait, which a Yakuza can now hold as well as a Villager, so the two attacks may come from two Yakuza, from one Armed Katana holder, or from the holder and an Armed Villager. Second exception: a 'Katana' cause is not always the Yakuza's. A Villager with the Stolen Blade trait makes one attack a game whose kill reads 'Katana', so two Katana deaths in one night (a Spare Blade or a Stolen Blade) don't show two holders, and one stray Katana death doesn't prove a living Yakuza. | support: 5 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Judy: Bob died to both the Serial Killer and the Katana on the same night, and Erin (SK) kept killing after the Yakuza fell.; Frank: on N5 the ignite, the Katana and the Cardinal curse all fired together. Check every death's cause before assuming a single killer.; Heidi: Frank died on N4 to both the ignite and the Katana, and the revealed causes showed two killers.; Frank, Grace, Ivan, Judy: the N4 multi-death with no Katana explanation pointed to an Arsonist ignite.; Alice: Grace's N2 death showed both a Katana and a Serial Killer cause, which proved two separate killers alive on D3.
- [t162] (Everyone) A night with no Katana kill right after a blocker hit player X is a lead that X holds the Katana. It is strongest when only one Yakuza can be left (Sheriff checks then read the holder Not Evil anyway). Verify the blocker first: the blocked player confirms the block publicly, earlier blocks were confirmed by independent players, or the only other blocker role (Pharmacist) is already exposed by a mechanical result. A lone holder can skip a night on purpose to frame the block target, and a kill can also fail to protection, so one match is weak. Two or more matches with a confirmed blocker log is a real mechanical case to hang on, and so is one match plus an exact-role result that breaks X's claim (an Oracle WRONG on their claimed role). Ask what each side would have done: at 4 alive a Yakuza pair would have killed and then held the vote, and an unblocked lone holder had a kill available, so a quiet night there points at the blocked player and rules out 'the blocker and X are a pair'. The same reading works for a Neutral killer who is blocked on a night with no death of their cause. | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Ivan blocked Mallory N2 and Heidi N4, both no-kill nights; Olivia confirmed her N3 block after Pharmacist Niaj had flipped. Heidi was hanged D5 as the last Yakuza. Reported by Ivan, Dave, Erin, Grace, Judy, Carol, Frank, Olivia and the moderator; Alice added the skip-to-frame caveat.; The N4 no-kill night on which only Frank was blocked was central to his D5 hang. Niaj, Grace and the moderator called it near-proof.
- [t175] (Everyone) When asking the town to hang on your single unverifiable log or result, commit publicly: 'if the night kills or later results contradict my log, hang me next.' Roles stay hidden on death, so name in advance what would show you were wrong. It costs an honest claimant little and makes the town willing to act today. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.50 | recent evidence: Ivan's reflection on the D5 Heidi hang on his block log.; Alice: an 'if the Katana kills again, X is next' fallback kept the town unified behind early nominations.
- [t181] (Everyone) In an endgame of three with a Jester alive (a killer, which may be the last Yakuza or a solo Neutral, plus a Villager and the Jester), the Jester's vote decides every trial. Hanging the Jester gives him his win and removes no killer, and the killer then kills the last Villager that night. Village: never vote Guilty on the Jester there. Make the killer's hang the only goal and win the Jester's vote by negotiating, not with ultimatums. Pass his self-nominations early (a Yes only starts the trial), so the killer must vote publicly on any hang he promised, and expose a broken promise while you still have votes. If the numbers allow hanging the Jester without leaving you alone with the killer, that is also an option, because his win doesn't block yours. Killer: you can buy the Jester's Innocent by promising a Guilty on his next self-nomination, but weigh the revenge draw on Guilty voters. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.85 | recent evidence: Moderator, Niaj, Olivia, Mallory: Olivia and Mallory voted No on every Carol self-nomination D8-D9. Rupert's promise went untested until D10, when he voted Innocent three times, and he then ignited the rest.; Moderator: Ivan's D7 hang left Bob (last Yakuza), Grace and Jester Frank; Frank would never vote Guilty on Bob, so Grace was doomed.
- [t183] (Everyone) Before each endgame trial, work out each consequence separately. Who is left after the death? Does the R1 check end the game? Roles that never block wins are ignored (Jester, Executioner, Bounty Hunter, Magpie); every other Neutral (Serial Killer, Arsonist, Vulture) keeps the game going, so a game that continues doesn't show a Yakuza is alive. Is a Jester or Executioner win earned? A hang wins for them however the game ends. Can revenge still happen? It needs a following night and a living Guilty voter. Never accept an opponent's version of these rules. | support: 8 games, total weight 2.33 | recent evidence: Bob, Carol, Dave, Heidi, Judy, Olivia, Erin, Mallory, Frank: several players argued the R1 and Jester-win interaction wrongly in the D8-D10 endgame.; Dave and Mallory: with two players alive, a nomination needs both Yes votes. Dave couldn't force Mallory onto trial, and she ignited him.; Carol, Dave, Alice: with two alive a nomination needs both votes, so Carol could never force Mallory onto trial and was ignited.; Heidi and Mallory: at two alive a nomination needs both Yes votes, so Mallory could never hang Arsonist Heidi and was ignited.; Moderator, Grace, Bob, Frank, Alice: with two alive a nomination needs both Yes votes. Grace nominated Carol four times on D5 for 1 Yes each and was killed.; Carol, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each): at two alive (Carol and SK Heidi) no nomination could pass, the Katana couldn't hurt Heidi, and she killed Carol. All three say the count had to be done at 5 alive.; Alice, Bob, Dave (0.16 each): at two alive (Alice and Carol) no nomination could pass without a self-vote, so the hang at 3 alive on D6 was the last one that could decide the game.; Bob (0.16): at two alive his nomination of Ivan failed 1-1 because Ivan voted No, and the Katana killed him that night. The count had to be done before the D4 nomination vote.
- [t188] (Everyone) Answer a direct question about your night action in one plain sentence the first time it is asked. Evasion repeated over days becomes a case of its own, even when the true answer is harmless. If the full answer would name your exact role while a Ritualist may be alive, give one short, true answer (whether you acted, and that the details are in your will until they bear on a vote), repeat it word for word, and give the full claim as soon as it does bear on one. Yakuza and Neutrals: before Day 2, prepare your answer to the obvious yes/no questions ('did you hold the Katana?', 'did you act last night?') and give it flatly the first time, as a Villager would. 'No claim, I want data' repeated for a Day is a case with no result behind it. Never deny a visit that a result could expose (t93). | support: 21 games, total weight 10.16 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.16: Frank filed 'hurt': his late, three-version answer was used against him. Misapplication: he broke the tip. | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Dave, Erin (0.16 each): Erin's 'no night action' before 'Vanilla Villager' became Frank's main case for three Days. Erin's 'hurt' on the no-switch clause is a misapplication, since she lacked a ready claim.; Moderator, Carol, Frank: Grace never gave one plain answer about her chat slip, and the repeated non-answers became the case that hanged her.; Carol: players who gave bare 'no action' answers were pushed repeatedly, and plain early answers were harder to target.; Moderator (0.4), Dave, Grace, Erin (0.16 each): Erin's two dodges about her role drew the whole table's suspicion onto a Good Scrutineer. Erin's 'hurt' is a misapplication: she broke the tip.; Moderator, Alice, Bob, Erin, Frank, Grace: Carol went from 'no night action, not the Diviner' to Smuggler to vague N2 answers, and that shifting story was the basis of her 5-2 nomination and 4-3 hang. Dave's 'I'll share my result when it's most useful' also drew pressure until he claimed.; Moderator: Ivan was asked over D3-D4 to quote Heidi's PM, delayed until nominated for it, and answered 'no role claim' at trial. The evasion was the whole case.; Frank, Carol, Erin (0.16 each): Auditor Frank described Night 3 as 'audit pending', 'no submitted audit' and 'no action'. The harmless gap got him nominated and cost the Village Day 4.; Carol: her dodge of the direct N4 visit question and Heidi's long trial defenses both read as guilt. A one-line answer would have cost less.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each): Bob dodged 'did you hold the Katana?' about eight times on D2. Erin nominated him for it on D3, and Alice, Dave, Erin and Heidi all cited it in their Guilty reasoning.; Moderator (0.4): Carol answered 'no claim' all game with no cover prepared. Alice nominated her for it on D3 and shot her for it on N3; only hidden Narrow Escape kept her alive.
- [t192] (Everyone) Check every claimed neighbor-group result (Surveyor, Diviner) against the circle as it stood at the start of that night. Remove the dead, skip the user's own seat, and walk both directions to the nearest eligible player. Write the steps out publicly. A group with the wrong neighbors exposes a fake claim at once, and it is a clean mechanical contradiction. A Census Taker's group is different: its target plus two players drawn at random from everyone else alive, so no seating check applies to it. Check for a swap notice too: a swapped target's group is built around the other player (R23). A wrong 'impossible' call puts a true investigator on trial and costs the accuser credibility the Yakuza will use later, so get the steps right before calling it. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator, Alice, Bob, Dave, Heidi, Carol: Dave's skip-rule error sent Surveyor Carol to trial on D2. Grace later used the retraction against Dave.
- [t195] (Everyone) Before every trial vote, compute the Guilty majority (strictly more than half of all votes counted) and how many votes would flip the result. A tie acquits. On a close trial of a strong suspect, a single Innocent from a Good voter on a technicality hands the Yakuza another night. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Ivan: Erin's 4-4 D2 trial acquitted her, and Heidi died that night. Two Good Innocent votes decided it.; Carol: Dave's first D4 trial tied 2-2, which acquitted the SK. One miscast vote decided it.
- [t210] (Everyone) When two surveys (or Diviner groups) both show the same role and the groups share one player, that player is the likeliest holder, not a proven one. Each result samples one random member, and roles can repeat. Weigh it as odds: unless the role is common, the shared player is far likelier than anyone else. Don't let 'not proof' acquit them; combine it with their claim, token count and votes, and hang if nothing better explains it. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.45 | recent evidence: Heidi's N1 and N3 surveys both showed Clerk and overlapped only on Bob. Bob, Dave, Judy, Mallory and Ivan correctly said it wasn't proof. But Bob was the Clerk and admitted it, and the moderator says the town should have hanged him on D4.
- [t211] (Everyone) Before each vote, re-read your own private vote reminder and make sure your ballot matches the stance you just posted; say 'as of now' if you might change it. Ballots are secret, but announced totals are compared with stated votes. A stated Innocent that the totals show was Guilty puts everyone on that side under suspicion, and the town can hang a good player over it. If you slip, correct it publicly before the next nomination. A Fixer can flip your trial votes without telling you (your reminder still shows what you cast). If the totals don't fit your own honest vote, say so at once as a possible fix. | support: 9 games, total weight 3.12 | recent evidence: Mallory posted Innocent on Ivan and Heidi but voted Guilty both times. She was hanged on D6 as a Vanilla. Reported by the moderator, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Judy and Mallory (public-ballot game; reworded for totals-only settings).; Moderator: Grace (the nominator), Ivan, Judy and Dave said they leaned Guilty on Erin and then voted Innocent, which saved the Katana holder 2-7.; Moderator: Heidi announced Guilty on holder Erin but voted Innocent. Erin was acquitted on a 5-5 tie on D4 and then kept killing.; Bob, Carol, Dave: Bob's stated Innocent and secret Guilty on Frank produced the 5-vs-4 mismatch that hanged Carol.; Moderator: on D5 Niaj posted Guilty on Grace while his ballot said Innocent. It saved the holder 4-4 and set off a false Fixer hunt.; Alice, Bob (0.16 each): Frank posted that he voted Guilty on Bob on D3, then corrected it to Innocent once the totals were out. The false post left the table an extra mystery to resolve.; Moderator, Grace, Frank: Grace nominated Dave, said Guilty and cast Innocent, so the SK's trial tied 2-2. She first claimed Guilty, then corrected to Innocent, and was hanged for the mismatch.; Moderator: Heidi announced Guilty on Alice and cast Innocent with no public correction. The gap between the stated votes and the count set off a Day of Fixer theories and cost Heidi and Alice credibility.; Bob, Erin, Grace, Carol (0.16 each): Bob announced Guilty on Erin while his reminder said Innocent. - [t216] (Everyone) Cartographer and Scout groups are undirected. A Katana kill puts its attacker in the victim's group that night (the holder, or once a game a Villager using the Stolen Blade trait, whose kill also reads 'Katana'), but the group alone doesn't say who attacked whom. First narrow the attacker to the group's members. Then eliminate: remove the victim, any member confirmed by an exact-role check as a role that doesn't visit, whose link the victim's own visit can explain, and any member with a corroborated harmless visit. If only one unconfirmed member is left, treat them as the likely holder and act that Day, especially if they dodge a plain yes/no about their own visit. A Stolen Blade story from that member is only a claim, since no investigation sees traits: test the role claimed beside it. 'A link isn't a visit' and 'test it from outside the group first' must not acquit that last member. Use a direction-aware result (Lookout, Tracker) only when elimination doesn't finish the job. | support: 5 games, total weight 2.96 | recent evidence: Alice and Bob: the group linking Bob, Carol and Heidi didn't pin the holder by itself; Bob's own Lookout claim did.; Moderator, Heidi, Niaj, Ivan, Dave: Dave's N2 sizes, with Bob's, Mallory's and Grace's visits placed, left Heidi's lone 2-group partner as the holder. Elimination named Judy.; Moderator (0.4); Alice, Grace, Frank, Ivan, Mallory (0.16 each): Niaj's N2 map (Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Judy, Mallory) plus the claimed visits of Grace, Heidi and Ivan left Judy unexplained. She dodged and was hanged.; Dave mapped the Katana suspect and crossed group membership with Carol's counts, turning Erin's word into a mechanical case against Bob.; Moderator, Erin, Bob: Erin's N2 map of the victim's group (Bob, Dave, Frank) plus elimination left Dave as the one unconfirmed member. 'The link could be Frank visiting me' did not acquit him.
- [t218] (Everyone) Know which actions are Astral: Oracle and Ritualist guesses, the Juror's and Scrutineer's checks, the Arsonist's ignite, the Cartographer's map, the Coroner's exam, and the Jester's and Executioner's revenge attacks. No Lookout, Tracker, Fortune Teller, Insomniac, trap, Veteran or Katana holder can see or react to them. 'My target would be exposed' is never a reason to withhold an Astral result. A Smuggler's swap is different: it moves every action aimed at a swapped living player, Astral ones included. An Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a Scrutineer pick, a map or a Ritualist guess aimed at one swapped player lands on the other and is reported under the chosen name. Only random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cursed player's curse) are not moved. Yakuza: an Oracle isn't a visit-reader. Rank Trackers, Lookouts, Fortune Tellers, Cartographers, Scouts and Insomniacs as the visit threats, and the Oracle and Coroner as exact-role threats. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.50 | recent evidence: Bob and Frank: Bob wanted to kill Carol as a 'visit-reader', and Carol's 'exposure' excuse for an Astral guess was called out by the moderator.
- [t219] (Everyone) State your reason in the same message as every nomination, and if you drop a push, say why. An unexplained nomination becomes a lasting lever against you, even when the target was right. If someone else's nomination has no reason, ask for it once and weigh the answer against checkable results rather than repeating the pressure. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.08 | recent evidence: Frank, Dave, Alice and the moderator: Carol's unexplained D3 nomination of Bob, who was Yakuza, was used against her for three Days and helped hang her.; Frank, Heidi, Grace (0.16 each): Carol's nominations of Grace and then Erin had no result behind them, only 'silence' and 'no rush' that everyone shared. Nobody made her give a checkable reason before the Village vote went in. Carol notes the Grace nomination persuaded because it named one specific unanswered question.
- [t220] (Everyone) Speaking budget is limited, and late-round messages cost more. Day 1 is two rounds of talk and nothing carries over from it, so budget saved there is simply lost: use it, briefly. From Day 2 on, ask a concrete question once, by name, and repeat it only when something changes. Save budget for your trial defense and for the late rounds when votes are decided. A nomination costs budget too, and its price also rises with the round: if you mean to nominate, do it in an early round, or check that you can still pay for it before you spend on filler. | support: 24 games, total weight 7.52 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.32: Heidi ('hurt') and Judy ('wrong'): the repeated N2-target demands on Judy burned rounds. This is a misapplication: the table broke the ask-once rule. | recent evidence: Erin, Grace (0.16 each): several players repeated 'no nominations until Night 2 results' on D2, burning budget with no new information.; Olivia: nearly every Villager repeated 'no nomination until more results' for two Days, burning budget while the Jester drove the only trial.; Alice, Bob, Dave, Erin, Frank, Ivan (0.16 each): most D2-D4 posts were 'no nomination from me yet' filler or restated unverified claims. They burned budget and moved no votes.; Moderator: 'post stated votes' and 'only hard results' filler ended most messages on D4-D5, used up budget and moved no vote.; Grace: long, repeated 'no hanging without evidence' posts burned budget over many rounds. Short questions to named players got more.; Moderator, Erin: on D2 every player posted 'no nomination, waiting for Carol's check' for about 8 rounds. Nobody asked about Dave's Not Evil read or the failed N1 Katana.; Moderator: D2 ran about seven rounds of near-identical 'waiting for Heidi's N2 result' messages from all six players and ended with no nomination, though the Dave-to-Carol PM was a live lead.; Frank: repeated 'no nomination from me' posts spent budget and added nothing. Specific questions to named players were what was missing.; Dave, Frank (0.16 each): Day 2 was mostly identical 'no nomination from me, post your names' posts that spent budget and added nothing. One specific check per turn was what was missing.; Frank (0.16): many turns were 'no nomination from me, still waiting' filler that spent budget and added nothing.
- [t221] (Everyone) Reconcile visit counts every night (Fortune Teller totals, Cartographer sizes, Scout groups) against all claimed and known visits. If an extra visitor/visited pair keeps showing up night after night and no living claimant explains it, a hidden actor is visiting, most often an Arsonist dousing or a Neutral killer. Ask unclaimed and Vanilla claimants for night accounts, and point a Lookout or Tracker at them. Treat the gap as a lead about a hidden visitor, not as proof the counter is lying. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: Alice's sizes showed a 2-player group every night and Ivan's N3 numbers were 2,2, both from Heidi dousing Frank, Ivan and Judy. Instead Judy, Frank and Bob called Ivan's numbers 'unsupported' and nominated him. Heidi ignited N4 and won.; Frank (Arsonist, 1.0): Niaj's Fortune Teller counts left one unexplained visitor on N1 and on N3, which were his douse visits, and with his uncheckable tracks that got him tried twice and hanged.
- [t225] (Everyone) (If you have the Sleepwalker trait) You visit one random other living player every night on top of any action of your own, and you aren't told whom. Lookouts, Trackers, visit counts and a Veteran's alert all see that visit. The first time you are pressed about a visit you can't explain, say so in one plain sentence beside your role claim: you sleepwalk, you can't name the target, and a role-block stops it. That explains a sighting of you at a victim or an extra visitor in a count; evasive 'I can't name a target' answers build a case for your trial. No investigation sees traits, so the claim can't be checked and a killer can make it too: expect your role claim to be tested instead. Keep a will that maps where Cartographer, Fortune Teller and Lookout data place your random visit each night, so sightings of you aren't misread. Don't volunteer the trait before a visit needs explaining. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator and Erin: Erin dodged for five rounds and was nominated 9-2. Once she claimed Ex-Con on trial, the claim fit Judy's pair and the archive, and she was acquitted 11-0.; Ivan: claim early and note in your will that the census counts an Ex-Con as Good.
- [t228] (Everyone) Report only what your role actually saw, and label everything else as a deduction from public claims (e.g. 'from Judy's and Frank's posts, X visited Y'). A Medic sees only her own protection, a Lookout only her target's visitors. A visit list your role can't produce costs you credibility for days, even when it's right. Village: ask once for the source of any full-night visit list before using it, and treat an unsourced one as the claimant's mistake to explain, not as evidence. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.56 | recent evidence: Moderator, Carol, Dave, Frank, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Judy: Medic Alice posted a full N1 visit list she couldn't have seen, retracted it, and was doubted for two days.; Alice, Carol (0.16 each): a Veteran is told only how many visited and whether the attack succeeded. Bob tried to cast Alice's naming of Grace as a result her role couldn't give; calling it an inference from the announced death kept her account credible.
- [t229] (Everyone) A nonzero token count on a player claiming any tokenless role is a hard contradiction. That covers Jester, Executioner, Serial Killer and Arsonist, and the tokenless power roles: Tracker, Lookout, Sheriff, Oracle, Fortune Teller, Accountant, Juror, Auditor, Scrutineer, Census Taker, Scout, Eavesdropper, Party Animal and Trapper. It also covers a 'Vanilla Villager' or 'Grunt' claim, which is false anyway unless a hand-picked list names the role (t307). A Philanthropist can't explain it: a gift to a role without tokens is thrown away and isn't counted. Only a token role can explain the count. Name the count, the claim it breaks and the roles it fits in one message before the vote. If the claimant then switches to a token role that fits the count, the earlier claim is still broken (t268); test the new one the same Day. A 'Philanthropist' must name whom they gifted, and every recipient is told that a Philanthropist handed them a token, even one who threw it away, so a named recipient who got no notice breaks the claim. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.12 | recent evidence: Frank's 1-token audit on 'Vanilla' Mallory and 2 on 'Vanilla' Carol. Some players raised Philanthropist gifts as an excuse, which the rules rule out for tokenless roles. Both were Yakuza.; Moderator (0.4), Dave, Erin (0.16 each): a 1-token audit on 'Tracker' Alice was a hard contradiction and drove the D4 hang. Bob, Carol, Grace and Heidi filed the same lesson.
- [t233] (Everyone) Count the full cost of a likely-Jester hang before voting. The revenge Divine-attacks a random Guilty voter the next night. If it hits a player with the Cursed trait, the curse kills another Good player the same night. If an Executioner whose target was that player also wins on the hang, they leave and a second random Guilty voter dies. Add the Katana kill and any Ritualist kill that same night, and one Day can remove four or more Village players and hand the Yakuza half the table, after which no Village nomination can pass. Keep Guilty votes to a bare majority, with confirmed power roles and anyone who is Cursed voting Innocent. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Moderator: Judy (Jester, also Dave's Executioner target) was hanged 10-1. Dave left, both revenges killed Erin and Mallory, and the Katana killed Alice, taking the board from 11 to 6 with 3 Yakuza.; Moderator: the 6-0 Jester hang at 6 alive, then the revenge on Medic Alice and the Katana on Carol, left 3 alive with 2 Yakuza.
- [t234] (Everyone) Make vote pushes in public, not by private message. A PM asking for a Yes before a second source exists is visible to everyone as a trail, and opponents reframe it as a 'whip' to put you on trial. Post the evidence publicly and let the votes follow. Village: an honest player PMing for votes on a credible result (e.g. a Lookout log) is pushing clumsily, not proving a bloc. Judge the result they pushed, not the PMs. | support: 4 games, total weight 2.08 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Erin, Frank, Mallory: Frank's PMs asking for Yes votes on Grace (backed by Bob's true Lookout log) were turned into a 'whip' case, and the Yakuza hanged Vanilla Frank on D4.; Erin, Mallory, Olivia, Niaj: Vanilla Heidi's PM pushes to Bob and Niaj were visible and became the case against her.; Dave: Frank's visible PMs to Dave ('nominate Erin together') were cited by Erin as pressure.; Erin, Heidi, Ivan, Dave (0.16 each): Dave nominated Heidi right after a PM from Grace. The visible pact gave Heidi a credible 'coordinated push' defense and split the D6 vote to 3-1.
- [t246] (Everyone) Nomination and trial ballots are secret, so the only record of how anyone voted is what they say, and nothing tracks it for you. Keep a notepad. For every nomination and trial, write each player's stated vote (Yes/No, Guilty/Innocent, or silent), the announced totals and your own vote. Mark when someone's stated vote later changes, contradicts their stated read, or doesn't fit the totals (e.g. more Guilty votes than players who said Guilty means someone hid a Guilty). A stated vote that doesn't fit a small trial's total is most often one miscast or misposted ballot: ask that player once to re-check their reminder, and never hang on the mismatch alone. Ask players to state their votes once, when a vote is actually coming, and quote the notepad when a claim shifts. A 'post stated votes' line tacked onto every message burns budget and is not a case. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 19 games, total weight 10.52 | against: 3 reports, total weight 1.20: Moderator filed 'hurt'. The Yakuza who stated Innocent on doomed Judy were condemned by it, but that is a t16 misapplication. - [t247] (Everyone) You can't use tokens once you're dead: spend one-shot tokens (Clerk rig, Puppeteer, Ritualist, Medic tokens, swaps, a Kitsune's swallowed attack, ...) the first Day or night they can change a vote or remove a threat, rather than waiting for a perfect moment. A claimed or suspected player can die any night. A Villager's kill tokens (Vigilante and Sniper shots, an Armed Villager's hidden gun) are the exception to 'first chance': they need a lead first, and a target who can still stop your side winning. (Armed can be a Yakuza trait too; the lead rule here is for Village shooters, who can't see sides.) Never fire one on Day 1, which is talk only and gives nothing to aim on, or on Day 2 before any night result has been posted, never at a bare claim clash, and never to 'test' an unverified claim such as a claimed Medic's announced self-protection: a role-block leaves a real Medic with no Defense, and the test kills a Villager. Never fire on a lead a hang has already settled either: the surviving half of a Seer DIFFERENT pair whose other half was hanged as Yakuza, or a player whom a since-exposed Yakuza claimed to have visited. A lead is a break in the target's own claim, votes or visits. A second claimant of your own non-Unique role is often real, and twice now one real Sniper has killed the other. Never fire one at a claimed Neutral that never blocks wins (Bounty Hunter, Jester, Executioner, Magpie), least of all a Bounty Hunter whose one attack is already spent: the shot removes no threat. Once you have a lead on a Yakuza or Neutral-killer suspect, don't hold the shot for proof. At parity (the Yakuza hold half the votes) no nomination can pass, so fire it that Day even on a coin flip between two suspects. A puppet or doubled ballot lapses if you die the night you spend it (R21), and a Sniper's Day shot doesn't fire if you die before its random delay runs out (R25), so spend those ahead of a likely death. Yakuza: before spending a Day-effect token on a player (fix, puppet), check in night chat that your team isn't killing that player tonight. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 23 games, total weight 13.04 | against: 6 reports, total weight 2.36: Bob and Erin filed 'hurt': spending the Fixer 'now' went on Dave, whom the team's Ritualist killed the same night. The timing idea was sound, but the target check was missing.; Dave filed 'hurt': 'use it at the first chance' fed his D2 Standard shot at Heidi on a claim clash; she was a real Sniper. The edit adds 'on a lead'.; Heidi filed 'wrong': her queued Strong Day shot never fired because Dave's shot killed her first. Spending first didn't help with a delayed Day action. The edit adds the R25 caveat.; Bob and Grace (0.16 each) filed 'wrong': 'use tokens the first time they can matter' fed both D2 shots, fired before any night result. The 'needs a lead' condition was there but said nothing about a harmless target. - [t249] (Everyone) When a trial total doesn't fit the stated votes, the likeliest cause by far is that someone cast a different ballot from the one they announced: a miscast, a mispost or a lie. Start there: ask every player who stated a vote, once, to re-check their private reminder and repost it. Don't build a case on a hidden Fixer or Ballot Stuffer without a second sign, and never let that theory replace hunting the players you can see (the never-claimed, the broken claims): twice now a phantom-Fixer hunt has eaten a Day. An audit showing that a player's vote counted opposite to what they said doesn't separate a fix from a plain misstatement either, and it doesn't name who gained. Totals can be changed on purpose, though. A Ballot Stuffer's trial vote counts twice, so the total exceeds the voters. A Fixer secretly flips one player's Guilty/Innocent votes for a whole Day, in every trial that Day: the total stays the same, the player isn't told, and their reminder shows what they cast. If the reminders hold and the totals show your own vote counted as the opposite (you cast Guilty, the Guilty total is one short and the other stated votes account for the rest), assume you are fixed for the Day: say so, and cast the opposite ballot in that Day's next trial, or at least change your vote between trials to test it. A fixed Guilty voter who keeps voting Guilty saves the nominee every time. Never take the nominee's advice to 'vote exactly as you announced'. An Auditor (Village) and the Yakuza's Informant see fixed votes as counted. Neither role touches nomination (Yes/No) votes, so a Yes count that contradicts stated votes is the cleanest evidence of a hidden liar. A liar is not necessarily Yakuza, though. A nominee who votes Yes on their own nomination and then claims No is baiting the hang, which is a Jester tell (t91), not grounds to hang. Auditor: audit a player whose votes decided a close trial or who defended a suspect. Post night, target and the full record, and read a trial-vote mismatch as 'misstated, lied or fixed' but a Yes/No mismatch as a lie. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 9 games, total weight 3.28 | against: 3 reports, total weight 1.72: Bob ('hurt') and Frank ('wrong', 0.16 each): the Fixer theory on a 5-vs-4 gap was a misposted vote and wasted a Day.; Moderator, Carol, Erin, Mallory filed 'hurt': the 9-of-9 Yes count against Bob's stated No became the lead reason to hang him. The test caught a liar, but the liar was a Jester who wanted to be caught. Bob and Heidi filed 'helped' as a liar test only.; Moderator filed 'hurt': Heidi said Guilty on Alice and cast Innocent. Alice's audit showed the mismatch, and Alice, Dave and Carol built D3 cases on a 'living Fixer' who didn't exist, which pulled attention off unclaimed Ivan and Erin. Second game with a phantom-Fixer hunt. The warning was in the entry but came after the manipulation roles. Edited to lead with the plain explanation. | recent evidence: From a revision, not a game. It covers the new Fixer, Auditor and Informant roles, which change how vote-total arithmetic in t5, t34, t48 and t239 must be read.; Carol (Auditor): her audits confirmed stated votes against the totals.; Grace and Mallory (0.16 each): checking the totals and voter reminders resolved the gap quickly as a posting error.; Moderator, Alice, Bob: the unalterable nomination Yes counts found Carol as the unclaimed Yes. Nobody acted on the Fixer warning, and a fix decided Heidi's trial.; Grace and Frank: re-checking Erin's trial total against stated votes exposed the miscount before a wrong hang.; Erin filed 'helped': comparing stated votes with the 2-2 total found the misstated vote. Alice and Bob add that the table should have asked for the reminder explanation once and then dropped it.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Bob, Carol, Erin (0.16 each) filed 'helped': at 3 alive on D6 Carol's trials read 1 Guilty of 3 three times though Alice and Heidi both cast Guilty. Alice diagnosed the fix on Heidi from the totals. It failed only because Heidi kept voting Guilty as Carol urged.; Grace, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the 6-vote total from 5 voters on D4 was read as a Ballot Stuffer's extra vote, which kept the table from misreading the count.; Moderator (0.4) filed 'helped': after the 3-3 on Erin against the stated Guilty votes, Heidi and Alice asked voters to check reminders, Bob admitted he had cast Innocent, and nobody hunted a Fixer. The second changed vote (Carol's Merciful trait) went unnoticed; see t352.
- [t260] (Everyone) When a count or group result includes you (a census, an FT count, a Cartographer group), say publicly whether it fits what you know of yourself, e.g. 'the census counted me Good, which I know is true'. Several members confirming one result turn an unverifiable claimant into a trusted source, and later results from that source can then be used for elimination. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Frank, Erin, Heidi (1.0 each): Smuggler Dave publicly confirmed Roleplayer Frank's fake 'Dave = Vanilla CORRECT' on himself, which propped up a false result and a Yakuza for a Day. This anchored Bob over Erin's Sheriff claim.
- [t264] (Everyone) When a trial total doesn't fit the stated votes, list every voter in that trial, including players who have since died. A dead player who said Innocent may be the hidden Guilty. Deaths don't remove candidates: never hang the last living stated-Innocent just because the others are dead. A dead liar still points at their defense partners, so weigh the hidden vote as a lead together with defenses, PMs and mechanical results, not as proof against the survivor. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.96 | recent evidence: Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Heidi: the extra Guilty on Frank's trial was dead Bob's, yet the town hanged Carol as 'the only one left to explain it'.
