- [t265] (Everyone) An Insomniac list gives the true roles of that night's visitors, so check every listed role against the Yakuza role list as soon as it is posted. Any Yakuza-only role in it proves a living Yakuza of that role visited that night, and a player who claims that role is that visitor. A Yakuza role with no visiting action of its own (Wiretap, Informant, Clerk, Ballot Stuffer, Talent Agent, or a Grunt where a hand-picked list includes one) can appear only because that player made an attack visit, unless the Sleepwalker trait explains the visit. Usually that means they held the Katana and attacked; the other attack visits are a Spare Blade attack or the hidden gun of the Armed trait, which a Yakuza can now hold. On a Katana night, strike the roles whose visits are explained (the victim's own, confirmed claimants'). If one role is left, its holder made the Katana visit. Cross the list with a Cartographer map of the victim, a Lookout's or Tracker's posted target and the claimed visits to narrow the holder to the living players who could be that role, then post the chain in one message and nominate that Day. Yakuza: the list shows true roles, so no cover claim hides a visit. While a claimed Insomniac still holds tokens, kill them before you visit again (t306). | support: 1 games, total weight 0.48 | recent evidence: Erin, Heidi, Frank: Heidi's N2 'Grunt, Lookout' on the night Lookout Dave was killed showed the Grunt was the attacker.
- [t267] (Everyone) If the game tells you a cap on how many players can hold a role (e.g. at most two Vanilla Villagers), count the claims against it from Day 2. More claimants than the cap allows means at least one is lying, a hard check that needs no reads. Press the latest and least-supported claimants first. A cap is not a role list, so without a stated cap fall back on t60's odds reasoning. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Heidi and the moderator: four Vanilla claims against a cap of two made Frank's fallback claim an instant mechanical case.
- [t270] (Everyone) A nomination made within minutes of a claim, with a Guilty vote promised before the nominee has answered, is a tell worth noting. Ask the nominator once for the checkable reason, and hold your Yes until one is given. Don't hang on the pattern alone. Yakuza: never nominate fast with a pre-announced Guilty. It is the most readable pattern you can leave. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Frank, Mallory, Olivia (Village) and Dave (Yakuza, 0.16 each): Dave's fast D3 nomination of Frank with a promised Guilty became the main pattern against the team.
- [t276] (Everyone) Before every endgame hang and every night kill, count who will be alive at the next Day start and compare it with the Vulture threshold, max(floor(N/4),2), where N is the starting player count. That is 3 for 12-15 players and 2 for 11 or fewer. A living Vulture wins alone at a Day start with that many or fewer alive, and everyone else loses. A hang or kill that drops the count to the threshold therefore hands the game to any Vulture still alive. The Vulture is Unique and usually hides behind a quiet claim with no action and nothing checkable, voting with the majority. Village: while an unverified quiet claimant lives, don't let a mis-hang bring the count to the threshold; spend the hang on the best killer case or on that claimant. Killers: if the threshold is near, remove the unverified quiet claimant rather than a confirmed villager. Add this check to the Jester and Executioner checks before any late hang. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator, Bob, Dave, Frank, Ivan, Niaj, Alice: with 13 players the threshold was 3. The D6 hang of Vanilla Frank left 4 alive. The N6 Katana on Bob plus a missed Ritualist guess on Alice then left 3, and Alice (Vulture, quiet Vanilla claim) won. Bob noted that the Jester-focused check never prompted a Vulture count.; Bob, Carol, Grace, Mallory (1.0 each): with 13 players the Vulture threshold was 3. The town hanged quiet Vanilla claimant Olivia at 5 alive, before the count could reach it.
- [t281] (Everyone) Before calling anyone's vote-count claim false, agree which Day and which nomination or trial it refers to. Then count the voters alive at that vote: Yes plus No equals the living voters at the time, and dead players cast nothing. Include the nominee's own vote (a nominee may vote Guilty on themself) and any doubled ballot, then line each stated vote up against the total before naming a 'mismatch'. In a final three, recompute every past total this way before nominating, because one miscount can mislynch a Villager and hand the last killer the win that night. A tally from a different Day, or a miscount by the accuser, can hang a truthful player. Write the Day and the nominee next to every count you post or quote. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.96 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Heidi read Grace's '9 Yes, 3 No' (Grace's own D3 nomination) as the D5 tally, and Juror Grace was hanged 8-2. Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia and Grace (0.16 each) all filed 'count living voters only'.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank (0.16 each): all filed 'count the nominee's own vote before accusing'. Dave's miscount of Erin's trial drove two nominations and nearly lost the game.
- [t289] (Everyone) Keep a day-by-day log of every role claim, not just votes. A claim that gains a night, a token change or a new detail each Day (e.g. a Veteran's alerts going N1, then N1-N2, then N1-N3) reads as improvised, so quote the earlier wording and ask for the full history once. But piecemeal posting is also what an honest, disorganized claimant does, so treat drift as a lead to test, not a hang on its own. Claimants: state your complete history (every night, target, result, tokens spent) once, in one message, and repeat it word for word. | support: 4 games, total weight 0.88 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Frank all flagged Heidi's growing Veteran alert log as a tell. Heidi was a real Veteran, so the drift came from piecemeal posting, not lying.; Grace: Alice's D2 'visited Carol' and D3 'eavesdropped Carol' described one action, and the apparent conflict only surfaced later. A claim log with exact wording would have settled it at once.; Bob: each restatement of his real Fortune Teller numbers in new words made the table doubt him more. State the full history once, in the past tense, and repeat it word for word.; Alice, Frank (0.16 each): real Auditor Alice gave three versions of Night 1 (audit, no action, 'protected by someone'). Each change read as a lie, got her nominated on D2 and made her true audit easy to dismiss. Frank adds that the drift drew more attention than Grace's Yakuza-only claim deserved to lose.
- [t291] (Everyone) Line up Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer sizes and every claimed visit. If the visitor count equals the number of claimed visitors (no spare slot), the Katana holder is hiding the attack behind one claimed visit. Usually it is a claimed visit that 'happened' to land on the victim (a check, a census, a watch), or a no-visit claimer linked into the victim's group. Say so outright: those claimants are the suspects. Allow for a Sleepwalker's random visit as a floating edge, but no investigation sees traits, so a Sleepwalker claim that first appears to explain the spare edge is only a claim. Split the suspects by claim order (a claim first posted after the numbers were public is the likeliest fit), by archive or profile lists, and by exact-role checks. Hang that Day rather than waiting a night. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Day 2: the 6/6 count, the 4,3,2,1,1,1,1 sizes and the posted targets left Erin (Lookout, claimed first) or Heidi (Sheriff 'check' on Katana victim Mallory, claimed last). Heidi was the holder. Reported by Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Judy, Olivia.
- [t305] (Everyone) Before calling a posted result 'provably false', recompute it yourself from the role's text, exclusions included. A Juror's counts leave out every vote on the nomination or trial of either named player, so 'no shared votes yet' is correct when the only trials so far were of one of the pair. Scrutineer and Census Taker groups add random players, and a Seer pair compares appearances. A result that seems to conflict with the public record is a question for the claimant ('which rule gives that?'), not proof of a lie. One conflicting line is never enough to hang or prosecute on, least of all at 4-5 alive. Claimants: when your result will look odd against the record, quote the rule that explains it in the same message. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Grace, Bob, Dave, Frank (0.16 each): Dave's honest N2 Juror result 'Frank and Alice: no shared votes yet' was right, since the only trial was Frank's and the Juror excludes it. Both were wrong on the rules (night actions resolve together), and the false 'contradiction' fed the release on Bob.
- [t307] (Everyone) Vanilla Villager and Grunt are never in a game unless a hand-picked list names them, and a setup may name other roles that are never in play. Check every role claim against that the Day it is made. A claim of an excluded role (a 'Vanilla' claim above all) is impossible: call it out at once and ask for a real role. It is a lie, not a faction read. Paired with a thin push, a self-nomination or 'hang me', it fits a Jester (t91), so don't spend more Guilty votes on it than a bare majority. Yakuza and Neutrals: never use Vanilla Villager, Grunt or any other excluded role as cover. Say 'no night action' and name a role only when pressed, picking one the setup allows that fits your tokens and visits. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.56 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Carol, Mallory, Olivia, Alice (0.16 each): this setup excluded Vanilla Villager and Grunt. Jester Mallory claimed Vanilla on D2, Ivan caught it the next morning, and the claim plus 'hang me' drew 8 Guilty. One game.
- [t337] (Everyone) Two alive with a killer who can't or won't kill is a loss for everyone, not a draw. At two alive a nomination needs both Yes votes, so nobody is hanged, and if the last killer has no safe attack (an armed trap on the last Villager, a Standard Katana against Standard Defense) the R14 stall ends the game with no winner after three quiet Nights and Days. Village: the hang at three alive is your last winning move, so put the best remaining suspect on trial that Day, and if the totals look wrong, test for a fix (t249) before the Day ends. A killer: a stall loses for you too, so choose each late kill by asking who is left and whether you can still attack them. A Yakuza with an unused Katana Expert (Strong: beats Standard Defense, not a trap) or Katana Master (Divine: beats both) still has one such attack, so keep that upgrade for this spot. Refuse any offer from the other survivor to 'test' you by self-nominating or voting Guilty on yourself. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each) and the moderator's review: after Carol killed Trapper Heidi on N6, the trap sat on Alice, Carol couldn't attack, neither would vote on themselves, and N7-N9 passed with no death. Everyone lost on Day 10. One game.
- [t339] (Everyone) Bloodhound counts are cumulative over the whole game, so read them as a table, not as one night's attacks. For each posted result write the night, the trio and the total, then list which nights each member could have added an attack. A lone Katana holder adds 1 every kill night, so '2 attacks in this trio' needs only one attacker, and a kill made by someone outside the trio (a Bounty Hunter, a Serial Killer) adds nothing to it. Match each death's announced cause to a possible attacker before saying the count needs a second killer in the group. A count gives a pool, never a name: the member with a second break (a failed audit, a changed claim, a vote mismatch) is the case, not the one who argued most. | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Frank, Grace, Heidi, Bob (0.16 each): on D3 Dave nominated Juror Frank arguing that Erin's and Carol's deaths needed two attackers in Grace's N2 trio. - [t350] (Everyone) (For Yakuza and Neutrals faking a passive role.) A fake Fortune Teller, Scout or Cartographer result has only a few possible honest values, and one impossible value exposes you. Before posting, rebuild that night's visits from every public claim and every published will, including the one that came out this morning: count the confirmed visitors and visited, and never put two players a known visit links into different groups. - [t352] (Everyone) Two traits change how a trial vote counts (R24): a Merciful player's Guilty/Innocent votes always count Innocent and a Zealot's always count Guilty, whatever they cast, and the holder's private reminder shows both the vote cast and how it counted. So a total that doesn't fit the stated votes can come from a trait as well as a miscast, a lie or a Fixer. When you ask voters to re-check their reminders, ask each to say how the vote counted, not only what they cast. Holders of either faction: say it at your first trial vote, before you are in any danger, so the totals fit your words from then on. A trait first claimed on your own trial reads as a convenient excuse and changes no votes. Merciful means you can never add a Guilty; Zealot means you can never help acquit, and each of your trial votes puts you in a Jester's revenge pool. When you match stated votes to totals, count a declared Zealot as Guilty and a declared Merciful as Innocent. Yakuza holders: plan the vote math around it in night chat. A Merciful member can't bus a teammate by ballot, so a stated Guilty leaves the Guilty total one short, and a Zealot member can't save one. A Zealot teammate is a known Guilty on every trial, so don't be the only other Guilty beside them on a trial that fails (t160). | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator's review: Carol (Fabricator, Merciful) stated Guilty on Erin's D3 trial and it counted Innocent; with Bob's miscast the total came out 3-3 against four or more stated Guilty. The table asked for reminders, found Bob's and never asked Carol how hers counted. One game, thin.; Bob (Zealot, 1.0): his trial votes always counted Guilty, so he could never be the Innocent in a total. His lesson is to put the trait in the will and disclose it before stating a vote. Thin: no total was actually disputed.
- [t355] (Everyone) A Yakuza with the hidden Shaky trait makes Katana attacks of level None: they never kill, yet they are still attacks and visits, and the target is still told they were attacked with the Katana. So a Katana survival with no protector named no longer points only at innate Defense (Serial Killer, Arsonist) or a Medic; the hidden Narrow Escape trait also saves any player once (t370). A Katana notice can also come from a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade attack (Standard, and it does kill), so one notice doesn't even show that the Yakuza attacked that player. Before hanging a survivor on the Serial Killer fit (t43), check whether other players also survived attacks, or whether nights passed with no Katana death and no blocker: failures on different targets fit one Shaky holder better than several protected targets. A Lookout, Tracker, trap or Enforcer still catches a Shaky holder, because the visit and the attack happen. Yakuza: if one member's attacks keep failing on different targets, move the Katana (t45). Nobody holds Shaky with Katana Expert or Katana Master, so a member who was told they have either trait can't be Shaky and is a safe holder to move it to. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 1 games, total weight 0.00 | recent evidence: From a revision of the rules (new hidden Shaky trait), not from a game.
- [t357] (Everyone) A player can visit more than one target in a night: their role's action plus a Katana attack, a Spare Blade attack, a Villager's Stolen Blade attack, a Smuggler's swap (two visits) or a Sleepwalker's random visit, which the Sleepwalker isn't told about. A Lookout's 'X visited Y' shows one destination and says nothing about where else X went; only a Tracker on X lists X's destinations. So never call a claim impossible because 'X was seen at Y, so X can't have visited Z' (e.g. to deny a Veteran's alert kill). A player who pushes that argument hard against a claim that a cause of death already backs is a lead. - [t361] (Everyone) An exact-role 'CORRECT' that a revealed role then contradicts (a marked hang, a Coroner exam) means the claimant is most likely a Roleplayer. Don't rescue it with a swap unless someone posted a swap notice for that night and the swap partner could hold the guessed role. From then on that claimant's results are noise, even when they keep landing on the right faction: a Roleplayer picks its own targets, so the names it chooses are its read (a lean, like anyone's) while the CORRECT/WRONG is a coin flip. Don't rebuild them into a trusted source or hang on their next call alone: get a real check first (a true Oracle's guess, a mark, a count). - [t370] (Everyone) A failed attack with no protector named can be the hidden Narrow Escape trait (R24): any player of any faction can have it, they aren't told, and on the first night a non-Divine attack would kill them every such attack fails. So one unexplained survival of a Vigilante shot, a Katana or a Serial Killer attack doesn't prove innate Defense or a side, and the survivor honestly can't explain it. The trait is used up by that night; a night when something else stopped the attack (a Medic, a trap, a charm) doesn't use it up. It never stops a Divine attack (a 2-token Ritualist guess, a Katana Master's Katana, a Jester's revenge) or a death by Day, so a hang or a Sniper's Day shot goes through, and a Day shot the target survives still shows real Defense. Attackers: attack the same player again on a later night instead of writing them off (for Yakuza this is the exception to 'don't retry' when no Medic is plausible); a second failure with no protector does point to real Defense (t43). Village: don't hang the shooter because the shot 'can't be verified', and don't clear or convict the survivor on one survival. Judge both on their claims, votes and PM links. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Dave, Frank, Alice (0.16 each): Carol (Fixer, Katana holder) survived Alice's N3 Basic shot through Narrow Escape. The table argued whether it meant innate Defense, and the Yakuza hanged Alice on her 'unverifiable' shot the next Day. One low-strength game; the retry advice follows from the rule text, not from play.
- [t1] (Village) Never withhold a useful result to bait a counterclaim. Investigators: once you hold a result that bears on a vote, post the target and exact result, with as much of your role as the result needs to be believed and checked, in your first speech after getting it, or the moment someone asks, and keep your last will current. Naming your exact role feeds a possible Ritualist, whose guess kills through any Medic (t55): when one may be in the game, put the full log in your will before you claim and expect to die that night. A result held back and revealed after an opponent's claim looks retrofitted, above all when it names the player who just accused you. A hidden investigator who votes with the Yakuza looks exactly like a fake. Exception: with no result yet, or only a weak one, don't name your exact role on Day 2, and never say you will act or spend a token tonight. A firm exact claim of a role off the Roleplayer list (Bloodhound, Juror, Scrutineer...) is a sure Ritualist kill, so wait until the claim changes a vote. If someone else claims your role or exact result, say so at once, in your next message, with your full log. Only Smuggler and Vulture are Unique, so a second claimant of any other role may be as real as you: state your claim and ask them for something checkable (tokens, results), but don't call them a liar on the clash alone, and never fire a kill token at them over it (t313). Being second after a vague opener reads as copying, so ask the rival for the exact wording of their private result before you post yours. Never vote against your own information to 'wait and see'. If you must vote in a way that looks odd, explain why during that trial. | support: 38 games, total weight 18.34 | against: 15 reports, total weight 5.33: Moderator, Alice, Carol, Dave, Grace, Heidi, Mallory, Niaj filed 'hurt': Dave (Bloodhound, weak draw) and Carol (Juror, no result) named exact roles on their first turn, and the Ritualist killed both with one-token guesses. - [t2] (Village) When two Sheriff claimants call each other Evil, both results can be true: Executioners and Jesters read Evil. - [t11] (Village) Keep hostile Neutrals (SK, Arsonist) in the suspect pool all game, not only after the Yakuza die. Once an archive, survey or kill pattern suggests one exists, give every player a slot and keep a running list of candidates. Press the quiet unclaimed players who give only measured, non-committal comments, and ask each for a claim. An Arsonist kills no one until it ignites, so quiet nights don't mean it's harmless; expect a multi-death night. A Not Evil Sheriff result doesn't clear them, and a 'Vanilla Villager' claim is a lie unless a hand-picked list names that role (t307). - [t32] (Village) On Day 1 and Day 2, don't hint at a protective role, an info role, a Veteran or your tokens. Day 1 is talk only and nobody has a result yet, so a Day 1 claim buys nothing and picks you as the Night 1 Katana target. A half-hint ('my role reads past trials') is the worst of both: it draws a Day of pressure, forces the full claim anyway and marks you for the night kill. Either give no hint at all, or once you are pressed past one calm non-answer, claim plainly once rather than dodging for rounds (t188). If someone else drops a hint, don't ask them about it in public; that spreads the hint. A flat, repeated refusal to claim is itself a hint: answer claim requests with a quiet, cooperative non-answer instead of making the refusal the day's topic. Investigators should still post results publicly once they matter, not only in a will that may come too late; a Night 1 result that bears on Day 2 already matters. When a Ritualist may be alive, never name another living player's exact role in your will or in public; write 'one of X/Y holds info, ask them'. The same care applies to your own role: a firm Day 1 or Day 2 claim of a role off the Roleplayer list, made with no result or a weak one, is a free Ritualist kill (t55). | support: 37 games, total weight 18.23 | against: 15 reports, total weight 6.06: Carol and Ivan: no Ritualist existed, so naming exact roles cost nothing. - [t34] (Village) Once a player is shown to be Yakuza by a mechanical result (an Oracle RIGHT on a Yakuza role, a Yakuza-only role claim, a corroborated lone visit to a Katana victim), your top suspects are those who argued to delay or stop their hang, defended them, or exchanged PMs with them. A player merely caught lying is not shown Yakuza: the liar may be a Jester, and chaining from a proven liar to their early defenders lands on villagers. Nor is a player whom a lone Sheriff reads Evil: Jesters, Executioners, Notorious or Turncoat Villagers and framed players all read Evil, so run the Jester check (t91) before treating the read as a Yakuza result. Roles stay hidden on death, so a hang never confirms who was Yakuza: keep such links only as strong as the case against the dead player. Ballots are secret, so before each nomination and trial vote, ask every player to state their vote publicly. Then compare the stated votes with the announced totals: extra No or Innocent votes mean someone hid a defensive vote. Do the vote math first. An Innocent bloc that couldn't change a settled result proves little, and Yakuza will reuse it to steer suspicion onto townies. Pushing No or delay at the nomination, then Guilty once the hang is certain, is the more telling pattern. A stated Guilty vote on a likely Yakuza clears no one. A bloc is a lead, not a verdict: a hidden investigator voting on their own info fits the same pattern, so demand a claim and results before hanging. If the game goes on after you have hanged everyone you believed Yakuza, that doesn't prove a Yakuza is left: either one you missed or a win-blocking Neutral (Serial Killer, Arsonist or Vulture) is still alive. Jesters, Executioners, Bounty Hunters and Magpies never block the end. Vote links to the dead then mostly point at Neutrals who voted alongside them, and a Jester wants exactly that hang. Switch to who could have made the remaining kills (causes of death, douse or attack visits), and press for claims; if the kills have stopped, the blocker may be a Vulture behind a quiet claim (t96). If you hang a likely Jester anyway, keep the Guilty votes to a bare majority. | support: 47 games, total weight 22.36 | against: 8 reports, total weight 3.25: Moderator: players applied it loosely to Carol's and Heidi's Innocent votes, which could not have saved Mallory at 7-3. Bob hammered those votes, which led to Frank shooting Medic Heidi and helped hang Seer Carol. Judy's No on the nomination was the real tell, and it was used only on D6.; Moderator, Frank, Judy: after the Yakuza were dead, Carol and Dave hanged Grace for her No on Alice's nomination and her Guilty on Heidi alongside the Yakuza. She was the Jester; her hang won for her and her revenge killed Carol, while Arsonist Mallory stayed clean on votes.; Grace filed 'wrong': the 'shown Yakuza by a mechanical result' trigger rested on one Coroner claim. Weak: a Coroner exam is an exact-role check, and the chain hit the real Yakuza (Judy). Judy's 'hurt' is a Yakuza complaint that the tip worked.; Erin (Yakuza) filed 'hurt' because the town used the tip against her team. Weak: the tip worked as intended.; Moderator filed 'hurt': PM links were used as proof rather than leads (Mallory, Frank hanged), and the real Yakuza were never examined. Partly a misapplication: the tip already says PM links are leads only.; Erin and Olivia filed 'hurt': the Village applied the defender chain to Bob, who was only shown lying, not Yakuza. It pointed at Mallory, Olivia (both Good) and Ivan while Frank and Judy went unexamined. A misapplication, but the wording allowed it.; Carol filed 'wrong': her Evil read on Bob was true of a Jester, and 'look at who defended them' did not separate Jester from Yakuza. - [t38] (Village) Treat the nomination and trial votes as separate decisions, unless a Clerk may hold an unused rig token. In that case a passed nomination can mean execution whatever the trial vote: vote Yes only on someone you'd accept dying, and vote No at the nomination if you are a likely target. At low player counts, don't vote Yes just to 'hear a defense'. The nominator's Yes is automatic, so don't read it as a stance. Before voting Guilty, name one concrete contradiction or mechanical fit in their claims, votes or results, and say why an innocent explanation no longer fits. A missing result or announcement that a role-block fully explains (a blocked prosecution, an empty investigation, 'no named target') is never a contradiction; check the rules before calling one. Nor is a result that only looks false until the role's own exclusions are applied (a Juror ignores votes on either named player's nomination or trial): recompute a 'false' result from the role text before using it as your fit (t305), and remember that two claimants of a non-Unique role can both be real. A contradiction doesn't separate a Yakuza from a Jester who wants the hang. If the nominee self-nominated on any Day, voted Yes on their own nomination, asked for the rope, pushed hangs with no data, or the case rests only on behaviour (vote-steering, PMs), run the Jester check first (t91): an Evil Sheriff read on such a player is the Jester signature, not a Yakuza case. Don't let a recited 'contradiction' stand in for a mechanical case. Each of these counts as a mechanical case: a public claim of a Yakuza-only role (t227); a first-person slip about an attack or night action the player's claimed role can't make ('my Katana on Carol failed' or 'my own attacks' from a no-action claimant), which is a broken claim and not a wording quibble (t20); an unexplained Katana survival with no protector named, plus a Not Evil read and a refused night account (the Serial Killer fit, t43); an unexplained Evil result on a player whose claim doesn't account for it and who has not sought their own hang; a claim of a role the setup excludes, such as Vanilla Villager (t307), which proves a lie though not a faction; a denial reversed into a role claim; a group result (survey, census, map, archive slot math) whose other members are confirmed, so elimination names the remaining player; a corroborated lone visitor to a Katana victim whose claim doesn't fit; a visit count that leaves room for only one visitor on a Katana night; slot math plus matching blocks plus a refusal to claim any role. The result behind the case must itself be corroborated: by the players it names, by a second source, or by the claimant's proven record. One unverified claimant's map, count or watch is a claim, not a mechanical fit, least of all when it was held back and names the claimant's own accuser (t296). A corroborated case plus the suspect's refusal to answer a plain yes/no about their own visit or role is enough. A bare denial offered only once cornered on trial does not answer it. 'A dodge isn't a contradiction', 'name a contradiction in my posts', 'that's only wording' and 'what exact fact has been disproved?' must not shield them. The standard is a better explanation, not proof. If you said you would vote Guilty unless they answered and they didn't, vote Guilty. Never pad a case with a fact you haven't checked: one false detail lets the defense discredit the whole push. An Evil result explained only by an uncheckable claim (the Notorious trait, which no investigation sees) is not a clean acquittal: test the role claimed beside it before trusting it. Echoed concerns and corrected slips of timeline or phrasing are not new evidence. A stated vote that doesn't fit a small trial's total is most often one miscast or misposted ballot (t211): a lead to question, not a broken claim. Three Innocent trials end the Day with no hang, so give your trials to the strongest mechanical cases and convict on them. This standard decides which case to convict, not whether to hang at all. If one more night kill could let the Yakuza block every nomination (e.g. 2 Yakuza at 4 alive), hang the best case available today, even a 50/50 group result plus a claim wobble (t286). Waiting for a perfect fit then loses the game. When no investigator is alive and no result can come, 'checkable info only' is a stall, not a standard: the best lead on the table is then your case (a pair who PM every Day, vote alike and give a vague account of the PMs, t41; the player who has made nothing checkable, t317), and it must be nominated by Day 3 at the latest. Near the lock, aim that hang at the Yakuza lead, not at a Neutral killer (t286). | support: 64 games, total weight 34.71 | against: 40 reports, total weight 17.96: Bob (Jester) filed 'hurt': the demand for a mechanical fit let Dave survive D2. Weak: Dave was Village and the acquittal was correct.; Moderator filed 'hurt': the Village repeated 'only hard results' for two Days while the Arsonist set up a double kill. Neither correct kill (Ivan's shot on Bob, the hang of Heidi) rested on a mechanical result. See t317.; Alice, Dave ('hurt') and Heidi ('wrong'), 0.16 each: the town read one Sheriff Evil read as a mechanical fit and hanged Jester Erin 7-0. Partly non-application, since Erin had self-nominated on D2 and the tip says to run the Jester check, but the 'Evil result' item carried no such condition. The tip didn't say a first-person action slip against a no-action claim is a broken claim. The one correct hang (Bob, D3) rested on a PM trail and a failed push. Erin's 'wrong' argues the opposite (the hang was a lucky lean); Grace's 'helped' is from the SK. The 'hang your best case before the lock' half was ignored. Alice's 'helped' and Bob's 'wrong' both say the warning was right but not followed. The misses, Frank's hang and Alice's shooting, leaned on timing and how a claim looked.; Bob, Carol, Dave, Frank, Ivan, Judy, Mallory and the moderator: the town hanged real Profiler Dave 10-1 on a 'pattern' lean while his offered tests (Accountant, Coroner, next profile) were pending. The hang with a mechanical basis (Olivia's impossible FT count) hit a hostile.; Moderator (0.4), Dave, Erin (0.16 each) filed 'helped' on the 'no result coming, hang the best lead' clause: with Erin's N2 check blocked, the town hanged Bob on D3 on his refused yes/no and the PM link. Olivia's hang rested partly on a Roleplayer's random call and was right only through Niaj's real RIGHT.; Frank, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped' on the 'no result coming, hang the best lead' clause: with the Sheriff dead after N1, the town hanged Dave on D2 on the admitted claim-coaching. He was Yakuza. Carol adds that the hang came none too soon; Dave himself calls a 4-3 on behaviour thin.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Bob (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the 'name a mechanical fit' half acquitted real Trapper Dave 5-2 in the puppet-driven D2 trial. The 'no result coming, hang the best lead' half was then ignored on D2-D3, which the moderator says cost the game.; Carol filed 'helped': Mallory was hanged 12-0 on Ivan's pair plus Heidi's list (a mechanical fit), while the count-only push on Frank was acquitted 3-9. - [t39] (Village) Investigators (Sheriff, Lookout, Tracker, Oracle) and protectors (Medic): never announce tonight's target in public or by private message. An announced target tells the Pharmacist or Party Animal whom to block and tells the Katana where to strike or what to avoid. Don't announce that you will act or spend a token tonight either, even with no target named ('I'll scent tonight', 'my audit will test him'): it marks you as tonight's kill. A Wiretap reads the contents of every PM, and nobody is told it exists, so treat every PM as read by the Yakuza. Never suggest another player's night target or test by PM. If someone PMs you one, don't reply confirming it; just act. Even a PM whose contents stay hidden marks you: everyone sees that it was sent, and Yakuza read a PM from one claimed investigator to another as a sign that both are worth killing. This covers only tonight's plans. Once a result exists and bears on a vote, post it in full (night, target, exact result). Never stretch secrecy about tonight into withholding or vaguening a past result, and never invent a mechanical excuse for it: Astral actions like an Oracle guess can't be seen by anyone. The one honest reason to hold a result is a possible Ritualist: an exact role claim is a sure guess through any Medic (t55), so a result that changes no vote yet can wait in your will. Narrow exception: a Trapper may announce a trap only to shield a confirmed investigator who is a likely kill target. Killers simply route around an announced trap, so it never catches anyone, and on any other player it is wasted. Otherwise keep the trap hidden and reveal it only after it fires. | support: 46 games, total weight 23.87 | against: 8 reports, total weight 2.60: Heidi: publicly naming Judy as a hang target from her census made her the obvious N3 Katana target and her N3 result died with her. The tip covers announced targets, not posted results; she still filed 'hurt'.; Moderator: Carol stretched the target-secrecy rule into withholding a past result, and claimed her Astral Oracle target could be 'exposed'. The evasion helped hang her.; Grace filed 'hurt': Judy and Heidi posted watch targets and counts, and Heidi was killed N4. Mostly a misapplication: the tip says to post past results, and the Katana kills investigators anyway.; Alice filed 'hurt': she announced her protection of Judy publicly. Misapplication, since she broke the tip; the attack on Judy still failed. Judy (0.16) said the tip helped.; Dave filed 'hurt' for announcing his alert plans. This is a misapplication: he broke the spirit of the tip, and t64 covers Veteran announcements. Frank and Grace (0.16 each) said the tip helped.; Moderator filed 'hurt': Bob turned 'never ask or suggest targets by PM' into a reason to hang Mallory and Frank, both Vanilla. This is a misapplication, since the tip governs your own conduct and isn't a test of alignment. t41 now says so.; Ivan filed 'hurt': secrecy about his Scrutineer target read as evasion. Partly a misapplication: the tip says to post results in full once they exist, and he posted only half.; Alice filed 'hurt': she named Carol as her ward on D2 and was blocked N2-N3. Heidi announced her N4 self-protection, and Carol attacked Alice instead.; Moderator, Alice, Heidi, Judy filed 'helped': unclaimed Medic Heidi and Veteran Alice were never targeted until Alice's alert caught the last holder, while Mallory (Day 1 hint) and Erin (public claim) were the two Village deaths.; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Erin (0.16 each): Carol's Day 1 promise of a result tomorrow got her blocked and Katana'd on N1. - [t41] (Village) Map who starts PM clusters and who co-nominates. If the same few players DM each other early and then push the same nomination with no new information, treat them as a likely Yakuza bloc. Once one of them is exposed by a mechanical result, use the shared stated votes and pushes against the others. Link a PM to what happens next: if A messages B by Day and the player then under discussion (a fresh investigator claimant) dies to the Katana that night, A and B are a strong Yakuza pair. Ask sender and receiver separately, in public, for one sentence on what the PM said, compare the two answers, and don't accept a vague 'just a question': nominate one of them if no real answer comes. While mechanical results exist or are coming, a PM link alone is a lead, never a hang on its own, and what a PM said is never a mechanical fit. But a pair who PM heavily every Day plus one independent break on either member is a case to convict that Day: a slip about a night plan their claim can't have ('Night 2 cover' from a no-action claimant), a claimed role that is Yakuza-only (t227), a denied visit on a kill night, or their own account that the PM was advice on the other's role claim ('keep your Tracker explanation consistent') when that claim is a contested duplicate with nothing behind it. 'Wording isn't evidence' is no reason to acquit that, and each night of delay costs an investigator. Once one of the pair hangs, the partner who defended them is the next shot or hang (t34). When no investigator is alive and no result is coming, a pair who PM every Day, vote identically and both claim no night action is the best case on the table: nominate one of them before the vote lock (t286) rather than a quiet solo player. Asking a claimed Lookout whom they'll watch, suggesting targets or testing a claim by PM is careless villager play that t39 warns against, not evidence of Evil. A villager who breaks that advice is still most likely a villager. A Jester or Executioner leaves PM trails on purpose: one player privately asking several others for Yes votes, or a lone early PM to a player later exposed as Yakuza, fits a Neutral as well as a Yakuza. A villager PMing for votes on a credible result is pushing clumsily. Judge the target on their own results, not on who pushes. Before hanging on a bare PM link while better evidence may still arrive, require stated votes that the totals back up, or a mechanical result that needs them to be a teammate. Look hard at quiet players who pile onto a PM-only hang. | support: 42 games, total weight 15.17 | against: 10 reports, total weight 3.72: Bob (Jester), Carol, Ivan and the moderator: Bob's lone D1 PM to Niaj (Pharmacist) was treated as proof he was Yakuza, and he was hanged 13-0. Jesters can exploit the PM heuristic deliberately.; Moderator: Heidi's vote-soliciting PMs were Executioner activity, not a Yakuza bloc. The Yakuza (Ivan, Bob, Carol) used the PM angle as their main deflection for D3-D5. Frank, Judy, Mallory and Niaj still found it useful pressure.; Grace: with no PMs sent, the co-nomination pattern alone was weak and led to misplaced trust in both Yakuza.; Alice and the moderator: the PM clusters were ambiguous. Carol's private request to Grace to shoot Heidi was treated as a scum signal and helped hang the real Oracle on D6.; Alice: PMs were sparse and mostly from one player after a death, so PM clusters identified no one this game. The tip is conditional on PM clusters existing.; Ivan: his two PMs to Grace were treated as bloc evidence and helped hang him, a Vanilla. The tip's own caveat (a PM link is a lead, not proof) was ignored.; Frank filed 'hurt': no PMs were sent all game, so the tip gave no signal. The tip is conditional on PM clusters existing, so this is mostly a non-application.; Moderator (0.4), Judy (0.16): the town hanged Jester Judy on a PM-only case, exactly the trail the caveat warns about, and the Yakuza then used the 'private whip' framing to hang Vanilla Frank.; Ivan (Yakuza) filed 'hurt': Judy's PM trail fed Jester reasoning that kept the town wary of its own hangs. Weak, since it worked as the caveat intends.; Moderator filed 'hurt': the Carol-Bob PMs (both Village) became the main suspicion thread, Judy (Clerk) nominated Carol over them, and the Yakuza killed Bob as a 'PM hub'. The real bloc sent no PMs. The tip already says a PM link is only a lead, so this is a partial misapplication. | recent evidence: Frank, Ivan, Alice (0.16 each): the Bob-Carol PM link was acted on only together with Bob's copied Cartographer claim and Carol's Avenger death, and the shot hit the last Yakuza. Dave adds that PM volume alone is not a case.; Bob, Frank, Heidi (0.16 each): the D5 PM pair pushing the same hang was Bob and Dave, both Village. Heidi (Grunt) built her defense on it. They were the Yakuza pair. The Village named the lead but never nominated either.; Moderator (0.4), Heidi (0.16): the Carol-Grace PM pair (about 15 messages, both denying the same ground) was the Yakuza team. The Village kept it as a lead and convicted only once an independent break arrived (the 1-token audit and the Juror vote check).; Heidi, Erin, Carol (0.16 each): the Alice-Carol PMs followed by a shared push on Heidi were noticed, but nobody made each of them summarize the PMs in public before the vote. Carol was the last Yakuza steering Villager Alice, so the pair was mixed.; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Heidi (0.16 each): the Bob-Grace PM pair was the whole Yakuza team. Carol flagged the D1 PM, and all three investigators aimed their N1 actions at the pair (track and check on Bob, watch on Grace). Bob was never made to summarize the PMs.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Erin, Frank, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the town asked Dave and Bob in public what the PMs said. Their own accounts (coaching Bob's Tracker claim) were the break, Dave was hanged 4-3 and both were the whole Yakuza team. Erin adds: get each side's exact wording, not one side's paraphrase.; Heidi, Dave (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Carol's PM burst to Erin and Grace before the Alice nomination pointed at the whole Yakuza team. It came after the lock, with no independent break left to convict on. Carol's 'hurt' is from the Yakuza side and says the tip is accurate. Frank filed the same lesson.
- [t43] (Village) Survival alone confirms nothing. A power-role claimant who survives a Katana attack may be real, since Evil rarely spends its Katana on a fake, but Arsonists and Serial Killers have innate Standard Defense that stops the Katana at its usual Standard level, and Jesters and Executioners have Basic Defense. A Katana notice also no longer shows that the Yakuza attacked: a Villager with the Stolen Blade trait makes one Standard attack a game that reads exactly like the Katana, so the player it hit can be anyone, a Yakuza included. A survivor is told which role attacked them but never who protected them (R17). So ask the survivor once for the exact attack notice and for any Defense of their own (e.g. a Medic token self-protection), and ask the table which Medic or Kitsune covered that player that night. Don't spend a Day pressing the survivor for a protector's name they can't know. Survivors: give one account of the night, once, and don't revise it. If no credible Medic or Kitsune claims the save, keep innate-Defense Neutrals in the pool and check the claim against token audits and visit logs. One unexplained survival has two other causes the survivor can't know about: the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which saves any player once (t370), and a Shaky holder, whose Katana never kills (t355). A Not Evil Sheriff read doesn't clear such a survivor: Serial Killers and Arsonists read Not Evil, so an unexplained Katana survival plus a Not Evil read fits a Serial Killer. Add any evasion about their own night action and it is your best hang: make the case by Day 3, before the killer thins the Village. A survivor who first says 'innate Defense' or 'nobody protected me' and later claims Medic is far more likely a Neutral killer than a protected investigator. Role-blocks are different: only the blocked player is told, so 'I was blocked' is the claimant's own word and verifies nothing by itself. But it fully explains a missing result, so never treat that gap as a lie. Test the claim with an audit or a Lookout instead, and weigh repeated blocks reported before anyone pressed the claimant as mild support. | support: 25 games, total weight 10.17 | against: 11 reports, total weight 2.59: Ivan: Carol's Katana survival and Grace's protection story were unverifiable, and survival can come from innate Defense or an unclaimed protector. Carol wasn't a power-role claimant, so this is partly a misapplication.; Dave: Heidi's Katana survival was cited for her Lookout claim, but it was Arsonist innate Defense.; Erin: the town built a trial on the protector question; Heidi's survival came from innate Standard Defense.; Mallory: Heidi's survival came from innate Defense, not a protected Good role.; Alice filed 'wrong' because Mallory was blocked N3-N5 and then killed. Mallory was a real Oracle, so the tip held; this is a misreading.; Frank filed 'wrong': the Katana avoiding a Veteran isn't evidence for the claim. This is partly a misreading, since Dave was never attacked or blocked and the tip is about survivals and blocks, but it's a fair caution.; Alice filed 'wrong': the Katana killed claimants regardless. Misreading: no claimant survived an attack or was blocked, so the tip never applied.; Alice, Carol, Ivan and Judy (0.16 each) filed hurt or wrong over Ivan's N3 block. Mostly a misreading: blocks are told only to the blocked player, and the Village treated the missing prosecution as a lie. The moderator (helped) says the block half was simply ignored.; Dave, Heidi, Grace (0.16 each): survival from Arsonist innate Defense was read as support for a Medic claim. Mostly a misreading of a shortened sheet line.; Bob filed 'hurt': the Dave/Alice survival-plus-save pair confirmed each other circularly. Weak: both claims were true.; Heidi filed 'hurt': the table pressed Alice on D2 to name a protector for her N1 survival. A protected player is never told who protected them (R17), the real answer was Heidi's charm, and the question ate the Day. Alice and Dave (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the pressing did bring out the Kitsune claim, and nobody treated Alice as cleared. Edited so the protector question goes to the table.; Dave (0.16) filed 'hurt': the sheet line 'survival confirms nothing' was used to brush off Carol's survival of Alice's Basic shot with no protector named, and Carol was the Katana holder. Partly a misreading (the entry says to ask for the attack notice and keep such a survivor in the pool), and Frank notes the survival came from Narrow Escape, which any player can have. See t370. | recent evidence: Grace: Ivan's claimed protection explained Heidi's N2 Katana survival, which made both claims trusted.; Carol: Dave's N2/N4 survivals were explained by Alice's protection, not treated as proof by themselves.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Erin, Heidi (0.16 each): Bob survived the Katana plus a Serial Killer attack on N3 and asked at once. Erin's public Strong-protection claim matched, and both became credible.; Frank: survival claims by Dave and Heidi were asked about at once, and Alice's Medic claim pointed to the swap.; Alice: asking Bob directly for his protection targets exposed the gap between his Medic claim and the kills.; Grace: Heidi's public account of her N2 Standard protect matched Grace's Katana survival and made the Sheriff claim more credible.; Moderator, Bob, Carol, Heidi, Grace: Dave survived the N1 Katana with no protector named and read Not Evil to Carol. - [t44] (Village) Never hang a claimed power role just because their action produced nothing visible: no results, no announced prosecution, 'no named target'. A role-block makes any action invisible and spends no token, and only the blocked player is told. Ask the claimant for their night target and whether they were blocked. Then check it with an audit (a blocked Prosecutor still holds 2 tokens), a will or a Lookout. Require a concrete contradiction. The pair or trio who build a case on 'no visible result' and push it in lockstep are the suspects. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.16 | recent evidence: The Yakuza trio pushed Alice to trial on D3 over her empty results; Bob had blocked her every night. Alice, Carol, Frank and Grace all flagged this.; Moderator (0.4); Frank, Grace, Judy and Ivan (0.16 each): the blocked Prosecutor was lynched on 'no named target'.
- [t48] (Village) When an announced trial total (Guilty plus Innocent) exceeds the number of living voters, a Ballot Stuffer doubled a vote. Ballots are secret, so compare each side's total with the players who said they voted that way. The side with more votes than stated holds the extra vote, or a hidden liar. The extra vote can sit on either side, including the losing side and the nominee's own Innocent vote. This narrows the stuffer to that side's voters; it doesn't name one. Separate the candidates using each one's stated position against the totals, plus any token or claim evidence (an audit sees ballot tokens), before hanging. Once a mechanical result identifies the stuffer, stop treating the other candidates as mechanically implicated. | support: 6 games, total weight 3.12 | recent evidence: Heidi saw an extra Innocent at Carol's trial and an extra Guilty at Ivan's, matching Carol's own votes, plus Carol's 1-token audit. Carol was hanged and flipped Ballot Stuffer. Frank, Erin, Dave and Judy agreed.; Judy and Ivan: the 8-vote total from 7 voters proved a Ballot Stuffer, confirmed the archive entry and located the stuffer among the Innocent voters.; Grace, Heidi, Judy, Mallory and Carol: the extra counted vote proved a living Ballot Stuffer and, combined with slot math, pointed at Erin's Vanilla claim.; Moderator, Heidi, Bob, Grace, Judy: Bob's trial counted 3 Guilty against 2 revealed (Dave, Mallory). That narrowed the stuffer to two players, and Mallory's stance/ballot mismatch picked her. She flipped Ballot Stuffer.; Frank, Erin: Dave's D4 trial counted 4 Guilty of 5 votes from 4 voters, proving a Ballot Stuffer among the Guilty side (Bob, Grace, Carol). Frank noticed but nobody followed it up; Carol was the stuffer.; Grace, Heidi (0.16 each): Alice's D4 trial showed 4 Guilty of 6 votes from 5 voters. The table read the extra vote as a doubled ballot on the Innocent side, not a count error; it was Alice's own.
- [t50] (Village) Don't hang a power-role claimant just because the claim came late or is hard to verify, especially when the result fits the accused's own admissions. Before voting, ask who is pushing the trial and who benefits, including whether a Jester or Executioner wants the hang. This doesn't protect a claim made on trial that only explains away an Evil result (a Notorious trait claim, a Jester claim). No investigation sees traits, so test the role claimed beside it against that role's mechanics, the claimant's votes and their PM links, and don't acquit on the claim alone. | support: 11 games, total weight 5.45 | against: 4 reports, total weight 0.96: Judy and Carol: Niaj's and Erin's late claims (Ex-Con, Accountant) were reasoned away as 'not proof', and the town delayed hanging both Yakuza. Erin's claim came only after she denied the visit.; Moderator: Ivan and Dave had this tip but never asked who benefits when lone 'Tracker' Heidi was hanged 11-0. She was a Jester. The tip needs an explicit Jester/Executioner warning.; Grace: her Roleplayer 'Oracle' result was dismissed as a counterclaim, and she was hanged on the rigged vote while the Yakuza benefited.; Olivia: the tip helped acquit Carol (Diviner) on her fake Ex-Con claim on D2. | recent evidence: Judy led the hang of Ivan, who had a coherent, checkable claim, on her own unverifiable count inference. It cost a villager and her credibility.; Bob and Erin: Bob's late Cardinal claim was weighed on its mechanical fit and he was kept alive. Dave's miss on Erin wasn't treated as an alignment result.; Moderator: Bob's late Medic claim survived 3-8, and the only Guilty votes were from Yakuza Dave and Mallory.; Moderator: the town acquitted Carol on her Ex-Con claim alone, with no test of its mechanics. She was Yakuza.; Judy: Heidi's late Party Animal claim alone didn't justify a D2 Guilty. The town waited and hanged her on stronger vote evidence.; Grace: asking who pushed and who benefited kept Mallory and Olivia alive after their late claims.; Frank, Heidi, Grace, Judy and Ivan: asking who benefits was the missing step before the 3-2 hang of the unverifiable but credible Veteran Dave by a Yakuza-led bloc.; Ivan and Frank: nobody asked who benefited from hanging the hard-to-verify Prosecutor; Erin and Heidi led the wagon.; Ivan, Bob, moderator: nobody asked who was pushing Erin's D4 trial. SK Grace nominated and Yakuza Alice piled on, while Bob's mechanical lean (the SK killed Heidi, who tracked Grace, then Dave, who reported on it) pointed at Grace.; Alice, Judy, Mallory: don't hang a claimed exact-role investigator whose results are consistent and testable. Dave's hang was the swing that lost the game.
- [t52] (Village) A will that names a living player is a lead, not a verdict. Check the named player's own vote record and claims before hanging on it the same day. A Fabricator can replace the will of anyone who dies that night, so also check the will against what the dead player said in public: a lean on someone they never suspected, an argument a suspected player already made, or a rules claim the table has corrected marks a forgery, and then the will points at the Yakuza rather than at the player it names. Because your own will can be replaced, state your current leans in public during the Day as well. | support: 3 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Grace and Mallory: the case on Alice came from Mallory's will alone. It was correct this time, but thin evidence.; Dave, Erin, Frank: Heidi's will named Dave as Yakuza and was false. Both were real Trappers, and nobody tested the will's reading against the rules before the 3-2 hang.
- [t55] (Village) While a Ritualist may be alive with tokens, every firm exact-role claim (Archivist, Eavesdropper, Bloodhound, Juror, Seer) is a near-certain extra kill on top of the Katana, through any Medic. A wrong guess outs the Ritualist publicly and its tokens are spent either way, so it waits for sure claims: first-turn claims of roles a Roleplayer can't be told (anything except Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle, Accountant) backed by a result that fits known facts. A living Diviner also feeds it exact roles, and a Wiretap reads private claims. Survey or archive results can pin a survivor's role indirectly. Name your exact role only when it changes a vote, never just to introduce yourself or with no result. If the hang is already passing without you (a marked suspect, a broken claim), don't add your claim to it: keep the result in your will and stay alive for the next one. A result in your role's own words ('attack count for this trio') names the role as surely as the claim does. When a result must go up, claim only as much of your role as it needs, write in your will what each possible result of tonight's action would mean, and expect to die that night. Once a Ritualist kill has appeared, unclaimed players should stay vague. | support: 6 games, total weight 4.16 | recent evidence: Moderator, Judy, Dave and Mallory: Bob killed Ivan (via a Diviner check) and Carol (via Erin's will and her claim) with unannounced Ritualist guesses.; Moderator and Carol: Carol's list confirmed her as Archivist and Ivan killed her N2. Mallory's survey put the Seer in Carol/Dave/Erin, Dave claimed, and Ivan killed him N3. Both were certain guesses.; Ivan: Bob's public Lookout claim made him a certain Divine guess on N2, through the Medic's protection.; Peggy: Heidi's public Surveyor claim made her a sure Divine guess on N2.; Erin, Ivan, Olivia, Mallory, Carol, moderator: Dave (Bloodhound) and Carol (Juror) made firm public claims and Judy killed both with correct one-token guesses on N2 and N3. Vague Mallory and Olivia and Vanilla Erin were never ritual targets.; Moderator, Erin, Frank, Heidi, Niaj, Judy (1.0 each): Erin claimed Bloodhound on D2 though Carol's marked hang was already certain. Olivia's 2-token Divine guess killed her on N2 and her trio count of 3 on Dave/Judy/Olivia was never posted.
- [t59] (Village) When a trial total exceeds the number of living voters, a Ballot Stuffer exists. Recompute the threshold at once (count the extra vote) and see whether any trial can still pass. If evil is near half with the stuffer, hangs are impossible: kill the stuffer or the evil bloc at night, and act on mechanical deductions before parity arrives. Count evil's kills per night (Katana + Ritualist + misfires), not just heads. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Judy, Ivan, Niaj, Heidi, Frank, Carol and Dave: at 4v3 with a stuffed vote, every D3 trial tied 4-4. Then the Katana plus Ritualist took two Villagers a night.
- [t60] (Village) An archive or partial survey is a sample, not a census. It shows that roles exist, never how many. Never say a duplicate claim 'proves' a liar. Say instead that it shifts the odds, e.g. 'only half the archive's entries are real, so three claims of a role listed once are worth testing'. Overstating the math gets a correct suspicion discredited and the trial acquitted. Once a list is public, keep a role inventory every Day and post it. The list is one claimant's report until something corroborates it. Of the N roles shown, floor(0.5*N) are real and the rest are fakes drawn from all roles in the rules. So a listed role may be absent, and a role listed once may have an unsampled second holder. Vanilla Villager and Grunt are never in a game unless a hand-picked list names them, so an archive entry showing either is one of the fakes: strike it, which leaves fewer fakes among the rest. Use slot math to decide whom to press for an exact claim and whom to audit, watch or guess. Then hang on the contradiction that test produces (e.g. a token count against a tokenless claim, an Evil check), not on slot math alone. Roles proven by public effects (a prosecution proves a Prosecutor, a clean a Mortician, an ignite an Arsonist) are certain. Track Unique roles (Smuggler, Vulture) separately: once one is proven, every other living claimant of it is a proven liar. A claim made right after a result that only explains that result is not placed, so keep it open. When the inventory narrows to a few unclaimed players, demand an exact claim from each; a refusal then is heavy evidence, and a 'Vanilla' answer is a lie (t307). Keep deductions conditional: corroborating one entry doesn't verify the whole list. | support: 14 games, total weight 7.26 | against: 4 reports, total weight 1.70: Judy: an archive is a random sample that includes fakes, not a census. A role listed once can have another unsampled holder, so a duplicate claim isn't automatically a lie. The tip's 'duplicates a role listed once marks a liar' is wrong for archives.; Dave, Ivan and Judy filed 'hurt': the slot math rested on an archive only Bob had seen, and it split votes for days before the audits gave checkable facts.; Moderator (0.4) filed 'hurt', and Alice, Dave, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'hurt' or 'wrong'. Carol's census-style slot math on a partial archive drove bad trials of Bob and Carol and wasted D3. This is partly a misapplication, since the full tip already warns about samples, but the warning was too easy to drop.; Dave filed 'hurt': Carol's list never produced a decisive test and only added noise. The moderator says the list was never actually analyzed, so this is mostly a non-application. | recent evidence: Moderator, Alice, Dave, Erin, Olivia: Erin's archive plus Frank's surveys confirmed Alice and Dave, cornered Carol and isolated Bob's Vanilla claim.; All 13 players and the moderator credited daily slot math with every Yakuza hang.; Moderator, Heidi: the archive plus surveys isolated Bob as Clerk. Dave's and Judy's 'wrong' reports quote the old 'duplicate marks a liar' wording, which the current entry already corrects.; Moderator, Grace, Alice, Judy, Heidi, Frank, Dave: Grace's N1 archive showed Grunt, Pharmacist and Diviner. After the Pharmacist and Diviner flips, Alice's Prosecutor claim and the Oracle's Jester hit on Frank, only Bob was left for Grunt, and he was hanged 6-1.; Moderator: Grace and Alice used the archive to narrow Pharmacist and Grunt to Bob, Frank and Ivan, then set aside Jester-like Frank. Both of Alice's prosecution targets were Yakuza. Alice, Dave, Frank and Heidi said to do the slot math earlier.; Moderator, Ivan, Alice, Judy: Grace's daily archive slot math narrowed Pharmacist and Grunt to Bob, Frank and Ivan; Ivan's refusal to state any role for days was the strongest read on him.; Bob and Grace: the archive showed one Grunt, which exposed Erin's Grunt claim. The list plus Frank's 3-token audit fit Dave's Veteran claim.; Moderator: Bob's daily slot math narrowed the Grunt to five players and forced the Vanilla claims that audits then broke.; Moderator, Heidi: Bob's slot math put the Grunt among Carol, Erin, Grace, Ivan and Mallory, and it pointed at Erin. Mallory (Yakuza) said it put the team under pressure. Bob added a reminder that an archive is a sample.; Moderator: Carol's list showed Clerk and Ritualist (both real: Judy, Erin) plus Lookout and Roleplayer. Nobody did slot math, and the never-claiming Erin, Frank and Judy were never pressed.
- [t70] (Village) When a Clerk rig is announced, the Yakuza wanted that nominee dead. Treat the nominator, the players who pushed the nomination and everyone who said they voted Guilty on that trial as prime suspects. Ballots are secret, so compare the Guilty total with the stated votes to find hidden Guilty voters. Hang from that group next. Don't drift back to old suspects. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.60 | recent evidence: Dave, Erin and Niaj: the D6 rig on Heidi pointed at the Yakuza bloc, but the town never followed it up and lost at 3v2.; Moderator, Alice, Bob, Judy: the rigged Grace trial exposed nominator Judy and the Guilty trio Carol/Judy/Niaj, and the town hanged exactly those three on D3-D5.; Bob, Carol, Erin, Frank, Ivan (0.16 each): Erin was executed after a 1-Guilty-of-4 verdict and the rig was announced, which proved a living Clerk. The nominator, Ivan, was the Clerk. With 3 alive and the Day over, the Village had no hang left to use it.
- [t73] (Village) If you're told you are puppeted, you can't chat that Day, so write it in your last will at once and expect to be killed that night. Your nominations and votes are still your own: vote by your own reads, and vote No or Innocent on whatever your puppet pushes. If you are alive the next Day, say it in your first message before anything else: you were puppeted, every message and claim posted under your name that Day was the Puppeteer's, and what your real role and log are, as far as a vote needs them. A late or partial correction reads as a claim switch and gets you hanged for the puppet's lie. Name whoever the puppet's claim helped, or who immediately backed its push, as the likely Puppeteer. Town: a confirmed player who suddenly pushes out of character may be puppeted. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.18 | recent evidence: Niaj's will exposing the puppeting was hidden by Alice's clean, so the town never learned it.; Frank recorded the puppeted D3 post in his will next to his true Scout results, and the town used them after his death.
- [t78] (Village) On Day 1 (talk only, when nobody has a result) and on Day 2, power roles with no result worth posting (Party Animal, Medic, investigators with an empty Night 1) should avoid being the voice that steers discussion or calls out quiet players. The most leader-like voice is a common Katana target that night, Night 1 included. Investigators with a useful Night 1 result should still post it on Day 2 (t1); save the rest of your influence for when you have information. Staying quiet means not claiming yet, never a false Vanilla claim. A Vanilla claim later upgraded to a power role reads as a cornered switch, gets Oracle-tested, and the excuse 'I was hiding as the tips advise' convinces nobody. Village: a Vanilla-to-power switch right after a group or count places the player is a Yakuza tell, whatever reason they give (t292). | support: 15 games, total weight 8.46 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Alice, Bob, Grace, Judy, Mallory, Niaj, Frank filed 'hurt': the Yakuza used the tip as a ready excuse for a fake Vanilla-to-Medic switch. The town saw through it, so this is exploitation of the wording rather than proof the advice is wrong. Edited. | recent evidence: Olivia: forward-looking mechanical warnings on D1 read as an investigator and drew the N1 Katana and a clean.; Carol: she claimed Archivist and steered the Day, and was killed the next night.; Dave and Bob: early claimers Frank, Grace and Carol died while quiet players lived.; Heidi claimed Scout on D2 with no result to post and was the N2 Katana kill. Her 'hurt' on t32 actually confirms this tip.; Niaj: the loud early claimants Heidi and Mallory drew the attacks and pushes.; Grace: Alice led D2 loudly with a Census Taker claim and was killed N2.; Bob filed 'hurt', but it confirms the tip: he led D2 loudly with a Seer claim and a nomination and was the N2 Katana kill.; Ivan: Erin, who led loudly, was the N2 kill as predicted.; Heidi: with an empty N1 watch she was first to name a trial pick against a confident claimant, became his target and was hanged on D2.; Erin (Scout): she never claimed, but spoke first on Day 1 and set the agenda, was read as an investigator and was the N1 Katana kill.
- [t80] (Village) Accurate information proves access, not alignment. A Diviner can post real role results as a 'Surveyor', a Wiretap can quote real private messages as an 'Eavesdropper', and an evil player can truthfully expose a rival killer. A visiting killer can also present their own real visits (douses, a Katana hit) as 'Tracker' tracks, which then fit Fortune Teller counts and Cartographer groups perfectly. A fit with visit totals shows that the visits happened, not who made them or why. Before clearing an info claimer, ask which hostile role could produce the same evidence. Then look for a check that tells them apart: an Oracle guess, an Accountant audit, or a Lookout on the claimant's target. An exact-role result from an investigator with a proven record outweighs visit-count consistency. Confirming one detail authenticates only that detail. When such a claimer is exposed as hostile, re-examine everyone they cleared, but don't hang the subjects of their true reads just because they were 'confirmed' by them. | support: 5 games, total weight 3.88 | recent evidence: Frank, a Diviner posing as Surveyor, fooled the town for three days. Grace, Ivan, Mallory, Judy and Niaj all reported this.; Bob's real Diviner info under Surveyor cover fooled the town until audits and FT counts broke it. Nine players reported this.; Moderator (0.4), Grace and Judy (0.16 each): Alice's true Diviner groups (Arsonist in Ivan/Judy/Bob, Vigilante in Bob/Carol/Dave) were taken as proof she was Village.; Alice, Carol, Grace, Judy: correct Surveyor-style hits didn't clear Diviner Frank. A census and a profile decoy did the work.; Dave, Frank: Grace's real Talent Agent Counts (Fortune Teller, Enforcer) matched the deaths and were read as hard to refute. They showed access, not alignment; she was Yakuza.
- [t89] (Village) Don't vouch for other players in your last will unless something independent of their own claims verified them. A dead player's endorsement carries extra weight and props up a Yakuza. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Mallory's reflection on the trusted fake claims.
- [t90] (Village) Estimate the Yakuza count early (about 3 in 11, 4 in 12-13) and count evil votes every day. Before each late-game hang, work out the worst case and how many more mis-hangs you can afford: if every unconfirmed player could be Yakuza, a mis-hang may hand them the win. Ballots are secret, so don't assume a stated Guilty was real. When one more wrong hang gives Evil the majority, nominate only from the remaining unconfirmed pool, never a player outside it over a self-admitted slip. Once the Yakuza are one night kill away from a majority (e.g. 4 of 7), everyone with information must post it now, before that night; results revealed after evil holds the votes are worthless. Near that line, spend the hang on the strongest Yakuza suspect rather than a Neutral killer, who doesn't add to the Yakuza vote. | support: 11 games, total weight 6.08 | recent evidence: Moderator: the D5 mis-hang of Dave at low count lost the game outright. The town should have spent the hang on the best-evidenced suspect, Bob.; Moderator: the town let it reach 3 Yakuza vs 3 Village by D5, then lost the vote. The bloc lead had to be used while the Village outnumbered it.; Moderator: D4 was the last day the four Village players could hang on their own. Nobody nominated, it was 3v3 by D5, and the Yakuza hanged Dave on D6. Ivan (0.16) said a 3-of-5 bloc owns every trial.; Moderator: at 6 alive with 3 Yakuza, Bob's nomination of Grace stalled at 3 Yes despite strong evidence. At 5 alive the Yakuza hanged Frank 3-2. The Jester hang had cost the Village its numbers.; Moderator and Grace: at 5 alive, a single vote decided every nomination. The Village didn't do the math and counted the Jester as a swing voter too late.; Moderator (0.4) and Bob (0.16): at 4 alive Judy hanged Arsonist Bob with Yakuza votes, leaving her alone against Erin and Heidi, and the Katana ended it N5.; Moderator: with two Yakuza votes in a 5-player trial, one Village Guilty decided Carol's hang. The town didn't count the votes first.; Moderator: on D8 at 5 alive, Niaj joined Grace and Judy to hang Bob over a 'bloc PM'. Grace and Judy had matched 4/4 nominations and 2/2 trials. The hang handed the Yakuza the win that night.; Moderator (0.4), Bob (0.16): at 5 alive on D3 (2 Yakuza, SK, 2 Village) Bob gave the Yakuza pair the third Guilty on SK Grace. That left 2 of 4, the Katana on Bob made 2 of 3, and Erin was hanged D4. The hang belonged on the Yakuza lead, not the Neutral killer.; Frank and Judy: count parity before every late hang. The D5 hang went to the Arsonist rather than a Yakuza lead and handed the Yakuza the vote.
- [t91] (Village) Jester warnings: suspect a Jester in a player who pushes hard with no evidence, self-nominates 'as a test', votes Yes on their own nomination (or misstates that vote), refuses offered tests of their own result, or makes a lone, easily disproved claim that contradicts a well-supported report and seems eager to be tried. A typical case is jumping onto a survey or archive pool's role so the real holder counterclaims. Track the conversion: if an archive lists Executioner and anyone has died at night, an Executioner whose target that was is now a Jester. An Evil Sheriff result on a Vanilla claimant with 0 tokens fits a Jester or Executioner as well as a Yakuza. An Evil read on a player who self-nominates or won't prove their claim is the Jester signature: a Yakuza who reads Evil doesn't ask for the rope. A self-nomination is never a reason to vote Guilty. A mild early dissent plus a lone PM to a player later exposed as Yakuza doesn't rule a Jester out. A caught liar is a liar, not necessarily a Yakuza, and reckless, checkable lies are what a Jester tells. If a more clearly Yakuza player is available, hang them first. While the Katana still kills, don't spend the Day on a proven liar who doesn't fit as a killer. Before any hang, ask whether the nominee wins by being hanged, and if so prefer to leave them alive. Naming the Jester risk aloud and then voting Guilty anyway is the worst of both. If you must hang, agree in public on a bare majority of Guilty votes and have everyone else vote Innocent. The revenge Divine-attacks a random Guilty voter, and no protection stops it. Confirmed power roles (Medic, investigators, a Prosecutor with tokens) must vote Innocent, and so must anyone with the Cursed trait (R24), whose curse would kill a second Good player. | support: 25 games, total weight 13.89 | against: 8 reports, total weight 1.44: Bob filed 'hurt': five days without hanging Judy let the Katana kill. Misapplication: the stall came from not hanging Grace, not from sparing the Jester. See the new trial-slot tip.; Ivan filed 'wrong': no Jester was in the game. Untested.; Bob filed 'hurt': the 'does the nominee win by being hanged' check is framed around Jesters, so it never prompted a Vulture count before the D6 Frank hang. See the new t276.; Alice, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj filed 'hurt' and Erin 'wrong': the warning identified the Jester but did not stop 8 Guilty votes, and she won. Non-application: each says the tip was right and wasn't followed.; Alice filed 'hurt': she read Bob's self-nominations as a reason to vote Guilty, the warning reversed. Misapplication, but another game where the warning didn't stop a unanimous Guilty.; Erin filed 'wrong' and Frank 'hurt' (0.16 each): no Jester was in the game. Grace's hard trial fight was an SK's defense, and Erin's 'vote Innocent with me' was read by some as self-serving and used to dismiss a real Villager. Untested rather than disproved; the check doesn't separate a killer from a villager who fights the trial.; Heidi filed 'wrong': 'ask whether the nominee wants the hang' gave nothing at two alive, where the stall clock decided the game. Untested rather than disproved: no Jester was in the game. Alice's 'helped' (refusing Carol's requests to self-nominate) is a stretch of the tip; t337 now carries that point.; Frank (Grunt, won) filed 'wrong': sparing Erin was predictable and gave the Village no progress, and her self-nominations ate rounds for four Days. Weak: the cost was the Day 4 no-hang, which t236 covers (sparing the Jester is no reason to skip the Day's hang), not the refusal to hang her. | recent evidence: Niaj and Ivan: census-cleared non-power players should take the Guilty votes on possible-Jester trials, so confirmed info roles can stay Innocent and the revenge pool stays small.; Moderator and all thirteen players filed 'helped': Bob voted Yes on himself, lied about it and held an easily disproved lone claim. Only Judy (Yakuza) acted on it and voted Innocent. The Village voted 8 Guilty with 5 needed, the revenge hit Cardinal Olivia, and her curse killed Mallory.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Carol, Frank, Olivia, Dave (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Mallory self-nominated and said 'hang me' after an impossible Vanilla claim, and Ivan's profile listed Jester. The trial still went 8-4 with Ivan and Niaj on Guilty. The revenge hit holder Dave only by luck.; Moderator (0.4) plus Alice, Bob, Carol, Erin, Frank, Grace, Ivan (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Dave (Executioner turned Jester when Bob died) self-nominated four times and PM'd for Guilty votes. Alice, Erin and Frank voted No or Innocent every time, so no Jester win and no revenge. Dave's 'hurt' is from the Jester himself: the tip worked as intended.; Moderator and Heidi filed 'helped': Bob self-nominated twice and read Evil; the town hanged him 6-0 anyway, the Jester won, and the revenge killed Medic Alice. The Jester won and the revenge drew Sheriff Alice, who had voted Guilty. Erin's 'wrong' says the tip was sound and wasn't followed.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Bob, Dave, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Erin (Jester) self-nominated seven times. Erin's 'hurt' is from the Jester herself: the tip worked as intended.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Dave, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped': Frank self-nominated on D2 with no data, Alice named it Jester bait, and it drew only his own Yes.
