- [t96] (Village) If the last known Yakuza dies and the game doesn't end, a win-blocking Neutral is still alive (Vulture, Serial Killer, Arsonist); a continuing game does not prove a living Yakuza. Before hunting 'the last Yakuza', count the deaths that could already account for the whole team: hangs on mechanical cases, a trap or Enforcer death (that player attacked), and a Vigilante kill on the same night as a Katana kill (the shot victim may have been the holder; no Katana death the next night confirms it). One later Katana death doesn't prove a living Yakuza either: a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade kill has the same cause, and a trap or Enforcer death can be that Villager too. Katana deaths that go on night after night do show a living holder. If every Yakuza may be dead, the blocker is most likely a quiet no-action or late claimant with nothing checkable, often one who pushes hangs without results (a 'Vanilla Villager' claim is a lie outright, t307): hang them by vote, and don't spend a kill token on a claim clash first. Count the players, check the Vulture threshold (max(floor(N/4),2)) and hang that claimant before the count falls to it. Don't hang a player who says they are Cursed (a trait, R24) near the endgame: their death Divine-attacks a random Good player at the end of the following night and can hand a Vulture the win. No investigation sees traits, so the claim can't be checked; look at who is pushing that hang and count the worst case before voting. | support: 5 games, total weight 3.14 | recent evidence: Moderator and Dave: after Judy died the game continued. Heidi (Vulture) nominated Cardinal Erin, whose hang would have left 2 alive. Dave and Erin blocked it, and Heidi was hanged.; Olivia, Bob and Peggy: after all three Yakuza flipped and the game continued, the town forced claims and per-night ledgers, tracked the Vulture threshold and hanged Erin.; Dave, Niaj (0.16 each): count the Vulture threshold and track unexplained survivors. The D6 mis-hang brought the game to Alice's win.; Bob, Carol, Erin, Mallory, moderator: the game continued after Dave's N4 death because of Vulture Olivia (Vanilla claim), not a Yakuza. Hanging her at 5 alive won; the wrong 'a Yakuza is alive' premise cost Alice first.; Dave, Niaj, Alice: the game went on after the last Yakuza (Grace) was shot on D4, so a win-blocking Neutral was alive. The town hanged Frank, the claimant with nothing checkable, the same Day and won.
- [t98] (Village) Judge a Roleplayer claim by its timing and consistency. A Roleplayer is told a fake investigative role and believes it: they claim that role from the start, never claim Vanilla, and never 'know' they are a Roleplayer. They can only suspect it, once their results clash with a confirmed claim or result (an exact-role check, a self-proving action, a verified log). A real one flags the clash early, before others claim the slot, and pushes against other Roleplayer claimants. A Vanilla claim that later becomes Roleplayer, an 'I know I'm the Roleplayer', or a claim made only once cornered or prosecuted is almost certainly a lie. When two players claim the same investigative role and their results conflict, or an 'Oracle' result contradicts a claim that mechanics back (a claimant the Katana attacked, a Seer whose pair a trait claim explains), consider an honest Roleplayer before calling either one a liar. A Roleplayer's results are drawn at random from what the role could be told that night, whatever really happened, so each is true only by chance: a CORRECT means as little as a WRONG, nobody they 'cleared' is cleared and nobody they accused is accused. One result that checks out doesn't prove a claimant real. A log that keeps matching independently verified facts night after night makes a Roleplayer less likely, and one that fails against a verified fact is what random draws look like. If an archive, profile or survey shows 'Roleplayer', the duplicate claim may need no Evil player at all. A Roleplayer is Village: discount the claimant with the odder, unverifiable result instead of spending the Day hanging them, keep the other claimant's results, and spend the hang on the unclaimed pool or on whoever the discounted results protected. Also be suspicious when 'blind' results land exactly on the night's kill victims or fit claims made that same day. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.26 | recent evidence: Judy flagged possible Roleplayer on D3 before anyone else and was trusted all game. Niaj's Lookout 'results' landed on both Katana victims after Grace and Dave claimed. Erin went Vanilla to Roleplayer only once prosecuted. Both were hanged. Grace, Mallory, Dave and Carol reported this.; Alice, Frank, Olivia, Grace and Heidi all named Erin's Vanilla-to-Roleplayer pivot as mechanically impossible for a real Roleplayer. It decided the D7 hang.; Moderator and eight players: hanging Roleplayer Frank over his clash with Seer Erin cost a Village life; discounting his results was the right move. Olivia's 'hurt' on the role-text tip is non-application.
- [t99] (Village) If you hold a role a Roleplayer can be told (Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle, Accountant) and your results contradict a confirmed claim or result (an exact-role check, a self-proving action, a verified log, a claimant whose Katana attack a trap death confirmed, a role revealed at death by a Chronicler's mark), suspect first that you are the Roleplayer. A 'CORRECT' of yours on a role the player is then revealed not to hold is that contradiction. Don't explain it with a swap unless someone posted a swap notice for that night, and don't build theories on it ('so a Pharmacist is still alive') in public or in your will. Say so publicly and at once, before anyone else claims: post the result with that caveat, and don't build an accusation or stake your life on it. Volunteering it reads strongly town and locks in a timeline that later fake Roleplayer claimants can't match. Test yourself when you can: an Oracle can guess a claim the town already trusts, and a WRONG there means your results are random noise. A RIGHT there doesn't clear you: a Roleplayer's results are random draws and come out true by chance, so keep checking each new result against verified facts. 'CORRECT' Vanilla guesses that only their target confirms say nothing about whether your results are real. | support: 4 games, total weight 3.40 | recent evidence: Judy (real Roleplayer) did this on D3 and was trusted all game. She was hanged instead of flagging herself as a possible Roleplayer.; Frank, by counterexample: he never flagged himself as a possible Roleplayer though mechanics backed Erin, and was hanged 8-1 on D4.; Ivan (Roleplayer, 1.0) and the moderator, by counterexample: Ivan's 'Carol = Pharmacist CORRECT' was contradicted by her Mortician reveal. - [t101] (Village) Delay-the-hang pitches ('tomorrow's test settles it, Innocent costs nothing', 'vote Innocent to expose a rig', 'no nomination before tonight's results') are classic Yakuza lines. Every night of delay gives the Katana a free kill, and when it kills an information role each night, the result you are waiting for usually dies with its holder. When a hang already has a corroborated mechanical basis, ignore rig-test pitches; the pusher is often the Clerk. Once a player is the best mechanical fit (named by slot math, fits the observed blocks or visits, refuses to claim a role), hang them that Day. Before granting a reprieve for a test, state which result would discriminate, what interference is likely, and what happens if the test doesn't occur. A blocked or missing check is no result, not a clearance. Ask whether the test resolves today's dispute; if not, hang the strongest case today. Exception: when the whole case is one unverified claimant's result against the accused, and the accused offers a specific test of that claimant's result, the test is worth the night (t296). Don't hang either side of that duel; if you want a hang that Day, take it from the unverifiable claims beside it. Second exception: when the case against a nominee is only a lean or a wording 'contradiction', the nominee offers a free exact-role check of their own claim by a credible living Oracle, and the vote lock is not one death away (t286), don't hang them that Day. Either take the test or hang the other candidate the elimination left, and check who is pushing the lean. | support: 18 games, total weight 8.28 | recent evidence: Grace, Erin (0.16 each): D2 and D3 ended with no nomination while the table waited on an unresolvable count, and the Katana took Grace and Heidi. - [t121] (Village) An Evil Sheriff result on someone whose claim doesn't explain it is a real case, but it fits a Jester or Executioner (both read Evil, both have 0 tokens), a Notorious Villager or a Turncoat as well as a Yakuza. - [t127] (Village) Weigh a visit report by when it was made, but only together with confirmation. A count posted first that the real visitors contradict gains nothing from its timing. A list or counter-result that only repeats public claims is weak. - [t144] (Village) Cross group results (Surveyor, Diviner, census-type alignment counts) with token audits, confirmed roles and independently corroborated claims to eliminate candidates. A nonzero token count rules out tokenless roles (Arsonist, Serial Killer, Jester, Grunt, Vanilla). A confirmed role removes that player. A death doesn't: roles stay hidden, so a dead member is still a candidate for the night's group. A 'k of 3 Good' count becomes a named suspect once the other members are confirmed: nominate and vote on it rather than waiting for a direct contradiction. Remember that an alignment count reads true alignment and never counts Neutrals, so a non-Good slot may be a Neutral, not a Yakuza. It cuts the other way too: an 'Evil 0' count clears the group of Yakuza only, and a Serial Killer, Arsonist or Executioner sits in it unseen; only a Good count that covers the player (3 of 3 Good) rules a Neutral out. In an 'Evil 1' group exactly one member is Yakuza, but another member may be a Neutral steering the hang onto them (an Executioner on its target), so check who started the push. A player's own claim is a claim, not corroboration. State which reports the elimination depends on, then press the last candidate. A Vanilla claim doesn't explain a survey slot. | support: 9 games, total weight 5.55 | recent evidence: Moderator, Frank, Ivan and Judy: Judy's survey said one of Ivan/Mallory/Niaj was the Arsonist. - [t199] (Village) When a Clerk may hold an unused rig, a passed nomination can be an execution, and an Innocent majority won't save anyone. Treat every Yes as a vote to hang: never vote Yes 'just to get a trial' or 'so they can answer'. Nominate your top suspect first, before they can nominate anyone else. If a player from a suspected pool (above all one who has never claimed a role) opens the Day by nominating someone, vote No on the nomination itself however reasonable the nominee looks, and publicly ask trusted players to do the same. The rig cuts both ways: it executes whoever is successfully nominated, the Clerk included, so voting the suspect's nomination down and passing one on the suspect can hang the Clerk on their own rig. This matters most at 4-5 alive, where one rigged hang plus the night kill can leave two alive and no nomination can pass again. A rig is announced only when the trial goes Innocent; an execution after an Innocent verdict proves a Clerk was alive last night, and the nominator is the first suspect (t70). - [t227] (Village) A player who publicly claims a role that exists only on the Yakuza side has given you the strongest lead you will get. The full list: Grunt, Pharmacist, Ritualist, Puppeteer, Fabricator, Clerk, Smuggler, Ballot Stuffer, Fixer, Diviner, Mortician, Wiretap, Informant, Talent Agent. This holds even if they label it '(Village)' and withdraw it as a typo once called out, and above all when an exact-role result (an archive, an Insomniac's visitor list, a profile, a survey) shows that role and nobody else claims it. Check each claimed role, and each role any result shows, against that list the moment it is posted, and always before you vote in that player's trial: tables have twice acquitted on 'wording isn't proof' while the claim itself named a Yakuza role. Treat such a claim as a confession, not a wording slip, and hang that claimant the same Day, ahead of any vaguer case: left alive, they kill that night. Accurate results don't clear it. Evil info roles like the Diviner and Talent Agent get true information (a Talent Agent's Counts will match the deaths), and a player confirming one of their hits authenticates that result, not the claimant. 'It was a slip, show me a mechanical result' must not shield them. Nominate and hang them while the Village still has the numbers, and if you said you lean Guilty, vote Guilty. Once they hang, name the next lead the following morning (t34): take that trial's Innocent total, subtract the nominee's own vote, and whoever argued for Innocent in public holds the rest. At 4-5 alive that player is the Day's hang; don't let it get lost behind a claim dispute between two Villagers. | support: 11 games, total weight 4.64 | recent evidence: Frank: claims that switched to Clerk or Smuggler after a nomination were treated as strong Yakuza tells, and both hangs hit Yakuza.; Moderator: Erin said 'the Grunt it lists is me', then claimed Vanilla. - [t232] (Village) If you hold an exact-role result showing that a nominee is a Jester (an Oracle RIGHT, a survey or archive slot that pins them), post it in full (night, target, exact role, result) before the nomination vote, or at the latest at the start of the trial. Voting Innocent while hinting 'a hanged Jester wins' changes no votes. - [t238] (Village) Spend the Day's three trials purposefully. Nominate only someone you'd vote Guilty on if their answer fails, and state the test before nominating. A trial nobody intends to convict uses up a slot, reveals nothing and can end the Day without a hang. Keep a public count of the R14 stall clock (quiet Nights and Days since the last death), and when deaths stop, push for a real test or a mechanical nomination early in the Day. | support: 5 games, total weight 2.16 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): on D3 Bob, Carol and Frank were each tried and acquitted 0-8, ending the Day with no information. Pick one checkable contradiction and push the whole Village onto it.; Heidi and Erin: chained nominations on single reads and bare answers (Dave, Alice, Erin, Frank) cost the town Villagers. Require a stated, checkable basis before nominating.; Alice, Grace, Ivan (0.16 each): nobody tracked the R14 clock until Day 5-6 at 4 alive, when the Village had to choose between a thin hang and a stall loss. They ask for a stated nomination deadline.; Alice, Bob, Dave, Erin, Heidi (0.16 each): nobody counted the R14 clock until the last two players were deadlocked. N7-N9 passed with no death and everyone lost.
- [t239] (Village) Under secret ballots, stated votes alone prove nothing: Evil players can announce Innocent and vote Guilty, or split their stated votes to look independent. At small counts, though, the totals can catch a misstated vote. If 3 of 4 voted Guilty and the nominee surely voted Innocent, everyone else who said Innocent lied, miscast or had their vote changed. Do this arithmetic after every small trial and publicly name whose stated vote the totals contradict, then ask that player to re-check their reminder and explain. An honest miscast or mispost is the commonest cause, and a player who corrects their own stated vote in public is more likely confused than Evil. Check for manipulation too. A Fixer flips one player's trial votes without telling them, and a Ballot Stuffer adds a vote (compare the total with the voter count). Nomination Yes counts can't be altered by either, so they are the cleaner check, and an Auditor sees the vote as counted. The mismatch is a lead, and the player becomes the next hang only when nothing mechanical points elsewhere. Never hang it ahead of a player with a mechanical killer case (an unexplained Katana survival, a first-person attack slip), look at which unconfirmed players are pushing that hang, and at 4-5 alive count what the hang leaves behind first (t206). | support: 4 games, total weight 1.36 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.40: Moderator: the small-trial arithmetic named Grace (stated Guilty, total 2-2). Following 'make that player the next hang', Heidi joined Dave and Alice to hang her. She was a Villager who had miscast, and the SK won. | recent evidence: Moderator: on D5 Erin said she would vote Innocent on Bob and Heidi said Guilty. The trial still went 3 of 4 Guilty, which exposed Erin's lie, but the split made the two Yakuza look independent going into the final night.; Carol, moderator: the totals revealed Judy's hidden Guilty. Bob and Heidi caution that arithmetic resting on assumed votes is only a question to ask, not grounds for an accusation.; Moderator: the small-trial arithmetic found the hidden Guilty among the stated Innocents.; Moderator: at 5 alive Alice's trial went 3-2 Guilty. Carol and Dave stated Guilty, and Heidi had said 'leaning Innocent', so the totals showed her stated vote was false. Nobody ran the arithmetic; it would have pointed at the SK.
- [t240] (Village) A profile lists three roles in alphabetical order: the target's true role, one role held by another player alive at the start of that night (never the Profiler's own), and one role that no player in the game has. You aren't told which is which. Before posting a profile, ask the target to state their exact role publicly, so they can't tailor a claim to the list. Asking first is the method, not a tell: never hang a Profiler for 'posting lists after the claims'. The moderator builds the list, and a claim made before the profile that lands on the list is strong confirmation, because a fake claim only hits it by matching the one in-play entry. If a profile names your own true role, treat that Profiler as very likely real and back them. A list that omits the target's claimed role is a hard, hangable contradiction, unless it shows Roleplayer and they claimed a role a Roleplayer can be told. A profile also becomes an alignment read by elimination: if the target won't claim the list's Good role, or a trusted living claimant already holds it (it is then the likely 'other player' entry), every option left is Evil or Neutral. Roles can repeat, though. Two claimants of the listed Good role can both be real, and then the list's not-Good entry is most likely the in-play one. Once the target's role is settled, the other two entries split: one is a living player's role and the other is absent from the game. So a Yakuza-only leftover (Clerk, Wiretap, Pharmacist, Smuggler, Ritualist, Diviner, Ballot Stuffer) is a strong lead that the role is alive. The same not-Good role appearing in profiles of two different targets is almost certainly in play: say so aloud and hunt its holder among the claims nobody can check (vote-only roles such as Juror or Scrutineer, Vanilla), with Oracle guesses and audits aimed there. A leftover Arsonist or Serial Killer is the same lead for a Neutral killer: start the hunt (t245) before the ignite. If living players claim both leftover roles, one of those claims is almost surely false. Cross every profile with deaths, census results and confirmed claims to strike options. Post the full three-role list once it bears on a claim or a vote, and write it into your will as soon as you have it: a named Profiler is a sure guess for a possible Ritualist. Spend tokens on unclaimed players or on the contested side of a claim fight, whose role would decide a vote. Profiler: post the list as a test, not a nomination; nominating on a three-role list costs you the credibility you need. Don't re-profile an active claimant to confirm them. | support: 7 games, total weight 4.68 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Carol's N3 profile of Vanilla Frank listed Clerk and Wiretap as decoys, nearly proving Erin (who had quoted a PM) was the Wiretap. Nobody used it, Carol was hanged, and the Yakuza won. Carol (0.16): combine profiles with deaths to eliminate options.; Olivia's profiles on cleared players listed Diviner, Wiretap and Ballot Stuffer as decoys, proving those roles were in play. The town stopped PMing. Dave, Judy and Bob noted the same.; Olivia's profile of Erin listed Diviner as a decoy, pointing at Frank's Surveyor claim. Reported by Olivia, Dave, Niaj, Carol, moderator.; Moderator (0.4), Erin (0.16): Erin's N1 profile of Bob (Cartographer, Fabricator, Mortician) left him only Cartographer, which Alice already held, so he read as not Good. Her N3 profile of Archivist Dave left Arsonist or Puppeteer in play; nobody said so and the Arsonist ignited two nights later.; Carol, Frank (0.16 each): Carol spent all three profiles on N1-N3 on live questions (Frank, Heidi, then Erin). The posted lists backed Frank's Bounty Hunter claim and Heidi's Kitsune claim. Her announced N3 profile of Erin died with her.; Dave's real Profiler lists (Auditor/Ballot Stuffer/Informant on Mallory; Auditor/Ballot Stuffer/Mortician on Bob) were correct and pointed at the living Ballot Stuffer. Every player and the moderator named the recurring filler as the missed lead.; Moderator (0.4) as not applied: Heidi spent her N2 profile confirming Frank's already-backed Tracker claim while Grace's untested 'Sheriff' and never-claiming Carol went unprofiled. Her N3 profile of Erin (Bloodhound/Puppeteer/Trapper) pointed at the Puppeteer but came after the lock. The moderator adds that a Katana victim's profile can have its Yakuza options struck.
- [t241] (Village) When a player quotes or closely paraphrases a private message they weren't party to, only an Eavesdropper (who visited the sender or receiver the night before) or the Yakuza Wiretap could have read it. Ask at once which role, and which player they eavesdropped. The named target must be in that exchange, and the visit must fit any Lookout, Tracker or visit-count data. A claim that can't name a fitting target, or a role list or profile decoy showing Wiretap, points to the Wiretap. Don't vote on the quoted PM's content until the quoter's access is explained. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.88 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Alice, Carol, Heidi, Frank (0.16 each): Erin quoted Heidi's PM to Carol almost verbatim, and four players flagged it. Her untested Eavesdropper claim survived, the town never checked it against Carol's Wiretap decoy, and Erin won.
- [t243] (Village) When roles stay hidden on death, use the night kill list as a claim test. A claimed investigator killed by the Katana was probably real, because the Yakuza kill the threats they can't explain away. In a duplicate or conflicting claim (e.g. a Sheriff calling a Seer Evil), the rival who survives deserves the test (audit, exact-role check, watch), and a fake read calling the Katana victim Evil is very likely false. A Katana death makes it very unlikely the victim was Yakuza, not certain: a Villager with the Stolen Blade trait can make one kill a game whose cause reads 'Katana', on anyone. It doesn't show the victim was Good either: a Neutral can die to the Katana too, and it doesn't confirm every claim they made. Weigh it against the possibility that the Yakuza killed a real townie to leave a fake standing, and act on it before the next night rather than after. A Roleplayer is only ever told Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle or Accountant, so a Katana-killed claimant of any other info role (Census Taker, Coroner, Bloodhound...) can't be an honest Roleplayer. Don't spend a Coroner token or a Day verifying them: act on their posted result at once. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Alice and Grace (0.16 each): real Lookout Bob was the Katana kill after the D2 duplicate-claim fight. Archivist Carol and Vigilante Heidi died later the same way.
- [t245] (Village) Hunt Neutral killers on purpose; an unhunted Arsonist or Serial Killer often wins. But count the vote lock first (t286). Hanging a Neutral killer leaves the Yakuza count unchanged, so while two Yakuza may be alive at 5-6 players the Day's hang goes to your best Yakuza lead, even a weak one. That holds above all for an Arsonist who has just ignited: the douses are spent and it needs several nights to threaten again, while the Katana kills every night. Be wary of an unconfirmed player who opens with a quick round-1 nomination of the Neutral: the Yakuza want the rival killer gone and your hang used up. Only killers that block wins matter here: a Bounty Hunter, Magpie, Jester or Executioner never blocks a Village win, and a Bounty Hunter who has spent its one token can't attack again. While the Katana still kills every night, never spend a Day's hang on a confessed or spent never-blocks-wins Neutral. Roles stay hidden on death, so the death announcements are your main evidence. Read every cause of death. An attack cause that isn't the Katana, a hanging, a Vigilante shot, a hidden gun, a Veteran alert, a trap, a Bounty Hunter attack, a Jester or Executioner revenge, a curse or Avenger (e.g. a Serial Killer attack or an Arsonist ignite) proves that killer is alive. A 'hidden gun' death shows no side: Armed can be a Village or a Yakuza trait, so it may be a Villager's shot or a second Yakuza kill that night, and it never points at a Neutral killer. A 'Katana' cause is nearly always the Yakuza's, but a Villager with the Stolen Blade trait makes one attack a game that reads exactly the same: one stray Katana death doesn't prove a living Yakuza or that the victim wasn't Yakuza, two Katana deaths in one night don't show two holders, and neither points at a Neutral killer. A death by 'Avenger' means that player made a fatal attack on someone else who died that night. Two fatal causes on one victim mean two separate attackers, except that a hidden gun beside a Katana can be one Armed Yakuza who held the Katana. A multi-death night, or more deaths than the Katana, known shooters and trait effects explain, points at an ignite. Quiet nights don't clear an Arsonist, who kills no one until it ignites. From Day 2 keep a written Neutral-killer suspect list: unclaimed or Vanilla claimants nobody has watched, players who survived an attack with no credible protector, players whose visits leave unexplained gaps in counts or groups, passive-result claimants whose numbers don't fit the public visits, and anyone who admits an action but won't name it. Point Lookouts, Trackers, Oracle guesses (Arsonist, Serial Killer) and audits (0 tokens fits both) at them. A Not Evil Sheriff read doesn't clear them. The game can't end while one lives, and at two alive you can't hang them, so hang the best SK or Arsonist suspect while the table is still large enough that the Yakuza can't reach the lock afterwards. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 13 games, total weight 7.36 | against: 5 reports, total weight 2.52: Moderator (0.4), Heidi and Ivan (0.16 each) filed 'hurt': the 'hunt Neutrals' line fed the D4 hang of spent Bounty Hunter Erin, who never blocks wins, while Yakuza Grace kept killing.; Frank, Heidi ('hurt') and Carol ('wrong'), 0.16 each: the later D5-D6 hunt drifted into PM-based hangs of Vanillas. A misapplication: the tip asks for mechanical pointers, not PM reads.; Frank filed 'wrong': no Neutral killer was in the game, only a Bounty Hunter. Untested, not evidence against the tip.; Frank, Judy and the moderator (1.0 each) filed 'hurt': the town followed 'hunt the Arsonist' at 5 alive with two Yakuza alive and lost. Partly a misapplication, since the full entry already warned about the lock, but the warning came last. Edited.; Dave (0.16) filed 'wrong': no Neutral killer was in the game, so the line drew attention to a threat that didn't exist. Untested, not evidence against the tip. | recent evidence: Olivia, moderator: the D3 hang went to Jester Bob, who never blocks a win, while all three Yakuza lived. It was the Village's last real hang; D4 opened at 3 Yakuza of 4.; Bob, Carol, Olivia, Grace, Erin (0.16 each): the 'Serial Killer' cause on Frank's and Niaj's deaths proved a live Neutral killer and narrowed the hunt to Erin, hanged D4. Erin (SK) says it made her position harder.; Moderator (0.4, helped), Grace, Erin (0.16 each): the N5 'Arsonist' causes on Frank and Ivan narrowed the hunt to the four living players, and Heidi was hanged D6. Carol's 'Avenger' cause named her as Alice's attacker. Heidi's 'hurt' is from the hanged Arsonist.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Alice, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped': the 'Serial Killer' causes plus Dave's unexplained Katana survival built the right case on D4 and drew a 2-2 trial; it came a Day or two late. Dave's 'wrong' is from the SK and describes non-application.; Moderator filed 'helped': the 'Serial Killer' causes on N1-N2 kept an SK in the pool. Alice pressed Grace, a late Vanilla claimant read Not Evil, and her Strong Enforcer attack killed the SK on N3. Grace's 'hurt' is from the SK herself.; Moderator (0.4), Erin (0.16) filed 'helped' as not applied: the swallowed SK attack on N1 and the SK cause on Grace proved an SK alive, and Grace's charm showed it wasn't Alice. Nobody pressed late-Vanilla Heidi on D3, and she won alone.; Erin, Grace, Mallory, Niaj (1.0 each) filed 'helped': the town tracked the 'Serial Killer' causes, kept unwatched Vanilla claimant Grace on the list despite her N1 Not Evil, narrowed by block confirmations and hanged her D5 on the Oracle RIGHT.; Moderator, as not applied: the D3 kill went on spent, never-blocks-wins Bounty Hunter Frank (by Sniper shot, not hang) while the Katana and an SK were both still killing. Nobody asked who gained from removing him.; Bob, Carol, Olivia (1.0 each) filed 'helped': after the last Yakuza died the town pressed the unconfirmed Vanilla claimant and hanged Vulture Olivia, the last win-blocker. Carol adds that thinking about Neutrals a Day earlier would have saved Alice.; Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Judy, Niaj filed 'helped': Frank (Arsonist) stayed on the list because a census can't see Neutrals, and he was hanged at 4 alive for the win. Carol and Judy note he doused three players unhunted until then.
- [t251] (Village) A Seer pair becomes a mechanical case only through an anchor. An unanchored DIFFERENT pair shows only that the two read differently, which may come from a Notorious or Turncoat Villager, a framed player or a Good-reading holder rather than a Yakuza. It is a lead, never a hang on its own. Once one member is independently confirmed Good (a Sheriff Not Evil plus an Oracle RIGHT, a self-proving action, a verified log), a DIFFERENT pair names the other as non-Good, so nominate them. Short of a full anchor, weigh the two members: the one who claimed only after being named and has nothing checkable is the likelier non-Good half, and the one whose posted result later claims have borne out (an archive that named roles before they were claimed) is the likelier Good half. After a hang, a Coroner exam confirming the hanged player's role extends the chain. Once one member has been hanged and other evidence backs that they were the non-Good half (a reveal, their broken claims, their vote record), the pair is spent and no longer points at the survivor: the survivor should say so each Day, and nobody should reopen it in a will or fire a kill token on it. A SAME pair between a confirmed Yakuza and another player points at that player too, unless the confirmed Yakuza held the Katana that night (then the partner read Good). Before the vote, say which anchor the chain rests on. | support: 4 games, total weight 2.76 | recent evidence: Moderator: Carol was anchored by Bob's Not Evil and two Oracle hits, so Niaj's N3 'Carol/Alice different' hanged Alice. Ivan's Puppeteer exam plus the N2 'Alice/Frank same' hanged Frank. Ten players said a lone pair is only a lead, and the anchor is what made these pairs decisive.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Ivan, Niaj, Mallory: lone pair reads were unreliable. Moderator: the decisive N4 random pair (Heidi/Carol differ) was anchored by Sheriff Heidi's N2 Katana survival and exposed Carol.; Moderator, Grace: Frank's N1 pair (Erin/Carol different) alone was only a lead. Carol's exam confirming Frank as Seer, plus Ivan's profile of Erin, anchored it and the SK was hanged 6-3. Erin, Heidi and Niaj stress that a lone pair stays thin.; Erin, Ivan, Grace: the Judy/Niaj DIFFERENT pair became a case only once an anchor arrived (Judy's trap death fixing her as holder, plus Grace's counts). Niaj was hanged 10-1.
- [t255] (Village) Before nominating, count the stated Yes votes and nominate only when a majority is clearly there. A failed nomination followed by a public walk-back of your Yes wastes a round and gives opponents a lasting line against you. If the case isn't ready, post it and let the votes build, then nominate on a later round or Day. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.06 | recent evidence: Moderator: Bob nominated Frank on D2 with 4 Yes (5 needed), then retracted his Yes. The Yakuza used it against him for days. Frank was hanged D3 once the case was stronger.; Bob: his three nominations of Dave drew 3, 2 and 2 Yes. He never counted the Yes votes he could credibly get before spending budget on them.; Ivan: count the Yes votes before nominating; his 4-Yes failure gave nothing and made him the night's kill. Dave: his nomination of Mallory on a three-role list cost him credibility.
- [t256] (Village) Test a late Jester or Executioner claim on trial with mechanics, not the revenge threat. (1) Win check: Jesters, Executioners, Bounty Hunters and Magpies never block a win; every other Neutral (Serial Killer, Arsonist, Vulture) does. If the game didn't end when the last known Yakuza died, a Yakuza or one of those blocking Neutrals is still alive. That alone doesn't show which, but if the claimant is the only plausible blocker left, the Jester or Executioner claim fails and the hang is right whichever blocker they are. (2) Night pattern: a no-kill night on which only the claimant was blocked fits them holding the Katana. (3) Foreknowledge: did they name a role before its owner claimed it? That points to a Yakuza info role (Diviner, Wiretap), not an Executioner. (4) True-alignment counts: a census 'not Good' fits Neutral or Yakuza, so it decides nothing alone but combines with the above. If these converge, hang with a bare Guilty majority and keep confirmed info roles on Innocent. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Frank (Diviner) claimed Jester on D5. Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia and the moderator all cited the same proof: the game hadn't ended at Heidi's hang, N4 had no kill while only Frank was blocked, he had claimed Oracle before Grace spoke, and the census read him not Good. Hanging him won the game.
- [t258] (Village) Note on your notepad anyone who claims or names a role before its real holder has spoken, e.g. a Day 2 Oracle claim made before the real Oracle claims, which then forces a counterclaim. Picking exactly the role a silent player holds suggests exact-role or PM knowledge (Diviner, Wiretap), not luck. It is a lead on Day 2. It becomes decisive later, when the player retreats through cover claims (Roleplayer, Jester, Executioner) that can't explain that knowledge. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Carol, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj, Judy: Frank's D2 Oracle counterclaim came before Grace spoke. He was a Diviner, and the foreknowledge helped break his later Jester claim.
- [t262] (Village) Each morning, combine the visit-based results into one ledger: Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer sizes and named map groups, and Lookout or Tracker logs. Have every claimed visitor post their exact target, then reconcile. Together the counts fix how many hidden visitors exist and which groups they sit in. A single unexplained visitor, a 'no action' claim from a player in a group with an unexplained slot, or a missing kill on a block night almost always names the Katana holder. | support: 4 games, total weight 2.90 | recent evidence: Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj: Carol's counts plus Dave's sizes and map plus Erin's watches left one unexplained visitor each night and drove all three hangs.; Niaj, Heidi, moderator: the combined FT, Cartographer and claim ledger narrowed the holder to 2-4 names each night.; Moderator, Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Grace: combining the Insomniac role list, the map group and the Katana death in one message left one unexplained visitor, and Bob nominated at once.; By counterexample: nobody combined the claimed visits and wills into one ledger, so Olivia's and Grace's impossible passive results went unchecked for Days.
- [t268] (Village) A claim already shown false stays false after the claimant 'corrects' it. 'Already corrected' is not an answer, and you don't need a new contradiction. Token claims are the cleanest test. A Magpie's victim is told which token kind was stolen, so a token count posted after a theft notice is a checkable false claim. An Accountant audit gives the true count. A Medic's Standard protection of another player is free, so '0 tokens' never explains a Medic protecting nobody while players die. Ask a token claimant once, plainly, whether they got a theft notice, and line their posted counts up against every audit and theft. If the story has changed to fit each new fact, hang them. Apply one standard to every nominee: don't acquit a broken claim and then hang someone else on lobbying alone. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4) plus Bob, Carol, Frank, Judy, Niaj, Olivia, Mallory and Alice (0.16 each). Grace (Yakuza) posted 2 Medic tokens after a Magpie theft notice, Mallory audited her at 0, and she never used free protection. She survived 4-4 and 3-4 as 'already corrected' and went on to kill four more Villagers.
- [t269] (Village) Work from the roles that actually exist. If nobody claims a visit-tracking role (Tracker, Lookout, Fortune Teller) after the first request, stop asking for visit logs and work from votes, token audits and claims. Vote-reading roles (Juror, Scrutineer, Auditor) need trial data, so a Day 2 trial gives them something to read. But 'a trial for vote records' is a reason to open a trial, never to execute. Agree beforehand that it ends Innocent unless a corroborated case appears, and don't put one side of a live claim duel in the dock for it. Expect little from the records when the Yakuza vote with the majority and state their real votes. The ballots mostly pay off once you have a suspected pair to compare. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.12 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Moderator, Mallory: Carol, Ivan and Frank asked for a Day 2 'trial for vote records', and it became Lookout Heidi's execution. Every later Juror and Scrutineer result only confirmed stated votes, because the Yakuza stated their real votes. | recent evidence: Moderator: Day 2 ran 6+ rounds with no nomination, so Juror Carol and Scrutineer Frank had nothing on Night 2. Players kept asking for nonexistent Tracker and Lookout logs through Day 7. Frank (0.16): with no trial, a Scrutineer has nothing to scrutinize.; Moderator, Grace: D2 had about 70 'wait for Night 2' messages and no votes, so the vote-reading Juror and Scrutineer had nothing on N2.; Erin, Grace: with only Frank's Day 2 trial on record, the Scrutineer and both Jurors had almost nothing to read before the Katana reached them. Dave's caveat fits the tip: a trial opened on a bare claim-vs-claim dispute put a real Juror in the dock, and its votes are excluded from any Juror pair that includes the nominee.
- [t272] (Village) Read the Katana's victim list as 'who threatened the Yakuza', not 'who threatened today's suspect'. Keep a running list of each victim, whom they had last checked or challenged, what their next result could have shown about every living player (an audit reads any token-holder, a Bloodhound's trio count covers any attacker drawn into it), and who survives. When the Katana keeps killing whoever checked or pressed one quiet, uncontested claim, the player behind that claim and those who never challenged it are your leads. But when the victims were testers of a loud, already-disputed claimant who is left alive, don't conclude the kills protect him. Yakuza kill investigators for their own safety and leave a caught liar, often a Jester, standing as tomorrow's free mis-hang. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.96 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Moderator, Erin, Ivan, Olivia: Carol and Erin read the N2 kills of Grace and Dave as 'protecting Bob' and led his hang. The Yakuza chat said 'Bob is the natural D3 hang: leave him alive'; the victims threatened the Yakuza's own tokens and attack counts. | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Niaj (0.16): Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Frank, Alice, Carol and Niaj each died after questioning a claim, mostly Grace's or Judy's, yet the pattern never drove a hang.; Moderator's review: Dave died the night after nominating Grace, Frank the night he announced a check on Carol and Grace, Erin the night after naming Carol. Every victim had just pressed the same quiet pair, and the pattern wasn't used until D5.
- [t280] (Village) A Smuggler swap redirects every action aimed at either swapped player, whether or not it visits: the Katana, a Medic's save, a Sheriff check, a Seer pair, a Chronicler's mark, and Astral checks such as an Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a Scrutineer pick or a Cartographer's map. The result still names the player the user chose. Random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cursed player's curse) are not moved. Before hanging an investigator whose result looks impossible, or calling a survival or death story a lie, ask whether anyone posted a swap notice that night, then recompute the result with the two players traded. Only a reported swap explains an odd result. Both swapped players are always told, so if no Villager reports one when asked, don't lean on a swap: a Smuggler shown to exist proves only that swaps were possible, not that one happened. Check the fit too: the role or alignment reported must belong to the swap partner. When the odd result comes from a role a Roleplayer can be told (Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle, Accountant) and no notice exists, random Roleplayer results are the likelier explanation (t361). Smuggler is a Yakuza role, so a swap notice proves a living Yakuza Smuggler. Post it the next Day. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Moderator, Alice, Bob, Erin, Frank, Heidi, Judy, Mallory filed 'hurt' on the sheet's 'a swap explains an odd result' line (Dave as Yakuza too): once Dave was revealed as Smuggler the town used swaps to explain Roleplayer Ivan's wrong CORRECTs. No swap happened and nobody had posted a notice. Carol, Ivan, Niaj filed 'helped' only because the wrong story kept correct hangs going. Edited to require a posted notice. | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Grace's N4 Juror check on Alice/Dave landed on Alice/Niaj because Niaj and Dave were swapped, and Niaj had posted his notice. The town still called her result a lie and hanged a truthful Juror. Niaj, Erin, Judy, Heidi, Frank, Ivan and Dave (0.16 each) all stressed tracing where swapped actions land.
- [t282] (Village) When the Yakuza could be near half the living players, assume a Fixer or Ballot Stuffer can turn any trial. Don't vote Yes on a nomination without a real mechanical case. Once the trial opens, one fixed Innocent vote becomes a Guilty and can decide it, even the nominee's own vote. Count the likely evil bloc first, and pass only nominations the Village could convict on its own votes. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: at 3v3 on D6 Bob and Niaj voted Yes on Mallory's nomination of Heidi. Judy had fixed Heidi, so Heidi's own Innocent counted Guilty, and the trial passed 4-2 instead of tying 3-3. The Village lost the next Day.
- [t286] (Village) Count down to the vote lock. A nomination needs a majority of living voters, so once the Yakuza hold half the living players they can block every nomination by voting No. With 1 Yakuza that happens at 2 alive (a nomination needs both votes), so at 4 alive a mis-hang plus the night kill leaves exactly that, and the hang at 4 alive is your last. With 2 Yakuza it happens at 4 alive (3 Yes needed); with 3 Yakuza, at 6. You aren't told the team size, and a table of 8 has had 3 Yakuza: then the Night 1 kill leaves 7 and the Night 2 kill brings the lock, so Day 2 is the only Day the Village can hang on its own votes. On a small table treat Day 2 as that Day unless something shows the team is smaller. The Katana takes one Villager a night, a Ritualist can add up to two more over the game, an Armed Yakuza's hidden gun can add one more (Armed is no longer Village-only), and a Jester's revenge or a Cursed player's curse adds others. Ritualist victims are never Yakuza and Katana victims almost never are (the one exception is a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade attack, whose kill also reads 'Katana'), so unless a hang was confirmed Evil, assume every Yakuza is still alive. With 3 Yakuza in 13, two mis-hangs can bring the lock by Day 4, so the Day 2 hang needs a corroborated fit, not a claim duel, and the Day 3 hang must go to a kill-faction candidate. The Day before the lock is your last real hang. Put your best suspect on trial that Day, even on a 50/50 group result such as a census that leaves two names, and prefer the defender of a hanged near-certain Yakuza to a disputed claim. A hang that removes a Neutral killer doesn't move the Yakuza count: at 5 alive with 2 possible Yakuza, hanging the Serial Killer leaves 2 of 4 and the night kill makes it 2 of 3. That Day's hang must go to your best Yakuza lead, even a weak one such as a PM pair, and before you cast a deciding Guilty, check whether the other Guilty votes come from that pair. Don't wait for a result that only arrives after the next night, because its holder is the likeliest kill. Post the count each morning: 'X alive, at most Y Yakuza, lock after Z more deaths.' | support: 10 games, total weight 5.36 | recent evidence: Moderator plus Alice, Grace, Dave, Carol (7 players, 2 Yakuza). On D3 at 5 alive, Dave and Grace voted No on Frank's nomination of census-suspect Erin to wait for Grace's D4 Coroner read. Grace died N3, every D4 nomination failed 2-2, and N4's Katana plus Ritualist ended it. A 3-2 hang of Erin was available on D3.; Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj, Mallory: nobody counted down to the lock. Two mis-hangs plus double kills left the Yakuza with 3 of 4 votes on D4, and Ivan's nomination failed 1 Yes of 4.; Moderator (0.4), Bob, Frank, Grace, Alice (0.16 each): at 4 alive with 1 Yakuza the D4 hang was the last real one. The mis-hang of Dave led to 3, then the Katana left Grace alone against Carol, and her four nominations got 1 Yes each.; Moderator, Frank, Heidi: at 6 alive with 2 Yakuza, one wasted hang plus the Katana and a Jester revenge left Dave against both Yakuza on D4. Nobody counted before the D3 vote.; Moderator: after N4 it was 2 v 2 and the Yakuza could vote down every nomination. Only Bob's Day shot on Carol broke the lock; waiting for N5 results would have lost.; Moderator (0.4), Bob (0.16) filed 'helped' as not applied: on D3 the Village was 4 of 6 with one Yakuza and an SK alive and hanged nobody. N3 cost Alice and Carol, the D4 hang at 4 alive was the last one possible, and Bob's nomination of Ivan at two alive failed 1-1.; Frank, Judy and the moderator: at 5 alive with 2 Yakuza, hanging the Neutral killer let the Katana bring the Yakuza to 2 of 3. Frank's mechanical break of Grace's Scout claim came on D6, after the lock.; Moderator, as not applied: at 4 alive on D4 (1 Yakuza, a Jester, 2 Villagers) the hang was the Village's last. The Day ended with no hang, and N4 (Katana on Alice, Alice's shot on Heidi) ended the game.; Moderator (0.4) filed 'helped' as not applied: the whole town waited through D2 despite Bob's evasions, and Tracker Carol was killed that night. The Village still won only because Grace's Sleepwalker visit hit the Veteran's alert.; Moderator (0.4) filed 'helped' as not applied; Alice, Bob, Heidi (0.16 each) filed the same lesson: at 4 Village v 3 Yakuza on D2 the town repeated 'no nomination without new data' for about 8 rounds. Frank died N2, it was 3v3, and the D3 nominations of Grace (3 Yes) and Carol (2 Yes) failed to the bloc's No votes.
- [t287] (Village) Ask every claimed Medic for each night's protection target the next morning, and line it up against the kill list. A real Medic usually covers the claimed info roles the Katana is likeliest to hit. A Medic whose protections keep landing on unattacked players while claimed investigators die unprotected is a strong Yakuza lead. A matching token count proves nothing, since a Ritualist also starts with 2 tokens. When a group result narrows a Yakuza to two names, look next at who shields the likelier one (urging 'don't pile on', voting with them): that player is the second-Yakuza lead. Turn it into a nomination, not just a remark. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator, with Alice, Carol, Dave, Frank and Grace (0.16 each), all named it the strongest tell. Bob (Ritualist) claimed Medic, 'protected' never-attacked Dave on N2 and nobody on N3 while Census Taker Alice and Coroner Grace died. He said 'let's not pile on Erin' and voted with her. It was noticed but never voted on.
- [t292] (Village) Treat a role-claim switch made right after a map, count or census puts the player in a suspect group (e.g. 'I was Vanilla, actually I'm Medic') as a near-confession. It is exactly the cornered switch Yakuza are told to avoid. Spend that night's Oracle guess or profile on the new claim. A WRONG plus the switch is enough to hang. 'I hid as Vanilla on Day 2 to stay safe, as the tips say' doesn't excuse it. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.32 | recent evidence: Bob switched from Vanilla to Medic after Dave's N2 map placed him. Grace guessed 'Bob = Medic' on N3 and got WRONG, and Bob was hanged 7-1. Reported by Carol, Niaj, Ivan, Grace, Alice, Judy.; Erin, Grace (0.16 each): Alice switched from 'no action' to Tracker after the scent group placed her. The table spent that night's audit on the new claim, and the 1-token count plus the switch was enough to hang.
- [t293] (Village) Use a public archive list as a tiebreak once the evidence has narrowed to two rival claims. A real holder's role appears only if that player was sampled, and a fake appears only by chance among all roles. So a claimed role on the list is several times likelier to be real than one missing from it (roughly 5-10x in a 13-player game). That is enough to break a 50/50 the visit math already produced when a night's delay costs a kill. It is not a standalone case. A listed role is also a ready-made fake claim for the Yakuza, so a new claim of a listed role still needs a test. | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Lookout (Erin) was listed and Sheriff (Heidi) wasn't. Judy and Ivan used the list to break the Erin/Heidi tie, and Heidi was the holder.; Alice, Erin, Mallory: a listed role borrowed after the list was public (Bob's Enforcer) was treated as likely fake, and it was. Mallory (Yakuza) adds that attacking a list the later claims had confirmed only made her look worse.
- [t295] (Village) An uncontested Sheriff's posted results are the Village's best information, so don't discount them because no second source exists yet. Asking for a second check is fine when two investigators exist, but demanding one that can't come only stalls. An Evil read on an unverifiable claim is a mechanical case on its own (t38). When a credible Sheriff is alive, steer their checks to the unchecked players and spend hangs on unchecked suspects before anyone their reads have touched. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Dave (0.16) filed 'hurt' on a 'demand a second check' line: Grace's genuine reads were discounted, the town hanged Not Evil Erin, and the Sheriff died N4 with her clears unused. Alice (0.16) argued for second checks, Frank found them useful early. Thin, mixed evidence.
- [t296] (Village) A result from one unverified claimant that was held back and then names the very player who just accused them is retrofitted until tested, so don't hang on it. 'He posted his count before any visitor spoke' is not evidence either: a Jester or Yakuza can post a fake first and call every contradicting visitor a Roleplayer. When two independent claims confirm each other (e.g. an audit of 0 tokens matching a Lookout claim) against one lone counter, the story that needs two Roleplayers is the weaker one. If the accused offers a cheap overnight test of the counter's claim (a scent, an audit, a secret gift or visit the counter must report), vote Innocent and run it. A claimant who waves off a test of their own result is the likelier liar, possibly a Jester: test or ignore them rather than hang their target. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Moderator plus Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia, Alice, Bob, Judy: Jester Bob's fake one-visit count and held-back 'map' named Lookout Heidi right after she named him. Heidi and Accountant Grace contradicted him and offered Dave's scent and Mallory's gift as tests. The Village hanged Heidi 9-3 on 'he claimed first', and that mis-hang decided the game.
- [t297] (Village) An Oracle claim whose log holds only 'wrong' guesses is uncheckable and is the natural Ritualist cover: both guess roles astrally, with no visits. Treat it as unverified, more so once a Ritualist kill has appeared, when its guesses land on night victims, when its owner dodges a Jester revenge pool, or when its first decisive 'WRONG' appears just as it is needed against a rival of another claimant. Test it early, while the Village still has the votes: an audit (a Ritualist starts with 2 tokens, an Oracle has 0), a scent, or a hang ahead of roles that can't be faked. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Carol, Erin, Mallory, Grace, moderator: Judy (Ritualist) claimed Oracle with three 'wrong' guesses, was never nominated before D4, and used a fake WRONG to help hang real Scrutineer Ivan.
- [t298] (Village) A loud duel between two info claimants can have no Yakuza on either side, since the liar may be a Jester or a Roleplayer. The real Yakuza then coast beside it in claims nobody can check: a Vanilla, an all-'wrong' Oracle, a duplicated role, each voting with every majority and stating their votes truthfully. While the duel runs, point audits, scents and questions at those coasters. After one mis-hang, turn the next trial to them, not to the duel's other side, and check who quietly rode the majority. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Moderator, Dave, Erin, Grace, Mallory: Alice (Vanilla), Judy (all-wrong Oracle) and Frank (second Scrutineer) were never nominated before D4 while the Village spent both real hangs on the Bob/Heidi fight. All three Yakuza voted Yes and Guilty with the crowd; the three Innocent voters on Heidi were all Village.
- [t299] (Village) Vote-reading claims (Scrutineer, Juror, Auditor) are trivial to fake: a fake result only has to match the stated votes and totals, and disciplined Yakuza state their real votes. But only Unique roles (Smuggler, Vulture) are limited to one copy, so two claimants of one of these roles can both be real. A duplicate is a reason to test both, never proof that one lies, and 'only one can be real' is not a case. Two real holders who check the same obvious player (two Auditors auditing the nominator) get the same result, so a late claim whose result matches one already posted fits a second real holder as well as a copier; without a mechanical break it is no case for a hang or a shot. If a second player claims your role, test them that Day, and don't assume the later claimant is the copier. Ask each claimant for something only the real role sees, such as the exact wording and name order of the private result line, and get the rival's answer before the real holder posts theirs. On Day 2 these roles have no result yet (no trial has happened), so don't spend the Day demanding one or put a claimant on trial for lacking it; move to other leads. Later, compare the two claimants' results on a shared pair and against the record, applying the role's exclusions first (t305). Real holders: check the rival and suspected teammate pairs, not players whose votes nobody doubts, and post your own claim and log the same Day a rival posts theirs. | support: 3 games, total weight 3.00 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.40: Moderator: Frank and Dave were both real Jurors. The table spent all of Day 2 on 'they can't both be Juror', tried Frank for nothing and later hanged Dave on the premise that one had to be lying. 'Treat the second claimant as a top suspect' fed that. | recent evidence: Ivan, Olivia, Carol, moderator: Frank (Smuggler) claimed Scrutineer before real Scrutineer Ivan, who had opened vague. Ivan carried the 'copier' label all game and was hanged D4. He raised the result-wording test only when 3 of 4 living players were Yakuza.; Second game with two real holders of a vote-reading role (Auditors Alice and Bob) where the later claimant was treated as the copier and killed.; Two real Auditors again (Bob, Mallory), the third game with two real holders of a vote-reading role. Bob counterclaimed blind and pushed Mallory's hang. Frank and Alice add that vote-only claims (Juror, Scrutineer) cost a Yakuza nothing to fake: Heidi's Juror cover was never broken.
- [t304] (Village) If you hold a role a Roleplayer can be told (Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle, Accountant), don't volunteer 'unless I'm a Roleplayer' when your result contradicts a claim nobody has confirmed. It hands the accuser a ready way to dismiss you. State your result flatly and show that the rival's specific claim is false under every reading. Flag yourself as a possible Roleplayer only when a confirmed result contradicts you (t99). | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Heidi: her own Roleplayer caveat in her claim and will gave Bob and the voters the 'both visitors are Roleplayers' excuse. - [t308] (Village) A dead investigator's posted results are one claimant's word until something confirms the role. A Coroner exam showing the claimed role does that, and it also rules out a Roleplayer, who shows as 'Roleplayer'. Ask the Coroner for that exam before the town hangs on a dead Seer's, Tracker's or Sheriff's result. A confirmed role confirms that the result was real, not what you inferred from it: re-read it with the role's limits (a Seer pair compares appearances; the holder reads Good, and a Notorious, Turncoat or framed player reads Evil) and add a second pointer, such as a profile, a cause of death or a vote record, before the vote. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.56 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Grace, Carol, Niaj, Heidi (0.16 each): Carol's exam showed Frank was a real Seer, so his Erin/Carol pair plus Ivan's profile hanged the SK 6-3. Niaj, Heidi and Erin note that the exam proves the role, not the reading of the pair.
- [t311] (Village) When a player keeps self-nominating or begging for the rope, refuse the hang (t91) and note who keeps voting Yes or Guilty on them and trading PMs with them. Yakuza and other non-Village players gain from a wasted hang and a revenge on a Guilty voter. It is a lead, not a verdict: ask that backer for a role claim and a public summary of their PMs, and treat a refusal as the case. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: Ivan (Wiretap) voted Yes and Guilty on Jester Dave's self-nominations and had a PM cluster with Dave and Heidi. The Village turned on him, he dodged the PM content and any role claim, and he was hanged as the last Yakuza. Heidi, a Bounty Hunter, backed the push too, so the pattern also catches Neutrals. One game.
- [t312] (Village) Check a Veteran claim against the announced causes of death, which are public even though roles are hidden. A death whose cause is a Veteran's alert proves a Veteran was on alert that night and that the dead player visited them, so a claimant who named that night and victim is corroborated without any second source. Don't spend Days asking for more proof, and don't accept 'the dead player was seen visiting someone else, so they can't have visited the Veteran': a player can make more than one visit a night (t357). Use it: a visitor killed by an alert on a night with no Katana death was probably the Katana holder unless a Village visit explains it (a Medic, a watcher, a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade attack, which the Veteran is told was a Katana attack) or the visitor was a Sleepwalker, whose random visit can land on anyone. Either way, look at what else the dead visitor did that night (whom they were seen visiting, who was blocked) and at their PM partners. Veteran: claim the Day after an alert kill, report exactly what you were told (how many visited, whether your attack succeeded) and label the victim's name as an inference from the announced death. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Carol, Erin (0.16 each): Erin's alert account matched every death (Carol N1 with no Katana kill, Bob N2), yet the table argued for three Days over a dead Fortune Teller's count instead of treating the claim as checked. Alice, Frank, Heidi and Dave called it unverifiable; the death causes were the check. Thin: one game.
- [t314] (Village) Once a Not Evil read is corroborated (e.g. a Tracker saw the Sheriff visit that player), the players who nominate that player or say they vote Guilty against the read are your best leads. Sheriff: check them next and never spend a night re-checking a player you already cleared. Shooters: aim there. Everyone: ask each pusher for a role claim and a mechanical reason. The holder caveat (t31) still applies to the cleared player, but not to the question of who is pushing. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Alice, Dave (0.16 each): Frank nominated Dave on D2 and Frank and Grace stated Guilty against Carol's Not Evil read, which Erin's track corroborated. - [t316] (Village) (If you have the Armed trait) Your hidden gun is one Basic shot at night, and it is a visit. Fire it at your best Yakuza lead (a claim broken by a profile or count, the PM partner of a player shown to be an attacker) while a Yakuza is still alive: a gun you never fire is worth nothing. But the lead must be a break in the target's own claim. Don't fire on a chain of 'if X's claim is true, Y is evil': a visit that only a since-exposed Yakuza claimed is no evidence against the player named, and a Seer DIFFERENT pair whose other half has already been hanged as Yakuza is spent (t251). Signs that the target's claim is real (an archive that named roles before they were claimed) count for them. Basic can't kill a Serial Killer or Arsonist (Standard Defense) or a Jester, Executioner or Magpie (Basic Defense), so once only a Neutral killer is left the gun won't finish them and the hang is the kill. A shot that fails on a suspect no Medic guards is evidence of innate Defense or of the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which any player can have (t370), so post it the next Day as a lead, not as proof of a side. A wrong shot costs more if the victim is Cursed or an Avenger. Armed is no longer Village-only: a Yakuza can hold it too. So saying you fired the gun shows nothing about your side, and a 'hidden gun' death may be a Yakuza's second kill that night. Expect your shot to be judged by its target and by the role you claim, and judge any other self-declared shooter the same way. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Grace, Dave (0.16 each): Erin and Grace each held an Armed shot all game. Neither fired at Bob or Carol while they lived, and against Arsonist Heidi's Standard Defense the shots could no longer kill. One game.
- [t317] (Village) When no result is coming (the Katana has stopped because the Yakuza look dead, no living role can read a Neutral killer, and the R14 stall clock is running), 'I'll only vote on hard results' is a stall, not a standard. Rank the living players by what each has made checkable: an exact role, a target for every night, and results that deaths, an archive or a profile have since confirmed. Ask each for that log by name, once. The player who gives none of it, answers with filler and names targets only once on trial is the best case you will get: nominate them while enough Villagers are alive to convict. The Yakuza pair's vague PM answers and no-action claims were the best case available and went unused.; Frank: no investigator was alive after N1, yet the table waited on results through D2. The PM pair and the claimless Heidi were the best cases available and neither was nominated.; Moderator: by D3 every living player but Erin and Ivan had claimed a role, and the 3 Guilty on Alice's trial (Grace plus two) pointed at both. Alice and Dave (0.16 each) name Ivan's result-free pushes and missing claim as the lead they should have used.; Moderator (0.4), Heidi (0.16): on D2 no new result was coming before the lock, and the table said 'no nomination without new data'. - [t321] (Village) A Kitsune changes how deaths and survivals read. A kill released by a Kitsune is announced with the cause of the attack it swallowed, so a later 'Serial Killer' or 'Katana' death may be a release and not a fresh attack: ask every claimed Kitsune what they hold and whom they released on before counting killers. Test a Kitsune claim against the ward's own notices: a charmed player is still told the role behind each attack they survived, so the claimed level and cause must match what the ward reports. Two attack notices on one ward fit two real Kitsunes. A Kitsune holding an attack can't protect, so ask where the held attack is going before the night. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.16 | recent evidence: Grace, Erin, Bob (0.16 each) and the moderator's review: Carol's N2 notice of a Katana and an SK attack matched both Kitsune stories, yet the table treated one claim as fake. Erin's N3 'Serial Killer' death was Heidi's release, not Dave's attack. One game.
- [t322] (Village) Count the Days you have left on Day 2. Two different kill causes on one night (a Katana death plus a Serial Killer death) mean the town loses up to two players every night. At 6 alive that leaves at most two hangs, so a Day with no nomination loses the game and 'wait for tonight's results' is not an option. Ask each claimant once for night, target and exact result, set a round by which someone nominates, and put the best case available on trial: one side of a duplicate investigator claim, a late Vanilla claimant, an unexplained survivor. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.28 | recent evidence: Moderator: N1 showed a Katana death and an SK death. D2 ended with no nomination, N2 took the Sheriff, and by D3 the count was 2 Yakuza, the SK and 2 Villagers.; Frank and the moderator's review: two killers were shown on N1 (Katana on Erin, an SK attack swallowed on Alice) at 8 players. D2 passed with no hang, N2 cost Bob and Grace, and by D3 the Village was 2 of 5.
- [t326] (Village) In a duplicate investigator claim, see which claimant the Katana goes after. A claimant who reports a Katana attack the morning after, with a Medic stating a protect on them that night, is very likely the real one: Yakuza don't spend the Katana on their own fake. The one other source of that notice is a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade attack, which looks exactly like the Katana, so ask once whether any Villager made it before leaning on the attack. The rival who then nominates the survivor with no new result is your best hang that Day. Check the Neutral case too: an innate-Defense survivor has no protector to name (t43). | support: 1 games, total weight 0.16 | recent evidence: Heidi, Erin, Frank (0.16 each): Heidi posted her N2 Katana survival at dawn, and Erin's protect on her matched. Fake Sheriff Bob then nominated Heidi with no result. He was Yakuza and was hanged, though on the PM and the push more than on this reading. One low-weight game.
- [t327] (Village) Treat repeated public demands to name tonight's target, your stored attack or your trap's location as a warning sign, not a duty. The players who gain most from the answer are the killers, and they will ask in the Village's voice ('just so we can check it'). Refuse once in one calm line ('it goes up after the night resolves') and note who keeps pressing: that is a lead to question, not proof. Claim a power role only when the claim settles a vote that Day. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: Carol and Dave (Yakuza) and Heidi (SK) all pressed Kitsune Grace on D2 to name her release target. The town quoted it back as part of the D5 case that hanged her.
- [t328] (Village) Once the Village is a minority beside two hostile sides (e.g. 2 Villagers, 2 Yakuza and a Serial Killer at 5 alive), no hang passes on Village votes alone, and the two killing sides will otherwise agree on a Villager. Each wants the other gone, so pitch a hang to whichever side gives you the third vote, and name both leads aloud (the PM pair, the late-Vanilla killer suspect). Count what each hang leaves before choosing. Hanging a Yakuza with the Neutral killer's vote leaves no side holding half. Hanging the Neutral killer with Yakuza votes leaves 2 Yakuza of 4, which is lost unless an armed trap or a kill token can still hit the holder that night. Never spend that Day defending one Villager against another. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: on D3 Alice and Frank faced Carol, Dave and Heidi. Nobody pushed Heidi or the pair, and the three hostile votes hanged Alice 3-2. The moderator's version is 'push the late Vanilla; the Yakuza may help'. The what-it-leaves count is added by arithmetic, not from play. One game, thin.
- [t330] (Village) A death whose cause is an Enforcer's attack proves the dead player made an attack of their own that night. If a Katana death happened the same night, they very likely made it, usually as the holder, and a Not Evil Sheriff read on them that night is then expected, not a clear. The one Village reading is a Villager who used the once-per-game Stolen Blade trait, whose kill also reads 'Katana': it fits only if the role they claimed checks out and the Yakuza's own Katana is accounted for some other way (a second Katana victim or a reported failed Katana attack that night, or Katana deaths that continue afterwards). Otherwise treat their role claim as broken, then look at whose results propped that claim up and who defended it. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Mallory and the moderator (1.0 each): Carol died to Bob's Enforcer visit on the night Olivia died to the Katana and Frank read Carol Not Evil. The town read her as the N3 holder and her Party Animal claim as fake, which the Oracle WRONG confirmed. One game.
- [t331] (Village) Holding back your exact role doesn't hide you if you post a result in your role's own words. 'My read is consistent with her Vanilla claim' names a Sheriff, and 'I visited X and X made no attack' names an Enforcer: the Yakuza read the wording and block or kill you that night. Filler leaks too: 'nothing actionable to post yet' when everyone else says 'nothing mechanical', or 'I have a private result that agrees', marks you as an investigator holding something and hands the accused an easy 'nobody has seen it'. While unclaimed, use the same plain words as the rest of the table and either post no result at all or make the full claim when the result bears on a vote. The half-post is the worst of both. | support: 5 games, total weight 4.40 | recent evidence: Frank and Bob (1.0 each), confirmed by the moderator: Frank's 'consistent with her Vanilla claim' drew the N2 Katana (saved only by a Kitsune charm), and Bob's 'made no attack' wording got him blocked on N2. Ivan, who gave nothing until he had a cross-check on D3, was untouched until N5.; Erin and Grace were each targeted the night after wording that hinted at a held result.; Mallory: one Day 1 sentence in her role's own terms ('token counts') marked her as an investigator and got her killed and cleaned on N1.; Moderator (0.4): Carol's 'concrete information tomorrow' on Day 1 was wording no non-investigator would use, and it picked her as the N1 block and kill.; Dave (Trapper): explaining his D2 visit as 'a protective action not active until N2' was his role's own wording. The Yakuza read Trapper and had Grace self-divine for Strong Defense before attacking his ward.
- [t332] (Village) Protectors and other roles with no results to post: don't press everyone else to claim while holding back even your own role type. Once the table starts eliminating candidates for a hidden killer, the last unexplained player is the default hang. Give your category by Day 2-3 ('protective, no results') and the exact role with your night log as soon as the elimination reaches you. On trial, never name as a suspect a player your own log shows you trusted, such as one you just spent a token protecting: the table reads it as a contradiction. Name the other remaining candidate with the mechanical case against them, and offer a cheap check of your own claim (an Oracle guess tonight). | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Erin, Mallory, Niaj, moderator (1.0 each): Medic Erin held even her category through D3 while pushing claims, became one of the last two SK candidates, named Frank (whom she had just protected with a token) as a possible SK in her defense, and was hanged 5-2 on D4. Conflicts in part with t32/t94's 'stay unclaimed'. One game.
- [t333] (Village) Narrow a Serial Killer by who could not have made each kill. A player confirmed role-blocked on a night a Serial Killer death still happened can't be that killer. Build this list from Day 3, strike the confirmed players and name the candidates left; then aim an Oracle guess of 'Serial Killer' or a blocker at the likeliest one. Count a block confirmation only when blocker and blocked player are independent: a Yakuza pair can confirm each other's fake block and 'visited no one' track. A Katana survival does not strike anyone, since a Serial Killer's Standard Defense stops the Katana. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Erin, Niaj, Frank, Bob, Heidi, Grace, moderator (1.0 each): block confirmations from Judy and Carol's targets cut the SK pool to Erin, Grace and Frank, and Ivan's N4 guess on Grace was RIGHT.
