- [t336] (Village) After a trial ends Innocent, don't make the nominator the next suspect just for 'rushing'. Yakuza turn the table on whoever pressed them, and the first players to demand the nominator explain himself are often the acquitted nominee and their partner. Judge the nominee's answers, and ask whether anything checkable about the nominator has changed. The player who pushed a real Yakuza is that night's likeliest Katana victim, so a pusher who dies the night after is a point for their case: reopen it the next Day. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: after Grace's 2-5 acquittal on D3, Erin, Frank, Heidi and Alice all copied Carol's and Grace's line that Dave must explain his 'rush'. Dave was the real Enforcer, the Katana killed him that night, and the case on Grace waited until D5. One game.
- [t340] (Village) A Bounty Hunter wins by getting bounty targets hanged with their own Guilty vote. When a claimed or likely Bounty Hunter keeps nominating one player with no checkable reason, treat it as a bounty push: vote No, say why, and judge that player only on their own results. Repeated bare nominations of the same player are also a sign that an unclaimed player is one. Don't spend the Day's hang or a kill token (a Sniper or Vigilante shot) on the Bounty Hunter while the Katana still kills: they never block your win (t245), and once their one attack token is spent they can't attack again. Note that their one Strong attack can hit a Yakuza as easily as a Villager, so a 'Bounty Hunter' cause of death says nothing about the victim's side and adds no killer you must hang. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Dave, Grace (0.16 each): Bob nominated Dave three times on D3-D4 with no evidence. The Village refused each time (3, 2, 2 Yes) and kept its hang for the audited Alice. Bob's N2 attack had killed Smuggler Carol. One game.
- [t343] (Village) A death whose cause is a Trapper's trap proves the dead player made a visiting attack that night on the trapped player. If the trapped player reports a failed Katana attack that same night, the dead player made it. With no other Katana death or Katana notice that night it was almost surely the Yakuza's own attack, and the dead player held the Katana. If the Katana also struck elsewhere that night, the dead player used a second blade: a Yakuza's Spare Blade, or a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade, which looks exactly like the Katana, so check the role they claimed before reading them as Yakuza. Either way a 'no action' claim and will are false about that night (a 'Vanilla, no action' player can't die to a trap). For a holder, treat the whole claim and will as a proven lie, then re-read older results with the holder rule: on any night they held the Katana they read Good, so a Seer pair that read them DIFFERENT from X points at X as not Good, and Bloodhound counts for trios containing them can be re-solved with that attack known. Say aloud that you assume the Katana didn't move between those nights, and bring a second pointer on X to the vote. Trapper: claim in one line once the trap death makes the claim check itself. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Moderator, Ivan, Grace, Erin, Mallory (1.0 each): Mallory's trap on Erin killed Judy on N2 while Erin reported a failed Katana. Judy's 'Vanilla' will was exposed, Erin's N1 pair (Judy/Niaj DIFFERENT) plus Grace's counts then named Niaj, a Clerk, hanged 10-1. One game.
- [t344] (Village) (If you have the Notorious trait) Alignment checks read you Evil. Write the trait in your will from Night 1. The moment a Sheriff read or Seer pair comes out against you, claim your role, the trait and your exact token count in one message, and say the result is true: an invented result would not happen to land on the one Notorious player, so your claim backs the real investigator and exposes whoever contradicts them. Don't call the checker a liar. Everyone else: a Notorious claim made right after the read is not a lie on timing alone, but no alignment check can test it and a Yakuza can make it too, so test the role claimed beside it (tokens, a shot, results) and ask whether a fake checker would have picked that player. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Heidi, Frank, Bob, moderator (1.0 each): Heidi (Vigilante, Notorious) claimed role, trait and tokens as soon as Erin's Heidi/Grace pair read DIFFERENT. It confirmed Erin as the real Seer against Frank's fake 'Seer WRONG', and Heidi's shot then killed the last Yakuza. One game.
- [t345] (Village) A player confirming a result about themself ('yes, his guess of my role was right') verifies nothing when the result clears them: a Yakuza gladly confirms a wrong guess that names them a harmless Village role. Count an exact-role or alignment result as verified only when someone other than its target can check it. An 'Oracle' who reports a RIGHT on Vanilla Villager or Grunt is reporting something that can't be true unless a hand-picked list names the role (t307), which fits a Roleplayer's random draws or a liar. When an investigator's results are discredited (a likely Roleplayer, a broken claim), throw out their clears and look at the players those clears protected. One who leaned on the vouch, defended the investigator and cast the lone No on their nomination is a lead for an audit or a shot. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Frank, Erin, Heidi, Alice, Mallory (1.0 each): Roleplayer Frank's fake 'Dave = Vanilla CORRECT' was confirmed only by Dave, the Smuggler, who then cast the only No besides Frank's own on Frank's nomination. Bob's audit showed the No and Heidi shot Dave on N4. One game.
- [t349] (Village) Test every passive result claim (Scout groups, Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer sizes) against all the visits already public on the Day it is posted, not only when the claimant is cornered. Free known edges: audits, checks and protections named in claims and wills, Katana and released-attack deaths, trap and alert deaths. A Scout who names two players a known visit links (e.g. one audited the other) as being in different groups is lying; no Roleplayer is told Scout or Cartographer. A Fortune Teller whose visitor count is below the number of confirmed visitors is not reporting real counts: a liar, or a Roleplayer, since Fortune Teller is a role a Roleplayer can be told and its counts are random. Throw those counts out either way. A proven lie is a mechanical break worth hanging on; for a broken Fortune Teller, weigh the Roleplayer reading (t98) and look for a second break (a changed story, hangs pushed on the count) before the vote. Re-run the check each morning when new wills come out, and post the arithmetic in one message. A break found after the Yakuza hold half the votes is worthless, so do it while the Village still has its majority. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Olivia's N1 FT claim of 4 visitors was below the 5 known visitors (Carol, Erin, Grace, Dave, Niaj). Grace's N5 Scout put Mallory and Heidi in different groups though Mallory's will said she audited Heidi. Frank caught it on D6, at the lock. Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Ivan, Judy, Mallory and the moderator all filed it.
- [t351] (Village) Repeated role-block notices don't prove a living blocker. The hidden Drowsy trait (R24) blocks its holder on about half the nights with no blocker at all, and the holder is only told 'you were role-blocked'. So a Medic or investigator who reports blocks on two or three nights, with stated targets that match the players the Katana went after, fits an honest Drowsy player as well as a Pharmacist's victim or a liar. A block also explains why a Standard protection 'failed' against the Standard Katana. Don't hang or shoot on the block story, and don't take it as proof of a Pharmacist unless a second player reports a block too. Test the claim another way: a Lookout or Tracker on the claimant, a token audit, their vote record. If it is you: after the second block say you may be Drowsy, post your full log once, and never promise a protection or a result for tonight. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator's review, Heidi, Grace (0.16 each): Medic Heidi guarded Dave on N2 and Bob on N3, the right wards, and her hidden Drowsy trait blocked her both times. Her block story read as unverifiable, Carol turned Alice against her, and Alice's N4 'test' shot killed her while Drowsy blocked her self-protection. One low-strength game.
- [t354] (Village) A Turncoat is a Village player who wins only if the Yakuza win. They read Evil to a Sheriff or Seer but count as Good in a census, they aren't in the Yakuza chat and don't know who the Yakuza are, and the game's end ignores them. At most one exists, and no investigation sees traits. So an Evil read plus a Good census count on the same player fits a Turncoat or a Notorious Villager, and a census clear shows only that a player isn't Yakuza or Neutral. Hanging a Turncoat doesn't lower the Yakuza count, so near the vote lock (t286) spend the hang on a Yakuza lead. If you are the Turncoat: you can't find your side by any notice, so vote with the town, read the kill list and the vote totals to work out who the Yakuza are, and help them only where it won't mark you, since a Sheriff read already makes you a suspect. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 1 games, total weight 0.00 | recent evidence: From a revision of the rules (new Turncoat trait), not from a game.
- [t356] (Village) (If you have the Remorseful trait) If you vote Guilty and the nominee is hanged and was truly Village, you die at once of 'remorse', so each wrong Guilty costs the Village two players. Vote Guilty only on a corroborated mechanical case (t38), and on a thin or 50/50 hang vote Innocent and let others carry it; your vote is counted as cast, so a stated Guilty that you cast Innocent will show in the totals, and you should say why. It goes by true alignment, so a hanged Turncoat or Notorious Villager kills you too, while a hanged Jester or other Neutral doesn't. Everyone else: a death by 'remorse' right after a hang proves the hanged player was Village and the dead voter was Village and voted Guilty. Use it at once: the case that hanged them was wrong, so look again at who built and pushed it. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 1 games, total weight 0.00 | recent evidence: From a revision of the rules (new Remorseful trait), not from a game.
- [t358] (Village) Combine a role-block notice with a Lookout's list for the blocked player. If X reports being blocked and the watch on X shows only visitors with a claimed, harmless action plus one more, that one is the blocker. Ask once whether a Village blocker (Party Animal) claims the block; if nobody does, the visitor is very likely the Pharmacist, and their daytime PM partner is the next suspect. Say the chain aloud in one message the Day the two results meet. Caveat: a blocked player with no unexplained visitor may be Drowsy (t351). | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): on N2 Erin was blocked and Dave's watch on her showed only Carol (claimed Tracker) and Grace. Grace was the Pharmacist, and her PM partner Bob was the last Yakuza. Nobody stated the chain; Bob was hanged on evasion alone. One game.
- [t360] (Village) A Chronicler's mark makes a hang check itself: the marked player's true role is announced on death, and a Mortician can't clean a Day hang. Ask by the end of Day 2 whether anyone holds marks or another way to reveal a role. When leads are close, hang the marked suspect before an unmarked one: the reveal settles every claim tied to them (their own cover, an 'Oracle' call on them, a counterclaim) and shows which defenders argued for a Yakuza. Don't accept 'test it tonight' in place of a marked hang. After the reveal, go to whoever argued No or delay before the nomination (t34). | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Alice, Frank, Heidi, Ivan, Judy, Niaj, moderator (1.0 each): the marked hangs of Carol (Mortician) and Dave (Smuggler) were mechanical proof, and Dave's pre-nomination No on Carol made him the next lead. One game.; Carol, Erin, Dave, moderator: the marked hang of Bob checked itself (Talent Agent revealed), confirmed Olivia's census and Ivan's will, and let the town re-solve the N1 counts.
- [t367] (Village) A will shown as 'cleaned by mortician' proves a living Mortician and tells you the Yakuza chose that victim in advance, expecting an investigator's will. Re-read what the cleaned victim said by Day (hints about tokens, checks, results) as a sign of what the Yakuza feared, and treat anyone that talk pointed at as a lead to test. It is not a result: the victim may have been a Roleplayer with nothing real to hide. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.50 | recent evidence: Erin (1.0): Carol cleaned N1 Katana victim Mallory after Mallory's Day 1 'token counts' hint. Mallory was a Roleplayer-Accountant who had 'checked' the Mortician. Thin, one game.
- [t368] (Village) (If you have the Kindred or Faultline trait) After every trial vote you are privately shown how one or two other players' votes counted. Write each reveal in your will (Day, nominee, player, Guilty or Innocent) and compare it with that player's stated vote: a mismatch is a miscast, a lie or a fix (t249), and several quiet Guilty votes behind a loud claimant show the table agrees with them without a counterclaim. Post a reveal when it contradicts a stated vote that matters. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.30 | recent evidence: Alice (1.0), in passing: she says Kindred and Faultline results showing who votes with whom are worth recording in the will. No concrete use was reported. Thin.
- [t371] (Village) Messages posted while a player is puppeted are the Puppeteer's, not that player's. So when a second player 'independently' confirms a fresh result the same Day with a role they never hinted at, and pushes a hang on it at once, don't count it as corroboration until they repeat it on a later Day (a puppet lasts one Day). When a player reports having been puppeted: throw out everything posted under their name that Day, don't hang them for it or call their real claim a switch, and treat it as proof of a living Puppeteer (who has one token, so no second puppet is coming). Hunt that Day, while you can still pass a hang: who gained from the puppet's push, who backed it, and any profile, survey or archive list that shows 'Puppeteer'. A profile listing Puppeteer whose other entries are held by other claimants or go unclaimed by the target names that target (t240). Caveat: a Yakuza can also say 'I was puppeted' to drop a broken claim, so check whether that Day's messages helped or hurt the player. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Carol, Dave, Bob, Frank, Alice (0.16 each): puppeted Alice's fake Lookout list seemed to confirm Frank's track of Dave and drove Dave's D2 trial. After Alice reported it on D3, Heidi's N1 profile of Bob already listed Puppeteer and her N3 profile of Erin (Bloodhound/Puppeteer/Trapper, with Dave the claimed Trapper) all but named Erin, but nobody pinned it on her while the Village had the votes. One low-strength game.
- [t373] (Village) A claimed Tracker or Lookout whose every target is a player who never visits (a Scout, a Fortune Teller, an Archivist, other passive claimants) reports only 'visited nobody', which nothing can ever check. It is the safest fake result and a natural cover for a player who really visits every night: an Arsonist dousing, or a Yakuza. Flag it by Day 2-3, not at 4 alive: ask the claimant to target next a player whose visits are known, compare their claimed visits with the Fortune Teller's counts night by night, and aim a watch, an exact-role check or a mark at them. Two players claiming the same tokenless role with only such results are two untested claims, not a pair that backs each other up. Before the endgame, list every claim nobody else can confirm. A census won't find them for you: it never counts Neutrals, so 'Evil 0' leaves a Neutral killer in place. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj, Olivia, Grace and the moderator (1.0 each): Frank (Arsonist) 'tracked' Scout Erin, FT Niaj and Archivist Heidi, all 'visited nobody'; Grace's fake Tracker results were the same kind. Nobody tested either until Day 4, when the pattern hanged Frank at 4 alive. One game.
- [t374] (Village) A trap that goes off on a night with no death by the trap means one of two things: the stopped attack was Astral (no hitback), or the attacker visited and survived the Strong hitback through Strong Defense (a Yakuza Diviner picking the holder or itself, a Medic's Strong token, the hidden Narrow Escape trait). Trapper: post it the next morning, and ask the ward for their attack notice. If the notice says Katana, the holder visited the ward that night and is still alive: line up that night's visit counts and look at anyone who claimed a visit to the ward or reported a blank or 'held' night. Don't let 'nobody died, so the trap story is fake' discredit the Trapper. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Dave (Trapper), Niaj, Olivia, moderator (1.0 each): on N3 Dave's trap on Olivia stopped Grace's Katana; Grace had divined herself for Strong Defense and survived the hitback. Dave's report plus Grace's 'held' N2 backed Alice's shot on her. On D4 Frank used the missing hitback death to cast doubt on Dave. One game.
- [t375] (Village) When a hang or reveal shows a player was Yakuza, go back through everything they claimed that same Day. Strike their claimed visits and results and re-solve each night's visit counts with their real role: the gap that opens often names a teammate. Any claim that only their story seemed to fit loses that support and needs a new test. A player the exposed Yakuza claimed to have visited, or pointed suspicion at, is no more suspect for it: a Yakuza's lie about someone is not evidence against that person. Then check who shared a small vote minority with them (t34). | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Carol, Judy, Niaj and the moderator (1.0 each): once Bob was revealed as Talent Agent, his fake 'Enforcer visit' to Heidi was struck, the N1 count was re-solved and Grace's track lost its support. Niaj had already shot Heidi on N3 partly because of Bob's fake visit to her; she was a Good Archivist. One game.
- [t4] (Yakuza) Assume the real holder of any role you fake-claim is alive and will counterclaim, that an Accountant can audit your exact token count, that an exact-role check (Oracle, Ritualist, Diviner, Archivist, Surveyor or Profiler list) can rule your claimed role out, and that a public role list plus self-proving effects (prosecutions, cleans, ignites) and confirmed claims can leave no slot for it. Never claim a Unique role whose holder may be alive. Never copy a role whose actions were already public (e.g. a Prosecutor): the real holder will counterclaim with earlier reads and correct night labels. Never claim the role that a dead victim you visited had claimed in public or in their will. Pick a fake role whose tokens match yours: claim a tokenless role only if you hold no tokens. Never volunteer your token history or report more results than the role's tokens allow. Visit results include Lookout and Tracker logs, Insomniac role lists, Scout and Cartographer groups and FT counts. Before any claim that includes a visit, check it against every visit result already posted: a claimed track or watch that an existing count rules out (e.g. a 'track' on a night an Insomniac saw exactly one visitor, a confirmed Lookout) is exposed within a round. A no-action claim is unsafe if you visited a target someone may have watched, so prepare a story that fits a token check or visit report. If you fake a visiting role (Tracker, Lookout), name your real visit destinations as your targets so these results can only confirm you, and fix the claim in night chat before you need it. Never improvise targets. If your claim is broken, don't switch to another claim: a withdrawn claim plus a fallback (above all a crowded Vanilla) is a mechanical case on its own. | support: 30 games, total weight 16.04 | against: 10 reports, total weight 3.60: Bob: his fake Lookout claim was contradicted by Alice's group result. Misapplication: the tip already warns against claims a visit result can contradict.; Bob filed 'hurt', saying the team's plans relied on checkable claimant counts. The report is unclear and doesn't show the tip backfired.; Carol filed 'hurt': her Diviner claim was contradicted by Heidi's track. Misapplication: she claimed her real Yakuza role, which the tip set (t176) forbids, and the tip already warns about Tracker-contradictable claims.; Carol and Erin filed 'hurt'. Misapplication: the team broke the tip with tokenless Vanilla claims against an Accountant, then fell back on Yakuza-only roles (t176).; Moderator filed 'hurt': Ivan's Tracker claim was safe from the Lookout but broken by a Scout group result the tip didn't mention. The edit now covers groups and real-destination covers.; Erin and Heidi filed 'hurt' over Erin's three role lines. Misapplication: Erin broke the tip's no-switch clause.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Erin, Frank, Grace filed 'hurt' on the sheet line about Frank's Tracker claim. Misapplication: his claimed visit didn't match his real visits or the posted Insomniac count, against the tip.; Dave, Grace, Mallory, Niaj (0.16 each) filed 'hurt' over Grace's broken Medic claim. Misapplication: she kept spending tokens after claiming, and then switched stories, against the tip. The moderator says the matched start count helped. See t271.; Dave filed 'hurt': Ivan claimed Vanilla while holding Ritualist tokens. A misapplication: the tip says to claim tokenless roles only with no tokens, and the loss came from the guess, not the claim.; Bob ('hurt') and Erin ('wrong'): a token-matched fake Medic still gets checked by its protections against the kill list, which the team chooses. See t288. | recent evidence: Moderator: Grace's Medic claim matched her real token count when she made it, so it survived until the audit. Her later switches nearly hanged her, which confirms the no-switch clause.; Carol, Dave, Frank (0.16 each): Bob's Medic claim matched his 2 Ritualist tokens and Erin's Vanilla matched her real role output, so neither was mechanically disproved.; Twelve reports (Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Olivia, moderator): Bob broke the no-switch clause (Vanilla to Medic) and an Oracle WRONG hanged him. Heidi's no-visit plan forced a late Sheriff claim.; Moderator, Carol: neither Yakuza had a Village cover matching their tokens and visits. Carol switched from 'no night action' to Smuggler, and the switch was the named contradiction at her trial.; Frank filed 'helped': his tokenless, no-visit Vanilla claim matched a Grunt's real output and was never contradicted.; Moderator (0.4), Bob (0.16): cornered by Erin's profile, Bob claimed Cartographer and copied Alice's posted sizes exactly. Carol's tokenless, no-visit Vanilla claim matched a Grunt's real output and held all game.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Carol, Frank, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each) filed 'helped' or the same lesson: Alice (Ballot Stuffer, 1 token) went from 'no action' to Tracker to Philanthropist. Heidi's audit showed 1 token against the tokenless Tracker claim and she was hanged 4-2 on D4.; Grace's fake Scout result, made up without checking the newest will, contradicted Mallory's posted audit of Heidi. Heidi's tokenless, Astral Juror cover held to the end, even through an audit.; Bob, Dave (0.16 each): Bob's fake Tracker claim met the real Tracker's counterclaim at once, and a Tracker reads exactly the holder's and blocker's visits. - [t6] (Yakuza) Every Yakuza must submit their agreed night action and Katana vote every night. Confirm the exact holder and target in night chat. With two Yakuza, one missing vote cancels the kill. Submit exactly what you told your team. If you change your plan (e.g. decide to spend a one-shot token after all), say so in night chat before the night ends, because teammates plan their votes and Day stance around it. Make plans that still work if one teammate does nothing. | support: 41 games, total weight 16.69 | recent evidence: Carol filed 'helped': holder and target were confirmed in night chat every night, and each agreed Katana attack was submitted (Frank N1, Heidi N2 stopped by the Medic, Heidi N3).; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Dave, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target matched in night chat every night and all three Katana kills (Frank, Heidi, Bob) landed.; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Dave (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were confirmed every night, and every Katana on a target without Defense landed (Erin N1, Grace N2, Frank N3).; Carol, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were confirmed in night chat every night and all four Katana kills on N2-N5 landed.; Alice, Carol (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were confirmed each night, and the N2 (Erin) and N3 (Grace) Katana kills both landed.; Grace, Ivan (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were agreed in night chat every night and no Katana was lost to a missing vote.; Niaj, Olivia, moderator: holder and target were agreed in night chat each night and no Katana vote was missed.; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Frank (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were confirmed in night chat every night, and all four Katana attacks (Grace, Dave, Bob, Alice) killed.; Bob, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder and target were agreed in night chat and both Katana votes went in; the N2 attack on Carol killed.; Erin, Carol (0.16 each) filed 'helped': holder (Carol) and target were confirmed in night chat every night and all three voted; the Katana killed Bob, Frank, Dave and Heidi with no miss.
- [t15] (Yakuza) Right after a teammate is hanged or exposed, don't lead a push on a quiet Villager who argued against that teammate. A Vigilante reads it as revenge for the dead player. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.78 | recent evidence: Bob drove Heidi's hang the day after Frank flipped. Heidi was Vanilla, and Carol shot Bob that night citing the push.
- [t16] (Yakuza) Don't defend a doomed teammate. Ballots are secret, but the totals are announced, and the town compares them with what everyone said. A hidden defensive vote still shows up as an extra No or Innocent, and your public defense is remembered if the teammate is later exposed. Do the vote math in night chat first. If the team's No and Innocent votes (plus a Ballot Stuffer's doubled vote) can stop the execution, defend them: an exposed teammate saved beats a clean record. Otherwise vote Yes and Guilty with the town and say so. Follow both halves: say the vote you actually cast. Defending and saying Innocent while secretly voting Guilty leaves a hidden Guilty in the total, and the town pins it on whichever stated-Innocent teammate is still alive. Plan the whole team's votes and stated positions across trials in advance. A doomed member's last words shouldn't defend or attack anyone in a way that maps back to teammates. The bus rule is for teammates only. If the nominee is a non-teammate who is likely a Jester, don't all vote Guilty: every Guilty vote is a ticket in the revenge draw, so some of you should vote Innocent. | support: 56 games, total weight 31.79 | against: 9 reports, total weight 5.16: Moderator: Grace was exposed but not doomed, because the doubled vote allowed a 4-4 tie. Bob and Mallory chose not to bus her and saved her; bussing would have cost the win.; Niaj: the team applied 'join the bandwagon' reflexively to a non-teammate likely Jester; all four voted Guilty and the revenge killed Peggy.; Ivan: the bus votes were clean, but Niaj's dying defense of Ivan and Judy's public defense of Erin left trails. A doomed member's last words should not defend or attack anyone in a way that maps back to teammates.; Ivan filed 'hurt': the D2 bloc and later votes left the team traceable. The tip's bus logic wasn't the cause; the D2 Guilty bloc on a non-teammate was (see t160).; Grace filed 'wrong', saying Yakuza self-nominations and Guilty pushes cost them. This misreads the tip, which is about bussing doomed teammates.; Erin and Frank filed 'hurt'. Misapplication: they defended doomed teammates against the tip.; Moderator (0.4) and Carol (0.16) filed 'hurt'. Misapplication: Bob followed only the ballot half and Carol defended against the tip. The edit now says to state the vote you cast.; Heidi filed 'wrong': Judy and Grace voted Innocent on each other and lobbied privately, which made the pair visible. Misapplication: they defended against the tip.; Grace filed 'hurt' over her stated Innocent on Erin. Misapplication: she defended publicly, against the tip.; Bob (Chronicler, 1.0) filed 'hurt': the Yakuza bused every teammate, yet the marks and reveals gave mechanical proof anyway and the town just moved to the next lead. Weak: busing kept Grace hidden to the end. What it couldn't hide was Dave's public No push before the nomination. | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Carol (0.16): Carol publicly defended Alice and stated Innocent on a hang she couldn't stop (4-2). The two Innocents pointed straight at her and Bob named her on D4. Only the Village's Juror misread saved her.; Dave, Carol (0.16 each): Dave publicly defended Carol on a hang he couldn't stop (4-3). It didn't save her and cost him credibility before his own D3 trial.; Moderator (0.4), Dave, Judy, Frank (0.16 each): Judy defended Heidi and voted Innocent in a hopeless 5-2 hang and was shot that night. Dave, the holder, voted Guilty on likely-Jester Mallory and the revenge killed him with his puppet unused.; Moderator's review: Carol stated Innocent on Bob in a 3-2 hang she couldn't stop, and the one Innocent beyond Bob's own named her as the next suspect. She survived only because the hang ended the Day and the Katana hid her from the N3 check. Bob's 'wrong' is a misapplication (he lobbied by PM).; Moderator (0.4) filed 'helped': Ivan argued and voted Guilty on doomed teammate Grace (8-1), was never tied to her and won alone as the last Yakuza.; Moderator, Dave, Niaj, Ivan (1.0 each) filed 'helped': Dave voted Yes and Guilty on doomed teammate Niaj (10-1) and stayed unsuspected through D3; Niaj's lone Innocent was obvious.; Grace and Heidi voted Guilty on doomed teammate Niaj (8-1). No stray Innocent pointed at them, and their votes matched every audit. Nearly every player filed it.; Moderator, Carol, Grace, Olivia, Niaj filed 'helped': every trial showed 1 Innocent (the accused's own). Grace voted Guilty on Carol, Dave and Olivia and was never traced by votes. Dave, who argued No on Carol before the nomination and then voted Guilty, was the next mark, scent and hang.; Moderator (0.4) filed 'helped' as not applied: Bob argued for Dave and voted Innocent in a 4-3 hang that two Yakuza votes of 7 couldn't stop. It marked him as the partner and Heidi shot him that night. Grace's and Heidi's 'hurt' describe Bob breaking the tip. Carol's 'wrong' (0.16) says only that the tip gave Dave himself no help.; Bob, Carol, Grace, Mallory and the moderator filed 'helped': Bob and Grace voted Yes and Guilty on Mallory (12-0) and Grace did the same on Bob. No stray Innocent pointed at them, and Grace stayed hidden until Day 4.
- [t17] (Yakuza) While a Vigilante or Sniper is alive with shots left, prioritize killing them: each shot on a Yakuza costs you more than any info role does. This is decisive at parity. Once you hold half the votes you can vote down every nomination and win with one more night kill, and the only thing that can still break that is a kill token. A Sniper's shot is a Day action that no block stops, so on the night that brings parity kill the claimed Sniper (or the likeliest Vigilante, or block them if you can't), not the investigator whose result will arrive too late to matter. | support: 6 games, total weight 2.30 | recent evidence: Carol's shots killed three Yakuza. She was only killed on N5, after her last shot.; Bob: Alice's shot killed him the same night the Katana killed her; the team should have removed the Vigilante earlier.; Judy: the Vigilante's group sat in the Diviner's draws for two nights, the team kept killing info roles, and Olivia's shot killed Judy while she held the Katana.; Moderator: on N4 the Yakuza killed Tracker Frank and left Bob alive with a publicly claimed Sniper token. At 2 v 2 on D5 Bob shot Carol and Heidi was hanged 2-1. Killing Bob would have kept parity and very likely won.; Moderator (0.4): at 4 alive last Yakuza Carol put the N4 Katana on Vigilante Alice, not on claimed Medic Heidi. Alice's own shot removed Heidi the same night and the game ended.; Moderator (0.4): at 3 of 5 the Yakuza hanged Alice 3-2 the Day she claimed Vigilante with 2 tokens left, removing the one tool that could still break their majority, then killed Heidi to win.

- [t19] (Yakuza) Keep your last will matching your current public claim every day. A will that contradicts your claim (e.g. Vanilla vs Medic) proves the lie when you die. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.78 | recent evidence: Frank's reflection after his flip.
- [t33] (Yakuza) Never speak publicly from your hidden perspective. Say 'died to a Katana', never 'my Katana'. Never state as fact anything you know only from night chat or the Yakuza action report: who held the Katana, who acted or was role-blocked, a teammate's result. This applies even when it's offered as a 'mechanics check'. It also covers endgame faction-count arguments: 'if you hang me, only Village is left' shows you know the team count. If you need a mechanic to reconcile a result, phrase it as a hypothesis about anyone ('if someone in that pair held the Katana, they'd read Good'), never as a fact about a named player. Agree on public wording in night chat. If you slip, a vague withdrawal with no source reads as confirmation. Commit at once to one Village role that explains the knowledge (e.g. Party Animal), but first check it against every visit a living Tracker or Lookout could have seen. Move the Katana off the exposed member, and kill whoever caught the slip that night. Teammates: distance yourselves and vote with the town. | support: 12 games, total weight 5.79 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.48: Carol and Judy filed 'hurt' over Judy's slip. That is a misapplication: the slip broke the tip. Erin (lone last Yakuza) said the tip gave no help answering holder questions on D5-D6. Carol and Frank pressed him on it repeatedly.; Moderator, Alice, Carol, Ivan, Frank: on D5 Alice said Erin 'was hit by the Katana on Night 4' before anyone had announced the attack. The slip opened the nomination that hanged her.; Moderator, Erin, Carol, Mallory, Dave: Erin's 'the Grunt it lists is me' and her vague 'wording slip' retraction stayed open for days and became the Village's main lead. A plain retraction with a ready cover was needed the same day.; Bob, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each): Judy said 'Ritualist' and 'night chat' in public while claiming no role. She then moved through a 'correction', 'private night notes' and 'scratch notes', and the slip was quoted until she was hanged D4.; Moderator: Alice said 'Carol was Smuggler' on D3, a role never revealed. Erin, Frank and Dave pounced, the 'typo' retraction failed, and she was hanged 4-3 the same Day.; Moderator: on her own D5 trial Heidi said the Katana had passed to her when Carol died, which proved she was Yakuza. - [t35] (Yakuza) If you must fake-claim, build it on info your team created, e.g. a Roleplayer or Sheriff 'Evil' result on your Fabricator's frame target. That also explains your suspicious votes, then pivot pressure onto whoever pushed your trial or said they voted Guilty. But the frame is a visit, and a fake Sheriff check claims a second visit to the same player. If a Lookout watched that target, the frame shows and your claimed visit doesn't. Prefer a Roleplayer cover (no visits) or a frame target nobody is likely to watch, and never have the claimer be someone who didn't visit. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.58 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.40: Moderator: Mallory built a fake Sheriff 'Ivan: Evil' on Bob's N1 frame. The frame visit itself was seen by Erin's watch on Ivan, and Mallory's claimed check had no visit there, so she was hanged. The real Sheriff checked Dave, so the frame was wasted. | recent evidence: Carol claimed Roleplayer with 'Alice = Evil', matching Bob's N1 frame. She was acquitted, then nominated and hanged Erin, the sole Guilty voter.; Bob: Alice framed her own Sheriff targets, and the 'Carol Evil' frame helped hang Vanilla Carol 9-2. The Erin read was later broken by Erin's Katana survival.
- [t40] (Yakuza) Never put team plans (Katana holder, targets, check-dodging, claims) in daytime private messages on any Day. An Eavesdropper or Wiretap reads them word for word, and the town can publish them. Don't PM teammates at all on Day 1 or Day 2. Day 1 is talk only and comes before the first night chat, so nothing is agreed yet: hold all team talk until Night 1 and plan in night chat from then on. On any Day a teammate is nominated, under pressure or has just made a role claim, send no PMs at all: even PMs to Village voters read as vote-buying, and the sender becomes the next suspect. Everyone sees that a PM was sent, and the town will ask sender and receiver in public what it said. If one is already on record, agree in night chat on a dull account that both of you give and that has nothing to do with any claim. Never describe a teammate's PM as advice about your claim or story ('keep the explanation concise and consistent', 'don't add details we can't back up'): that reads as admitting the claim is invented. If you must DM, summarize the content publicly at once, and never give several different DMs the same stock excuse. A visible PM triangle becomes the town's lead for the whole game. | support: 60 games, total weight 31.79 | against: 3 reports, total weight 0.64: Bob and Erin filed 'hurt': the 'also message villagers' clause gave Carol no cover on a hanging Day. - [t45] (Yakuza) After a failed Katana attack, assume the target is protected or has innate Defense. Don't retry blindly; kill or block the likely protector first. If no credible Medic is claimed, the survivor has either their own Defense (Arsonist/SK Standard, Jester/Executioner Basic) or the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which works only once (t370): one retry on a later night tells the two apart, and a second failure means real Defense. Then push them to the gallows (a rig works) instead of spending more night kills, which also looks pro-town, or send a teammate with an unused Katana Expert (Strong: beats Standard Defense and a Medic's free guard) or Katana Master (Divine: beats everything) on a night that member holds the Katana (t369). One more cause exists: the hidden Shaky trait makes every Katana attack by its holder level None, so it never kills, and the holder isn't told. If the same member's attacks fail on two different targets with no protector in sight, move the Katana to another teammate before blaming the targets. - [t57] (Yakuza) Give the Katana to a member whose own role matters least (Wiretap, Grunt), not your Diviner or other info source, but only if that member is also unlikely to be blocked or watched tonight. Blockers repeat, and you lose both the kill and that member's own token action. A holder with no visiting role (Vanilla claim) has no cover if a watcher places them at the victim, so agree a cover story before Day starts. Expect the obvious target, e.g. a player whose test you just learned about, to be watched. Move the Katana that same night to an unexposed, unblocked member when either of these happens: a Lookout or Tracker publicly names the holder as a visitor to a victim, or a Cartographer, Scout or Fortune Teller result narrows a victim's visitors to a few names that include the holder. The exposed holder will be blocked, shot or hanged, and the Not Evil read stops none of that. A holder who stays the lone visitor to victim after victim becomes the one name left on every elimination list. A confident organizer on Day 1 or Day 2 is often a Medic or investigator and a good kill that night, but may be an alerting Veteran. - [t61] (Yakuza) Agree the team's claim set in Night 1 chat, before Day 2. Day 1 is talk only and comes before any night chat, so no member should claim a role there: an unagreed claim can collide with a teammate's cover or with a real holder. Vanilla Villager and Grunt are never in a game unless a hand-picked list names them, so no member claims either: every cover must be a real Village role that fits that member's tokens and visits. Once a role list is public, count the unseen slots first: subtract roles proven by public effects or exact-role checks and your own unlisted Yakuza roles (an unseen Mortician fills one). Assign at most that many off-list claims across the whole team, and never put two members in one slot. Never fake-claim a role the list shows once while its holder may be alive, and never a Unique role (Vulture, Smuggler) whose holder may be alive: their counterclaim disproves you for free. A cover that fits your tokens and visits can still collide with a real holder: a duplicated claim is the first thing an Oracle guesses, and one WRONG ends it. If a real holder counterclaims, expect the test that night and kill the likeliest exact-role checker (t294) rather than hoping the duel stays open. Roles stay hidden on death, so a listed role whose known claimant is dead is the safest choice. Pick it early and keep it: switching claims once cornered is fatal. | support: 12 games, total weight 7.26 | against: 4 reports, total weight 3.16: Carol filed 'hurt' on the no-switch line. - [t87] (Yakuza) Track where a Trapper's trap could be: every visit that doesn't kill rules a player out, the Trapper's PM partners are likely spots, and the most obvious ward (a confirmed Katana survivor, a claimed investigator who was attacked before) is the likeliest. Avoid visiting the likely trapped player, or have the Diviner give the holder Strong Defense, until the winning night. A Katana Master's once-per-game Divine attack ignores a trap entirely (it isn't stopped and there is no hitback); a Katana Expert's Strong attack does not. A Trapper can't trap herself, so once she is identifiable (a soft protective claim, a known save) she is the safe kill, and her ward is open the next night unless she moved the trap. Check whether the Trapper or a ward has a death-trigger trait. In the last three or four alive, see t338. - [t100] (Yakuza) If you will need a Roleplayer cover ('my results aren't real'), set it up early, before a real townie takes the slot, and post fake results chosen before the deaths could shape them. - [t109] (Yakuza) Kill the player whose public information is narrowing your options before the Village builds a shortlist from it. Rank by future output: ask whose next result could separate your team from the Villagers under suspicion, not who has hurt you so far. A Census Taker comes first (it reads true alignment, so the holder's disguise and frames don't fool it), then a Cartographer and a Fortune Teller. A claimed Oracle or other exact-role checker joins the top of the list whenever your team's covers are Vanilla or duplicate a live claimant: each guess of a claimed role breaks one cover, and it still works on the last Yakuza when Sheriff reads no longer do. A claimed Accountant joins the top whenever a teammate holds tokens under a tokenless or 'no action' claim, has just changed claims, or the Accountant has said audits will test claims: one audit breaks that cover. A Bloodhound's running attack counts close in on a holder who attacks every night, so kill it early or keep the holder out of its repeated trios; once its counts are public and can't split your holder from a Villager in the same trio, it ranks below the checker whose result is still to come. A working Village blocker ranks here too (t163), even if you think they may be a Neutral. All of these come ahead of a Lookout or other investigator whose result is already posted. A spent Lookout also draws many visitors, so the victim's mapped group can trap your holder. Medics and blockers of a single target cost you a night each; a running count or a nightly exact-role guess exposes every lie your team tells. Choose kills by the obstacle they remove from your winning path, not by convenience. Exception: never kill the claimant whose role a teammate has just duplicated (t315); that death settles the duel against your teammate. Second exception: on the night that brings you to half the votes, a living Sniper or Vigilante with tokens outranks every info role (t17), because a shot is the only thing that can still break parity. Third exception: don't kill a claimed investigator on the night their obvious next check is your publicly suspected teammate. Their will names the target, so the kill points at that teammate (t193); take the Census Taker or another checker whose target isn't written down. | support: 32 games, total weight 15.60 | against: 7 reports, total weight 4.12: Moderator filed 'hurt': following 'kill the Cartographer' the Yakuza killed Alice, the claimant Bob had duplicated. - [t177] (Yakuza) A Cursed player's curse (a trait, R24) Divine-attacks a random living Good player, so killing one costs the Village two players. Don't avoid a player who has revealed they are Cursed as 'dangerous'. Read each death-trigger trait to see which side it punishes: Cursed hits only Good players, while Avenger kills every player whose attack was fatal, i.e. your Katana holder, a Ritualist whose guess hit the same target that night, and an Armed teammate whose hidden gun hit it (Yakuza can now hold Armed). No investigation sees traits, so any night kill on a Villager can cost you the attacker: keep the Katana on the member you can best afford to lose, never on one holding unspent one-shot tokens, and don't stack two killers on one target. An 'Avenger' cause of death also names the dead Yakuza as the attacker in public, so the town turns to their PM partners and to any teammate whose claim the victim disputed. Count the curse as a second kill when you choose a Katana target. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Moderator, Bob, Carol, Frank, Erin, Heidi: the Yakuza avoided Alice on N2. - [t189] (Yakuza) Assume a freshly claimed or hinted investigator is protected by the Medic, trapped by a Trapper and watched by a Lookout. A player whose Day 1-2 wording hints at a result ('nothing actionable yet') is exactly whom a Trapper or Medic covered on Night 1, and a trap placed then is armed from Night 2: an attack into it kills your holder and leaves a Trapper cause of death that proves the holder attacked. Don't send the Katana at such a player on a wording hint alone. Hit an unhinted target, or if the kill is worth the risk send the member you can best afford to lose, never one holding an unspent one-shot token. A Smuggler's decoy swap gets past a Medic's same-night guard (t248) but not past a trap already sitting on the target. A holder with an unused Katana upgrade changes this (t369): Katana Master's Divine attack ignores Medic, trap and charm alike, while Katana Expert's Strong attack beats a free Standard guard but is still stopped by a trap, a charm or a Strong-token protection. Never send the Katana at a player a Lookout or Tracker has publicly said they will watch. Kill the watcher first, or hit someone else. When your Wiretap or Eavesdropper reads a Village PM plan (e.g. 'guess X tonight'), assume the players in that exchange will watch or guard the tester. Killing the tester then exposes the holder, so kill the sender instead, or send a holder whose agreed public claim explains a visit there. A Katana holder sent straight at a watched target will be named as a visitor even if the attack fails, and a low-value holder with a Vanilla claim has no answer for that visit. Prefer an unclaimed or less obvious target. - [t193] (Yakuza) Killing the investigator whose public result already points at you doesn't erase it: their will and their posted results still stand for the town. The same goes for any player whose public push or will names your teammate. Kill them and that will is the first thing everyone reads in the morning, and 'they were silenced' beats 'it's a frame'. It also holds for a check that hasn't resolved yet: a claimed investigator's will usually names tonight's target and how to read each result, so killing them on the night they check your suspected teammate publishes the plan and reads as 'the Yakuza feared this result'. If the Day's talk made that check obvious, kill the investigator a night earlier, before they commit to a target, or kill someone else and prepare the teammate's cover for the result; a second investigator may land on the same teammate anyway. If a posted result or accusation is already damning, plan the Day around it instead: prepare a counter-narrative, bus the exposed teammate, or spend a Day tool (puppet the main accuser). Spend the Katana on someone whose information isn't public yet, or on a player neutral to your team, and let a lone accuser look paranoid while he lives. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator and Frank: killing Census Taker Heidi on N2 made her flip and will confirm the census. Dave opened D3 by nominating Frank on exactly that basis.; Bob, Grace, Judy, Mallory (1.0 each): the N2 Katana on Seer Ivan didn't stop his Bob+Carol check. His will named the pair and said 'if I die, look at Bob', and the kill became the main reason Bob was hanged on D3.

- [t200] (Yakuza) When a teammate fake-claims an investigator, pick 'Evil' targets nobody can soon prove Good: not a likely Katana target, not a claimed Medic or Vanilla who may soon be confirmed (by surviving an attack, an exact-role check or a verified log). - [t204] (Yakuza) If an Arsonist may be alive (e.g. an archive lists one), hunt it as well as the Village. A doused Yakuza is told an Arsonist doused them. Report it in night chat at once: it proves a live Arsonist and means the next ignite will hit you. Good players are never told, so you can't see the douses on villagers, and one ignite can kill your whole remaining team. Never say in public that you were doused: only a non-Good player can know it. Steer the town's suspicion toward quiet unclaimed players, and consider spending a kill on the likeliest Arsonist rather than one more villager: a Ritualist's 2-token Divine guess of 'Arsonist', or the Katana on the night a teammate with Katana Expert or Katana Master upgrades it (t369). A plain Standard Katana fails on the Arsonist's Standard Defense, though that failure helps name them (t45). | support: 3 games, total weight 0.66 | recent evidence: Ivan: the Yakuza ignored the archive-listed Arsonist, and Mallory's ignite killed Ivan, the last Yakuza.; Judy: the team had no plan for the living Arsonist, whose N4 ignite killed two Yakuza.; Bob and Carol: the Yakuza killed only info roles, while Arsonist Heidi's ignite killed two Yakuza and she went on to win.

- [t224] (Yakuza) Use the Night 1 Katana attack. Day 1 is talk only: aim at whoever claimed or hinted at a power role there or led the talk, and if nobody did, pick any non-teammate rather than skip. In a large town with many unclaimed investigators, a skipped kill lets every power role reach Day 2, claim and cross-check results. That shared picture corners your team fast. Skip only for a concrete reason. A Night 1 kill can land on an alerting Veteran and kill the holder, so on Night 1 move the Katana to the member you can best afford to lose (a Wiretap or Informant, whose role needs no action), never one holding an unspent one-shot token (Puppeteer, Clerk). | support: 11 games, total weight 4.56 | recent evidence: Moderator: the Yakuza wasted N1. - [t248] (Yakuza) (Team with a Smuggler) Don't veto the swap out of fear it will redirect your own attack. A swap moves exactly the actions aimed at the two swapped players, visiting or Astral, so plan it in night chat and know where the Katana goes. Use 1: beat the Medic. Aim the Katana at a decoy and swap the decoy with the player you really want dead, whom the Medic is likely protecting. The Katana lands on the real target, and the protection aimed at them moves to the decoy. Before submitting, work through both cases (the Medic guards the real target, the Medic guards the decoy). The kill only lands if the save isn't on the player the Katana ends up hitting. Use 2: dodge an investigation. If you expect to be checked tonight, swap yourself with a Villager. A Sheriff, Seer or audit aimed at you lands on them and reports their result under your name, and so do Astral checks: an Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a Scrutineer pick or a map. Your own team's actions move too: a Ritualist guess or Diviner pick aimed at a swapped player lands on the other, and a guess that misses that way outs the Ritualist and spends the tokens, so tell the team who is swapped. Random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cursed player's curse) are not moved. Never swap anyone with the Katana's target unless you mean to move the kill, and never swap yourself with the target. The Smuggler visits both swapped players, and both are told they were swapped. Expect them to post the notices, and have a cover ready for the visits. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 5 games, total weight 3.40 | recent evidence: From a revision at the instructions' request, not from a game. A self-swap by Dave on N2 would have sent the mark, the scent and the guess to a decoy, and a decoy swap could have carried the Katana past Heidi's guard on Bob.

A continuing game doesn't itself prove a Yakuza is alive, so the argument bites only when you are the one plausible blocker. A no-kill night on which you were blocked then proves you held the Katana. - [t290] (Yakuza) Choose the Katana holder from the team's cover claims, and agree the covers on Night 1 with the holder in mind. A Katana visit shows up in Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer groups and Lookout lists. While an FT or Cartographer is active, never give the Katana to a member with no real visit (Wiretap, Grunt, Ballot Stuffer, Clerk, Informant) who claims Vanilla or another no-visit role. - [t117] (Archivist) Consult the archive early, while the living population and your sample are largest; waiting risks dying with the token unused. Report the full list, duplicates included, and remind the town how it is built. Of the N roles shown, floor(0.5 * N) are real roles of random players, and the rest are fakes drawn from all roles. So about half the list is fake, any single entry may be fake, a missing role may be present, and a role shown once can have several holders. Read it as odds, not a census. Post the list before the roles on it have been claimed: each listed role that a player then claims backs both the list and that claimant, while a claim of a listed role that first appears after the list is public, above all from a player who held their claim back, is the weakest kind and needs a test. Then match every listed role to a claimant. A dangerous entry (Arsonist, Serial Killer, Ritualist) that nobody claims is as likely real as fake, and its holder is then someone claiming a role off the list: name those off-list claimants as the pool and keep asking until each dangerous role is accounted for. Cross the list with confirmed claims and self-proving effects after every death to narrow the rest. - [t259] (Census Taker) Your count reads true alignment: the Katana holder's Good disguise, frames and the Notorious trait don't fool it. Ask every member of a group to confirm the count against what they know of themselves, so your claim becomes trusted. You pick only the first member; the other two are drawn at random from everyone else alive. Aim each census at one unknown: when both draws are already cleared, the count names that player exactly, and otherwise the group joins the chain for later subtraction. Post the group, the alignment counted and the count as soon as it bears on a vote or its members can confirm it. A Census Taker claim is a sure guess for a possible Ritualist (no Roleplayer is told it), so when one may be in the game write the full log in your will before you claim and expect to die that night. - [t275] (Bloodhound) Each night you learn three names and one combined count: every Attack those three have made this game, tonight's included. It never says which of the three attacked, and it is a running total, so early trios set the baseline. One Katana holder who attacks every night adds 1 each night, so a count of 2 can be one player's two attacks, and an attacker outside the trio (a Bounty Hunter, a Serial Killer) adds nothing to it. A 0 means none of the three has attacked yet (a role-blocked attack doesn't count, and a Yakuza who hasn't held the Katana also reads 0). A positive count gives a pool of three. Treat the counts as equations: subtract every attack that is proven (a trap, Enforcer or alert death proves that player attacked that night; a Katana attack proves its attacker did, normally the holder), write out each split that remains, and narrow it by crossing trios across nights with each other, with 0 trios and with confirmed players. Remember that Village attackers count too: Vigilante shots, Veteran alerts, trap hits, an Armed Villager's gun and a Villager's once-per-game Stolen Blade attack, which looks exactly like the Katana. Spend scent tokens to put the one player who stays ambiguous after that (typically a quiet player who claims no night action) into the trio. The other two are still random, so the count names that player only when both of them are already cleared. Don't spend a token on the loud disputed claimant: a 0 there can't tell a Jester or a non-holder Yakuza from a Villager. Post every trio and count with the night, as a small table (night, trio, total, and which nights each member could have attacked), and say outright that the totals are cumulative, before anyone nominates on them. Write every remaining reading into your will so the town can finish the deduction if you die. - [t8] (Medic) Protect the likeliest Katana targets: vocal analysts early, then claimed investigators. Never protect a claimed or likely Veteran who may still hold alert tokens: your protection is a visit, an alert Strong-attacks every visitor (your own Standard Defense wouldn't stop it), and a Veteran on alert already has Standard Defense (t153). Guard another likely target instead. Free Standard protection, used night after night, can carry the town, but it stops only Basic and Standard attacks such as the Katana at its usual level. A Serial Killer's attack and an Arsonist's ignite are Strong and go straight through it, and so does the Katana on the one night a Yakuza with the Katana Expert trait raises it to Strong (a Katana Master raises it to Divine once, which nothing stops). No investigation sees traits, so a ward can die to the Katana through your free guard once a game. Once a cause of death, an archive or a profile points to a Neutral killer, or the Katana has gone quiet because the Yakuza look dead, spend a token on Strong protection (1 token on another player) for your most valuable ally on each night a Strong kill is likely; an ignite hits every doused player at once. While the Katana still kills, the night after you publicly claim Medic you are the likeliest target, so spend a token on Standard self-protection that night. If a Serial Killer cause of death has already appeared, Standard self-protection won't stop its Strong attack: Strong self-protection costs both tokens and leaves your ward unguarded, so the real answer is to stay unclaimed and never say you will protect tonight (t94). After you save a claimed investigator, the Yakuza will either retry them or switch to you. Keep free protection on them on most nights, but while your claim is public and you still hold tokens, spend one within the next night or two rather than dying with both. Strong doesn't stop a Divine attack (a Ritualist's 2-token guess, a Katana Master's upgraded Katana). Don't follow a fixed pattern a Pharmacist or holder can predict. Dying, or ending the game, with unspent tokens wastes them. | support: 34 games, total weight 16.96 | against: 16 reports, total weight 5.14: Erin filed 'hurt': she never self-protected and died N5 with 0 tokens. - [t94] (Medic) Never announce your protection target, in public or by private message (a Wiretap reads PMs). That includes a self-protection: the Katana simply goes to someone else, and your own Vigilante may 'test' the announced Defense with a shot. Don't claim Medic or name your ward before a trial or a survival question needs it: a named Medic is who a Pharmacist blocks on every kill night, and a block stops all your protection, self-protection included. If you are told you were role-blocked, either a blocker has read your pattern or you have the hidden Drowsy trait (R24: blocked on about half the nights, with no blocker). Change wards, and post the notice once it bears on a death (if no Village blocker claims it, a Pharmacist is alive or you are Drowsy). After a second block, treat your protection as unreliable: say so plainly, give your full log of targets and blocks in one message, and never promise a save or a Defense for tonight. Free Standard protection stops the Katana at its usual Standard level but not a Strong attack (Serial Killer, Arsonist ignite, a 3-token shot, a Veteran's alert, a trap, or the Katana on the one night a Katana Expert raises it to Strong). Hold your Strong tokens while only the Katana threatens, and spend them on a confirmed investigator as soon as a Strong attacker is credible (t8). While the Katana still kills, keep one token for a Standard self-protection: at 0 tokens you can't protect yourself. Don't protect the same claimed player in an obvious nightly pattern. A Pharmacist can block it, and a Smuggler can aim the Katana at a decoy and swap the decoy with your ward, which moves your save onto the decoy and the kill onto your ward. - [t116] (Trapper) A trap fires only when the trapped player is attacked. Put it on a claimed investigator or other info role the killers must visit, never on a suspect: nobody attacks a Yakuza, so a trap there stays silent and clears no one. Place traps for the next night's threat: a new trap is not armed on the night you place it, and placing one removes your old trap at once. So moving it to a player in danger tonight protects nobody tonight: the new ward is uncovered and the old one is open. Once you have an armed trap on a player the killers may hit, leave it there. If the killers know where it is and stay away, it is already working. If you do move it, never say so: that tells the killers the old ward is free. The trap persists through your death and through role-blocks on you. Trapper isn't Unique, so a second claimant may be real. A trap placed on a player who died that same night was still a legal placement (night actions resolve together), so don't call a rival's trap 'impossible' without checking the rules. | support: 8 games, total weight 5.10 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Carol (Trapper, 1.0) filed 'hurt': she left her trap on the obvious ward (N1 survivor Alice). The Yakuza deduced it and killed her instead. Mostly a misapplication: her D4 soft protective claim (against t86 and t32) is what identified her. Her suggestion to move the trap now and then conflicts with the entry's 'leave it there'. | recent evidence: Carol's reflection.; Grace: a new trap replaces the old one at once and arms only the next night. The Katana killed Frank on N2 and Dave on N3; a trap moved to Frank on N1 or placed there first would have stopped Carol's attack and hit her back. - [t167] (Trapper) A publicly announced trap on a confirmed investigator deters killers, who will route around that player, so it works like protection. But a lone holder simply avoids the predictable target, so a hidden trap is the one that actually catches someone: choose between deterrence and catching. In the last three or four alive, deterrence stops paying. A killer who knows the trap is on the other Villager kills you instead and then never attacks, and at two alive no hang can pass, so the stall rule ends the game as a loss for everyone (t337). There only a trap the holder doesn't know about can win, so keep its location secret late in the game and put your effort into winning the Day's trial. Traps arm only from the next night on, so aim at whoever the Katana is likely to hit in two nights. A silent trap also gives free negative info: report it as 'no non-Village visitor to X'. If a Lookout reveals your visit to a victim, claim at once with mechanics others can check. Any Trapper claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, so claim no earlier than a sighting or a vote forces it. | support: 5 games, total weight 2.52 | recent evidence: Judy announced a trap on Sheriff Grace; Rupert never attacked Grace, and Grace survived. Judy noted a lone holder avoids a claimed Sheriff's trap. Her immediate Trapper claim cleared her Lookout sighting. Conflicts partly with t86.; Carol: her announced trap on non-target Dave deterred visits but caught no one. Deterrence only pays on a player the killers actually want.; Alice: a trap on claimed investigators Frank or Niaj would have hit a visiting killer. Aim at the Katana's likely target, not at a suspect.; Moderator: Alice's publicly known armed trap on Frank kept the Yakuza and the SK away from him on N2. The deterrence held until she moved it and said so.; Mallory: the trap stayed hidden, caught the holder on N2, and she claimed in one line only after the trap death made the claim check out.
- [t320] (Kitsune) Charm the player likeliest to be attacked tonight (a claimed investigator, the player who set the tone on Day 1, a player the killers just failed on), not an arbitrary one. Never announce a charm, a held attack or a release target, however hard the table presses: the players demanding it are often the killers, and a named Kitsune holding a kill is the night's first target. While you hold a swallowed attack you can't charm anyone, yourself included, and it is lost if you die. So release it within a night or two, but on a real lead: an unexplained Katana survivor, a claimless or late-Vanilla player once a killer's cause of death has shown, a PM pair who push the same line, a player who kept a stated Guilty after hard evidence turned the case. Never release on a claim clash. Only Smuggler and Vulture are Unique, so a second claimant of any role may be real, and a release on a rival claimant has now killed a Villager three times (twice a real duplicate claimant). Use the level you were told. A swallowed Strong attack (a Serial Killer's, or a Katana that a Katana Expert raised to Strong) is one of the few night kills that beats an Arsonist's or Serial Killer's Standard Defense; a swallowed Katana is otherwise Standard and fails there. Your charm doesn't stop a Divine attack (a Ritualist's 2-token guess, a Katana Master's upgraded Katana). The cause you swallowed proves that killer is alive and that your ward is not that killer: post it when it bears on a vote, weighing that an exact Kitsune claim feeds a possible Ritualist. Check whether the ward reported two attacks, which fits two charms. If another Kitsune exists, split your wards. A release is announced with the swallowed attack's cause, so say afterwards that the death was yours. | support: 5 games, total weight 2.52 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Erin, Heidi, Bob (0.16 each): on N2 both Kitsunes charmed Carol and swallowed a Katana and an SK attack. On N3 neither could charm, Carol died, Heidi released her Strong SK attack on rival Kitsune Erin instead of Katana-survivor Dave (the SK), and Erin died holding her Katana. Heidi's announced charm got her killed N4. One game.; Frank: his blind N1 charm on a random seat did nothing and he was the N1 Katana kill. His lesson matches the tip: charm the player whose death hurts most, a claimed investigator. Thin.; Moderator (0.4), Grace (0.16): Grace's N1 charm on Alice swallowed the SK's Strong attack. On N2 she released it on rival-claim Trapper Bob (a real Trapper) after announcing it, had no charm that night and died to the Katana and the SK. Alice, Bob and Erin filed the same lesson.; Olivia, Mallory, moderator (1.0 each): Olivia's N2 charm on hidden Sheriff Frank swallowed the Katana. - [t7] (Vigilante) Dying with unused tokens is the usual Vigilante failure: holding all your shots makes you a Vanilla Villager. Shoot on a lead, not on proof. Set a concrete trigger by Day 2-3 and fire a Basic shot (1 token) once it's met, while the Village still has numbers to spare: a first shot at 4 alive has to be right. Fire anyway by the time the Yakuza could be near parity; on a table of 8 that can be Night 2 (t286), and once no Village nomination can pass your shots are its only kills. Good leads include a claim conflict, an Evil check against an unverifiable claim, a credible map or group result that leaves one unconfirmed member as the likely holder, or the stated votes and PM partners of a player shown Yakuza by a mechanical result (e.g. those who said they voted No on that player's nomination, argued to delay the hang, or whose stated votes the totals contradict). Roles are hidden on death and hard leads are scarce, so the strongest claimless voice is a fair target: someone who leads nominations on 'silence' or always echoes the consensus without results, or the one player who still refuses to claim any role. A lone read/vote mismatch or one player's private request is not enough on its own; bring a PM'd request into public first. An unverified claim is not a lead either: never shoot to 'test' a claimed Medic's announced protection or a block story. A role-block (or the hidden Drowsy trait) leaves a real Medic with no Defense, so the test kills a Villager, and a protector whose stated wards match the Katana's victims fits the deaths. When a Yakuza has just been hanged on a Yakuza-only claim, the lead is the player who defended them and cast the other Innocent vote, not whoever that player points you at by PM. Traits (R24) raise the cost of a wrong shot, and no investigation sees them: killing a Cursed villager also Divine-attacks a random Good player, and killing an Avenger kills you too. A Basic shot that fails on a suspect no credible Medic protects fits innate Defense (Arsonist or Serial Killer Standard, Jester or Executioner Basic) or the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which any player can have and which works only once (R24). It is not proof of a side: shoot the same player again on a later night with 2 tokens, which gets through a spent Narrow Escape and Basic Defense, and a second failure with no protector then points to a Neutral killer. Post the failed shot the next Day only while the Village can still pass a hang; once the Yakuza may hold half the votes the claim just gets you hanged, so stay unclaimed and fire again. If you already suspect a Neutral killer and want the kill, only a Strong shot (3 tokens) beats Standard Defense. If your shot kills, you learn the victim's role. That is one of the few ways a dead player's role becomes known, so post the night, target and role the next Day. Claim only once your shots have paid off: the claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops, so expect to die that night and fire any shot you still have a lead for. | support: 20 games, total weight 11.37 | against: 9 reports, total weight 4.31: Alice: Niaj followed the 'stay hidden and shoot' advice and shot Erin, the Surveyor, on a behavioral read. That cost the town a power role and opened space for Mallory's fake Vigilante claim.; Dave: the tip listed vote/read contradictions as a mechanical lead. Acting on one killed Cardinal Grace, and her curse took Alice and Frank.; Moderator: Grace treated the strict trigger as a ban on shooting and died holding 3 shots; the No votes on Bob's nomination were a sufficient lead.; Grace: she never met the bar she set, and the Yakuza could predict her restraint.; Judy (Yakuza, won): Grace's three untouched shots were worthless while the Yakuza picked off power roles.; Grace: her Basic shot on Heidi failed against Standard Defense, cost a token and told the Arsonist a Vigilante was alive. The moderator counts the miss as useful evidence.; Moderator (0.4) and Grace (0.16): Grace read the trigger too conservatively, never shot despite a mechanical map lead on Bob, and died on N5 with 3 tokens. She broke the 'don't wait' clause.; Moderator (0.4) and Heidi (0.16): the strict trigger again led to a Vigilante dying with every token. Misapplication, but the third such game. The edit addresses it.; Mallory (Vigilante, 1.0) filed 'hurt': she was the blind N1 Katana kill and never fired, so a Day 2-3 trigger came too late. Bad luck rather than a tip failure: her proposed fix, a random Basic shot on N1, fires before any result exists and most likely hits a Villager (t247). The usable part is to put the token plan in the pre-Night-1 will.; Dave (Enforcer) filed 'wrong': 'set a trigger and shoot on a lead' doesn't fit an Enforcer, whose attack fires only if the target attacks. An audience misfit rather than a fault in the Vigilante advice; the moderator says he should have spent a token on a double visit (t323).; Moderator and Grace filed 'hurt', Alice 'wrong': Alice held every shot through N1-N3, then on N4 at 4 alive fired a Basic shot to 'test' claimed Medic Heidi's announced self-protection. - [t66] (Vigilante) If someone else claims your role, counterclaim at once with details only the real holder has (token count, shot reasoning) and put the claim in your will. Vigilante isn't Unique, so the other claimant may be real too: a claim clash alone is never the lead for a shot. Test them with a vote, a Sheriff check or an audit, and shoot them only once another result breaks their claim (a token count that doesn't fit, a claimed shot that matches no death). Then use at least a Standard shot (2 tokens) in case they have Defense, that same night. The counterclaim names your exact role, so expect the Katana, or a Ritualist guess that no Medic stops, right after it, and have your best-lead shot queued. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.90 | recent evidence: Niaj: Mallory's copied Vigilante claim muddied his identity until role-count math resolved it.; Moderator: Ivan stayed unclaimed while Carol fake-claimed Vigilante, then shot her the night of the conflict. - [t323] (Enforcer) Your free attack is Basic and can't kill a Serial Killer or Arsonist (Standard Defense), so against a Neutral-killer suspect pay 1 token for Strong. Visit the player likeliest to attack tonight: a late Vanilla claimant read Not Evil once a Serial Killer cause of death has appeared, or the likely Katana holder (Basic is enough for a Yakuza). Blind free visits on Nights 1-2 rarely fire, and a visit to a Katana victim or a silent player does nothing. Pay for the two-target visit as soon as the holder is narrowed to two names: two suspicious claimants, or a visible pair who PM each other every Day and push the same line. The holder counts as attacking, so covering both members catches whichever one carries the Katana. It matters most on a night the Katana is likely to move: it tends to pass to whoever the Sheriff is about to check or to a suspected fake claimant, because the holder reads clean. A kill there proves the dead player attacked. A blocked player makes no attack, so a Village blocker on the same suspect cancels your visit: agree in public that blocker and Enforcer cover different suspects. Your attack lands only if they attack that night, and it doesn't cancel theirs: if you are their target you both die. Don't describe a result in your role's own terms while unclaimed ('visited X, X made no attack' names you an Enforcer and draws a block or the Katana): give no result or a full claim. Claiming Enforcer and pressing a suspect in public can draw their attack onto you, so on the night after you nominate a suspect, visit that suspect and their partner: if they come for you, the trade at least removes the holder. Dying with all three tokens is the usual waste. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.96 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Dave, Alice, Heidi (0.16 each): Alice's free visits on N1-N2 did nothing. One game.; Moderator (0.4), Dave (0.16): on N3 Dave visited only Carol, who made no attack, while her PM partner Grace held the Katana and killed him. He died with 3 tokens; a paid double visit on the pair would have killed the holder.

When you do claim, give your full past alert record (each night, visitors or none) in one message and keep it fixed. A record that grows a night each Day looks invented even when it's true.
