- [t313] (Sniper) Shoot on a mechanical lead, never on a claim clash, never on Day 1 (talk only, when nothing exists to aim on) and never on Day 2 before any night result has been posted. Only Smuggler and Vulture are Unique, so a second claimant of any other role (a second Sniper, a second Auditor) may be real, and a late duplicate whose result matches the first claimant's is what two real holders checking the same player produce. Three times now a Sniper has killed a real duplicate claimant. Ask them for their token count or full log, test them with a vote or a Sheriff check, and shoot only if another result breaks their claim. Never shoot a player with a corroborated Not Evil read. Before shooting 'the last Yakuza', check that one can still be alive (t96): a trap death or a Vigilante kill on a Katana night may already have removed the team, and then the win-blocker is a Neutral behind a claim with nothing checkable, whom a vote can hang at no cost in tokens. Never spend a shot on a claimed Bounty Hunter, Jester, Executioner or Magpie: none of them blocks your win, and a Bounty Hunter who has spent its one token can't attack again. Before firing, ask whether this target can still stop a Village win; the shot belongs on a Yakuza or Serial Killer suspect, and when nothing better exists that means a player who has never claimed a role. Good leads: an Evil read the claim doesn't explain, a broken or copied claim, the PM partner of a player a death cause showed to be an attacker or who was hanged on a Yakuza-only claim, the players who nominated and stated Guilty against a corroborated clear, the one stated Guilty voter left beside exposed Yakuza in a small Guilty minority, or one of the last two names an elimination leaves once tracks, blocks on the kill night and proven roles have struck the rest. Nobody is told who shot, so early in the game don't announce a shot or claim Sniper to explain one: a declared shot invites one back, and a claimed shooter is the next night's kill. Near the end (4-5 alive, a mechanical lead, the vote lock close) do the opposite: claim Sniper and take the shot on the same turn. A shot that then kills the suspect you named backs your claim, and your vote can steer the last hang. Your shot fires after a random delay of up to n-1 speaking turns and not at all if you die first (R25), so a shot fired back at someone who already shot at you often loses the race. Night protection is gone by Day but innate Defense is not: a Basic shot fails on a Jester or Executioner, and only Strong beats a Serial Killer or Arsonist. The outcome of a Day shot is announced to everyone, so a 1-token shot that a suspect survives shows innate Defense to the whole table: a cheap public test of a Neutral-killer suspect you can't kill, with the hang to follow. Don't hold your last token for proof: fire it at your best unchecked suspect before the Yakuza can hold half the votes. If they already hold half (e.g. 2 v 2), they vote down every nomination and the next night kill ends the game, so shoot that Day even on a coin flip between two suspects: a hit gives the Village back its hanging majority. Or use the token as the innate-Defense test once only a Neutral killer is left. | support: 4 games, total weight 2.52 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Dave, Heidi, Carol, Bob, Erin (0.16 each): Dave and Heidi were both real Snipers. Dave named the two-Snipers possibility, then shot Heidi on D2; Heidi's Strong counter-shot at Sheriff-cleared Dave never fired because she died first. Dave then held his last token until Frank and Grace, who had pushed his hang, hanged him D4.; Moderator (0.4), Frank, Ivan (0.16 each): Ivan shot Bob on D3 once the copied claim, the profile and the PM link to Avenger-killed Carol converged, and it killed the last Yakuza. He then held his last token and died to the N5 ignite.; Moderator (0.4), Alice, Bob, Erin, Grace, Heidi (0.16 each): Grace and Bob were both real Snipers. Grace's Basic shot at Bob never fired because his Standard shot killed her first. Bob's last token, spent on Carol at 2 v 2 on D5, decided the game.; Carol, moderator and five others: third game in which a Sniper shot killed a real duplicate claimant (Auditor Alice). Carol says she ignored the warning.
- [t103] (Prosecutor) Your claim is self-proving: the prosecution is announced the next Day, and Yakuza can't fake it. Stay unclaimed while your tokens are live. Spend each prosecution as soon as a solid mechanical lead exists, while enough Village voters are alive to win the trial. If you hold tokens for the endgame, the Katana removes your voters first and you may die with them unused. Good leads: a public claim of a Yakuza-only role, the slip or PM partner of a player exposed by a mechanical result, the player who defended or stated Innocent on a hanged near-certain Yakuza, a claim that overflows a public role list, a player in a confirmed visitor list, or a small Guilty total on a proven townie that the stated votes don't account for. Never spend one on a claimant whose results keep checking out (e.g. an Oracle with a verified hit), or on a result you call false without recomputing it from the role text (t305). An acquittal wastes the token and one of the Day's three trials, and an execution ends the Day: at 4-5 alive a wrong prosecution uses up the Village's last real hang, so double-check the lead before the night. A prosecution skips the nomination vote, so No votes can't stall it. Before each prosecution, name the target as a suspect in public that Day, but don't announce that you will prosecute tonight: a Pharmacist will block you. Log each prosecution in your will by NIGHT (N2, N3), not by trial day. If a copycat counterclaims, those earlier reads and correct labels are your proof. If you are role-blocked, you are told and no token is spent. If you have already claimed, post 'N3: prosecution of X was role-blocked' in your first message the next Day, before anyone can call the missing trial a bluff, and ask an Accountant to confirm your token count. If you are still unclaimed, keep the block in your will instead: a Prosecutor claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops. Under R21 the prosecution still fires even if you die that night, so spend your last token on your top suspect even when you expect to be killed. | support: 5 games, total weight 2.23 | recent evidence: Heidi's prosecution proved her claim against Frank, who was hanged on D4. Her prosecution of Erin led to Erin's hang on D5. Erin said the Yakuza should have killed the Prosecutor first.; Grace's prosecutions of Olivia and Ivan both flipped Yakuza, and her posthumous prosecution of Heidi won the game.; Judy kept both prosecution tokens unused all game and regretted not forcing a trial on her top lead.; Moderator and Alice: Alice claimed Prosecutor D4, held both tokens 'for a lead the Village agrees on' while naming Ivan herself; Ivan blocked her N5-N6 and she was killed N6 with both tokens unused.; Moderator, Bob: Bob's N2 prosecution of Alice on her public Ritualist claim won a 4-2 hang. His N3 prosecution of Dave on a misread Juror result filled D4 at 4 alive with a mis-hang.
- [t84] (Philanthropist) Give tokens only to roles verified by something other than their own claim. Best is a role confirmed by public effects whose token pays off by day, e.g. a Prosecutor whose announced prosecutions hit players later caught by mechanical results. Never announce whom you plan to gift. Don't gift a player likely to die that night, such as one who just announced a night action: a gift to a dead player confirms nothing and looks invented. Don't sit on both tokens waiting for certainty either. You're never told if the token was thrown away, so a fake claimer can just 'confirm' the gift, and your credibility gets tied to theirs. Your gift is a visit, so a claimed Fortune Teller or Cartographer must account for it, which can test their claim. A swap can redirect a gift without telling you. | support: 5 games, total weight 1.99 | recent evidence: Ivan announced a survey token for Frank. Frank 'confirmed' it, and once Frank flipped Diviner, Ivan looked like his partner and was hanged.; Judy and Grace: the gift to a verified Prosecutor was decisive.; Niaj gave Mallory a medic token, and she died N4 with 3 unspent tokens. Niaj says verify which claimant is real before gifting.; Moderator: Erin gave both tokens to Surveyor Carol, whose claim was corroborated. The extra tokens funded surveys, and Carol's will confirming the gifts cleared Erin on D4.; Frank held both tokens unused for three nights while Veteran Erin, whose alert kills matched the announced deaths, spent her last alerts.
- [t115] (Party Animal) Choose blocks by the damage they prevent. Block the strongest Katana-holder suspect every night: a player a Lookout or Tracker saw at a victim, the last living name an elimination map allows, or late in the game the likeliest lone holder. Keep blocking that suspect every night until they are hanged. Switching away hands them a free kill, often on you. The same works on a Neutral-killer suspect: a quiet night after your block is a mechanical result. The Katana passes to a teammate when its holder dies, so re-check who likely holds it after every death. Before you have a suspect, use blocks as tests on claimed Vanillas and quiet players, or block a player likely to confirm it publicly and credibly (an investigator about to claim): a block confirmed by a trusted target is what separates you from a Pharmacist making the same claim. You are told only whether the block failed because the target can't be role-blocked. Such a failure shows an immune role (Pharmacist, Smuggler, Veteran or Party Animal). Report every block in the moderator's exact wording ('did not fail because of immunity'), never 'my block landed': a loose phrase gets attacked as a mechanics error. A no-kill night that matches your block is the town's strongest lead against a lone holder. Write each block target in your will before the night. Post the log publicly, before anyone can confirm it, once it bears on a claim, a no-kill night or a vote: a target who later confirms being blocked proves you are real. Posting names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops, so when one may be in the game don't post a log that shows nothing yet. Never block a protector or investigator confirmed by an independent source, and avoid blocking quiet players who defended confirmed claims, who may be a hidden Vigilante or Enforcer: a blocked killer makes no attack, so your block also cancels an Enforcer's visit to the same suspect. A persistent liar who keeps showing no visits may be an Executioner, and blocking them prevents nothing. | support: 5 games, total weight 3.71 | recent evidence: Alice's reflection on blocking Frank (Executioner) versus the Katana carriers.; Moderator: after Heidi tracked Niaj to Frank on the Katana night, Peggy blocked Niaj on N4 and no kill happened.; Niaj blocked the Katana holder on N3-N5, stopping every kill. He notes the town should then hang the blocked holder by day.; Moderator, Mallory and others: Mallory blocked Heidi on N2 (ballot token lost) and N3 (holder, no kill), then Frank on N4 (holder, no kill). The no-kill nights became key evidence in both hangs.; Mallory: write your N1 block target in your will before Night 1. Olivia confirming the block was all that saved Mallory's log.
- [t36] (Pharmacist) Blocking your kill target stops their action, not the Medic protection coming in, and never stops an attack on them. A block on the Katana's own victim is usually wasted: an investigator who dies tonight never posts tonight's result, so block the victim only to stop a same-night shot or attack (Vigilante, Enforcer). Putting your block and the Katana on one target hides a destination from Trackers, but it wastes the kill on an obvious protection target. Spend the block on the player who could stop or punish the kill (the likely Medic, a Kitsune, a Trapper about to move a trap) or on an investigator who will live to report, while the Katana hits someone else. After a failed kill, block the likely Medic (or the Vigilante or investigator) while the Katana hits someone less protected. If you also hold the Katana, you are block-immune. A claimed investigator is a prime Lookout watch target, so vary your blocks and have a cover action ready. | support: 7 games, total weight 3.19 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: The 'pair your block with the Katana kill' advice backfired. - [t242] (Pharmacist) You can't be role-blocked, and a Village blocker whose block on you fails is told it failed on immunity. Party Animals test claimed Vanillas this way. Don't hide behind Vanilla. - [t124] (Ritualist) Guess only an exact role you have real grounds for. A wrong guess is announced at the start of the next Day, naming you as a Ritualist, and its tokens are spent either way; only a role-blocked guess keeps them. But don't wait for certainty by elimination: tokens die with you, and a Ritualist can be hanged on Day 2 off a random Seer pair with both unused. - [t72] (Puppeteer) Puppet a trusted villager on the day you need a swing, ideally the same Day as a Clerk rig, and push a mis-hang subtly in their voice. Your puppeting visit can be tracked, so the victim's will can disown the Day. Kill them that night, and have the Mortician clean them, before they can expose it. - [t42] (Fabricator) Save the forge token for a night your target is actually likely to die (e.g. the Katana target when no protector is suspected). Roles stay hidden on death, so the town reads a forged will as the victim's own claim. It still checks the will against what the victim said while alive and against living claimants and investigators. A role claim the victim never made, a lean on a player they never suspected, or one a living counterclaimant disputes, exposes the forge and the frame. Forge something vague that fits the victim's public record: copy their public claims and add at most a mild lean they could have held. Never put a mechanics argument or a teammate's talking point in it. A will that repeats a line a suspected Yakuza already said, or a rule the table has corrected, reads as Yakuza-written and backs up the player it accuses. A forged will must fit the public transcript, and invented chronology others can check exposes you. Don't spend a frame on someone who already reads Evil (a Jester, an Executioner, or a Villager a checker has already read Evil, who may be Notorious or a Turncoat). Keep your own will matching your public claim, and don't let it copy a teammate's claim word for word. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.58 | recent evidence: Dave spent his forge on N1 on Alice, but Medic Frank protected her. - [t111] (Clerk) A rig bypasses the acquittal, not the nomination. Use it on a non-teammate who can get a nomination majority but probably not a Guilty majority. - [t214] (Smuggler) Spend your swap tokens on the nights they protect the team: when a Cartographer, Lookout or Tracker is likely to catch the Katana holder, when a Vigilante shot or trap may hit a teammate, or to redirect a kill onto a protected target's neighbor. The night after you or a teammate became the Day's main suspect (you argued against a hang, or players said they would check you) is such a night: swap that member with a Villager and a Chronicler's mark, a Bloodhound's scent, an Oracle guess or a Sheriff check aimed at them lands on the other player. Two players learning that a Smuggler exists is a small price next to a marked hang. If a claimed Vigilante or other night killer with tokens is alive and suspects you, swap yourself with a harmless Villager that night: the shot aimed at you lands on them, and your Katana attack on a third player is not moved. Do it above all as the last Yakuza, when your death would end the game. A swap moves that night's actions, not a trap already sitting on a player, so it can take the Katana off a trapped target but can't carry a kill past the trap. Swapping the holder with a non-Yakuza moves every action aimed at the holder, Astral checks included (an Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a map), so plan it in night chat. It moves your own team's actions as well: a Ritualist guess aimed at a swapped player lands on the other. A swap is a visit by you to both players, adds to Fortune Teller counts and map groups, and both players are told. So while a claimed visit counter or Lookout is alive, have a cover ready or wait. A swap is cheapest once those claimants are dead, or when both swapped players are likely to die that night. Tokens still unused at 2-3 Yakuza alive are usually wasted. | support: 14 games, total weight 6.48 | recent evidence: Carol held both swaps unused. - [t58] (Ballot Stuffer) Do the trial math in night chat. Spend a token only on a Day when your doubled vote can change an outcome, e.g. near half: a doubled Innocent makes trials need more Guilty than the Village has (7 alive means 8 counted, so 5 Guilty are needed). That blocks every execution, even of an exposed teammate. Holding tokens 'for a later close trial' with no plan is the usual failure. Once a teammate has been read Evil or hanged, expect the next close trial to be on your team, possibly on you, and spend a token the night before it. A bare-majority hang (4 of 7) becomes a failing tie with your extra vote. Exception: if a result has already named Ballot Stuffer (a profile, an archive), any total above the voter count proves you are alive and sends the town back to that list. Then hold your tokens until the Day your doubled vote decides the game: at 3-4 alive, you and a teammate plus your extra vote are a sure trial majority. Spend it the night before the Day you expect to reach parity. Nomination votes aren't doubled. Vote the way you said you would publicly: the extra vote narrows suspects to voters on that side, and a stance/ballot mismatch names you. | support: 8 games, total weight 4.64 | recent evidence: Moderator, Mallory and Bob: Mallory's N2 token turned D3 into a 4-4 lock, and the exposed Grace survived.; Erin planned a doubled Innocent with three Yakuza alive so that no trial could pass. Use them on a close trial or the token is wasted.; Niaj: doubling only matters on a trial it can swing. Save it for the deciding Day.; Heidi: the margins (11-1, 9-1, 7-1) were too wide for a doubled vote to swing anything.; Alice: she held tokens while Village majorities were large and spent one the night before the Day the team could reach parity. She notes the doubled vote never helps a nomination pass, so that math is separate.; Moderator: Carol spent a ballot token on N3, the night before the trial she expected, and voted Guilty on Dave as she had said (4 of 5). On D4 her doubled Innocent made it 6 votes counted, but 4 Guilty still passed. She was never exactly one vote short, so the token changed nothing.
- [t253] (Fixer) Before each night, count the likely Guilty votes on any trial that could hang a teammate. If fixing one pro-hang voter breaks the Guilty majority (a tie acquits), spend a token on that voter the night before. The trial on your own exposed teammate, or on yourself, is the trial that matters, so don't hoard tokens for a later one. Pick a voter who has said they will vote Guilty, since the fix flips the vote they actually cast, and during the Day publicly urge them to 'vote exactly as you announced': the fix works only while they keep casting that vote. Offensively, at or near parity, fix a Villager likely to vote Innocent the night before the first close trial of a non-teammate. Their own Innocent then counts Guilty and can turn a narrow acquittal into a hang. The totals won't match that player's stated vote. On a big table suspicion falls on them, not you; at 3-4 alive the gap is easy to read and a sharp Villager will name the fix within one trial, so have a reason ready for why the fixed voter shouldn't switch. Agree in night chat that nobody fixes a player the Katana or Ritualist targets that same night: a dead voter casts nothing, and the token is wasted. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.28 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Frank (0.16): Frank was read Evil on N1 and the D2 nomination drew 4 Yes. Thin: no trial was close enough for a fix to matter (5-2 on Dave, then a 3-2 her own bloc controlled).
- [t81] (Diviner) Your results look exactly like a Surveyor's, so informed towns treat a Surveyor claim as the Diviner's twin. Accurate hits don't clear it. An Oracle guess on your claim or a profile of you breaks it (your true role is always on your own profile list), a Diviner entry on an archive or on someone else's profile points the town at it, and an Accountant sees your 0 tokens against a Surveyor's 3. Prefer a tokenless visiting cover (Lookout, Tracker, Sheriff) that names your real divine targets as its targets, and share your draws with the team in night chat instead of publishing them. If you do use Surveyor, publish a few accurate, harmless groups early and put a plausible other suspect in front of the town. Divine teammates who must visit risky targets: Strong Defense stops traps and Vigilante shots. A token gifted to you is thrown away. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.56 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Frank, Niaj, Olivia: the Surveyor cover that t81 recommends was predictable and exposed by a Diviner profile decoy plus a census. - [t172] (Diviner) (Diviner) Picking yourself gives you Strong Defense for the night but no reading; do it once you hold the Katana and expect a block or attack. - [t53] (Mortician) A clean token is spent even if the target survives. Roles are hidden on death, so a clean hides the victim's last will: it shows as 'cleaned by mortician', and you privately learn their role and will. It also blocks a Coroner, who learns nothing from a cleaned body. The public notice proves a Mortician exists. Clean only when the kill is near-certain (no possible Defense, no likely protector) and the will or body likely holds something that hurts your team: an investigator who has probably checked you, a puppeting notice, or one side of a claim duel a Coroner would examine. A clean of a player whose results are already public saves little. Don't add your clean visit to another Yakuza's Katana target while a Lookout may watch; clean only when you hold the Katana yourself or the visit is expendable. | support: 4 games, total weight 0.66 | recent evidence: Niaj's reflection.; Mallory: clean only targets sure to die. - [t68] (Wiretap) Stay quiet and never hint publicly that a Wiretap exists. The Village's own fear of a Wiretap often shuts down PM evidence for you. Never quote or closely paraphrase a PM: a quote proves you read it, and the two people in that exchange will ask how. Use what you read to steer votes and your team's night choices without revealing content. If you must use it, have an Eavesdropper cover ready with a named previous-night target who took part in that exchange (see t119). If the table sends no PMs, you are effectively a Grunt, so put your effort into a visit-ready cover claim and vote steering. Don't take the Katana as the team's 'low-value holder' while a Fortune Teller, Cartographer or Lookout is active, unless your Day 2 cover explains a visit to the victim. - [t119] (Wiretap) If you will use intercepted PMs to prove a fake Eavesdropper claim, set up a consistent target early: the declared target must take part in the exchange on the following Day. - [t309] (Informant) You see every trial ballot. In night chat, report who voted Innocent on your exposed teammates and who led the Guilty side, and use that to choose Katana targets and to spot hidden investigators. Never show vote knowledge in public that secret ballots hide. Your own ballot is as readable as anyone's: once a teammate is doomed, vote Guilty and say so (t16). An Innocent on a hopeless trial plus a public defense marks you for the Vigilante. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.16 | recent evidence: Judy (0.16) and the moderator's review: Informant Judy defended Heidi and voted Innocent in the 5-2 hang, and Olivia shot her that night. Thin: the vote readings never visibly steered a kill.
- [t335] (Talent Agent) Spend Counts on a question that decides a live dispute or a team decision. Early on, Count the threat roles that shape your kills and visits (Veteran, Trapper, Coroner, Accountant): the exact number tells the team which visits are risky and which covers or bodies can be checked. Later, count a role two players both claim to learn whether one is fake, count Sheriff or Seer to tell your Fabricator whether frames matter, count Enforcer or Party Animal before moving the Katana onto a suspected teammate. Count is usually worth more than Scan once you are planning kills. Don't spend one on what you can already infer (a Serial Killer cause of death already proves a Serial Killer). You get one token a night and three in all, so a wasted Count is missing in the endgame, when one exact-role fact can decide a vote. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Alice (Talent Agent, lost, 1.0), with Carol and Dave: the team's N3 Count went on Enforcer too late to stop the Katana move, and earlier Counts confirmed things already known. - [t97] (Vulture) Never claim Vanilla Villager: it is not in the game unless a hand-picked list names it, so the claim is a proven lie. Claim a tokenless Village role with nothing to post early and keep it word for word, one whose results are passive or Astral so no visit log can contradict it; it must match an audit of 0 and still falls to an Oracle guess or a profile. A claim with nothing checkable wears thin late: once a cumulative result such as a Bloodhound's counts leaves you as one possible reading, the player with nothing verified is the one who gets pressed. Vote with the majority and never lead a push or nominate: the nominator of a mis-hang is written into wills and becomes the lead against you. You block the Village's win, so once the Yakuza are dead the game goes on and the town hunts the last unexplained claimant, which is you. You need the Yakuza alive to kill the count down to your threshold: don't help hang them when your vote won't stand out, and let Villagers drive the hangs of Villagers. Track your dead-role reports: a Yakuza-only role in one is strong evidence that player was Yakuza, and with the causes of death it tells you how many are left. Never ask in public where a trap or a protector is: only a killer needs that, and it is quoted back as the case against you. Never copy a role whose actions were already public or whose real holder may be among the unclaimed. If someone fake-claims your Unique role, counterclaim or open a private deal with them that Day. Avoid vouching for players, lone Guilty votes and aggressive endgame nominations, especially of a player who has said they are Cursed (the hang plus the curse is an obvious count drop). Count the threshold, max(floor(N/4),2) alive at the start of a Day, after every death. In the endgame, don't push a hang or vote that would let the town notice the count is about to hand you the win. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.56 | recent evidence: Moderator and Heidi: Heidi stayed unclaimed, nominated Erin on thin grounds, switched to a late Vanilla claim and was hanged 3-0 on D6.; Olivia: nominating Frank herself and pushing leads without results became the case against her, as the 'never lead a push or nominate' line predicts.
- [t166] (Serial Killer) Don't kill Yakuza early: each one you remove strengthens a large town against you. That includes players with duplicate or suspicious claims (a second Tracker whose result neatly matches a blocker's): they are often Yakuza, and they do your killing for you. Hit Village power roles that can read or stop you. A claimed Oracle comes first: a Sheriff reads you Not Evil, but once elimination narrows the killer to two or three players one 'Serial Killer' guess ends you. Then claimed Trackers and Lookouts, blockers, the Accountant, an Enforcer and a Kitsune holding a swallowed Strong attack. Watch who is being cleared: a player confirmed blocked on a night you killed is struck from the list, so a working blocker shrinks the pool onto you; kill or discredit them before it reaches two names. A claimed Sheriff is no threat to you and the Yakuza will kill them anyway: leave them to the Katana. Don't attack the player the Yakuza obviously want dead (e.g. a Lookout who just exposed their holder, a freshly claimed investigator); a double kill wastes your night, and if a protector stops it the victim and a Kitsune both learn a Serial Killer attacked. If your attack fails on a player nobody plausibly protected, it may be the hidden Narrow Escape trait, which works only once: attack them again on a later night (t370). Read each night's causes of death to see where the Katana goes. Don't volunteer a Vanilla claim early when nobody pressed you; it is the first claim exact-role checkers test once SK kills appear. But don't sit silent and unclaimed for Days either: the quiet player with a late Vanilla claim is the easy nominee, and the Yakuza will lead that hang to be rid of you. Take a visible stance from Day 2 (a named suspect with a reason) and answer the first direct question in one plain sentence (t188). On a small table, count: once the Yakuza need only one Village vote to hang you, push the visible Yakuza lead (e.g. a PM pair) yourself before they nominate you. Turn on the Yakuza only when the town is small. Plan the endgame on Defense: at its usual Standard level the Katana can't get through your Standard Defense, so the Yakuza can hang you, hit you with a Ritualist's Divine guess, or once per game raise the Katana to Strong or Divine if a member has the Katana Expert or Katana Master trait, which you can't see. A final two with the last Yakuza is a win if they hold no ritual tokens and no unused Katana upgrade. At four or five alive, let the Yakuza and the Village hang each other, quietly add your vote to a push on a Villager rather than defending anyone, and at two alive vote No on every nomination and kill. Ballots are secret, so you can state a cautious lean and cast the vote that thins the field, but at 5 or fewer alive the totals can show whose stated vote was false (t239). | support: 5 games, total weight 2.04 | recent evidence: Rupert killed Pharmacist Niaj N1 and Clerk Mallory N2, hit Alice alongside the Katana N3, volunteered Vanilla on D2, and was Oracle-hit and hanged D4. Rupert and the moderator.; Moderator's review: Erin's attacks on N2 and N3 landed on the same obvious investigators as the Katana, wasting both kills and showing the town an SK cause of death that got her hunted.; Dave (won): killed Tracker Bob N1 and Sheriff Carol N3, then Heidi and Alice. - [t324] (Serial Killer) An Enforcer's attack lands only on a player who attacks that night, and a Strong one (1 token) beats your Standard Defense. - [t12] (Arsonist) Ignite as soon as 3 or more living players are doused, or with 2 if anyone has started questioning you or your claim. If you have been put on trial and survived, ignite the very next night with whatever is doused. Every night spent dousing is a night the town can hang you first, each douse is one more unexplained visitor in a Fortune Teller's or Cartographer's counts, and a fake protective claim breaks the first time the night results don't fit it. Ignite is Astral: trap-safe and invisible to Fortune Teller counts. Douse players likely to survive, not today's lynch targets or the Katana's likely victims (the Day 1 agenda-setter, fresh investigator claimants): a douse that dies before the ignite is a wasted night. If one player is tracking the visit counts, douse them early so the ignite removes them. Count living douses before each night. The ignite's cause of death is public and proves you exist, so count who will be left: if it leaves 4 or fewer alive and you are the one without a detailed claim, you are the next hang. Ignite while the table is bigger, with a full claim and a target for every night already posted (t208). - [t13] (Arsonist) Ignite is a Strong Astral attack. It is invisible to Lookouts, Trackers, Insomniacs and Fortune Teller counts, but it fails against Strong Defense (a Medic's Strong token protection, a Diviner's Strong Defense on a Yakuza), and a Trapper's trap or a Kitsune's charm stops it too. The hidden Narrow Escape trait, which any player can have and nobody is told about, also saves its holder from one ignite. So don't douse a player who already survived an attack or an obvious ward (the N1 Katana survivor, a claimed investigator): a trap or charm is probably on them. Douse quiet players nobody guards. An ignite uses up every douse, so a player who survives it is no longer doused and must be doused again before a later ignite. Doused Yakuza and Neutrals are told an Arsonist doused them; Good players are not. Douse is a normal visit, so don't douse a player a Tracker or Lookout is likely watching or has publicly committed to. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.79 | recent evidence: Niaj's own analysis.; Heidi: her Strong ignite on N5 killed Ivan through Grace's Standard protection, and Frank with him.

- [t208] (Arsonist) Have a claim ready before Day 2 that explains your douse visits. Never deny acting, and never admit a vague 'I took an action elsewhere'. A Tracker cover that reports your real douse targets as 'tracks' fits Fortune Teller counts and Cartographer groups well and matches your 0 tokens, provided you claim every night's visit so the counts show no spare visitor. But it wears out: if your targets are passive roles, every result is 'visited nobody', the table calls the claim uncheckable within a few Days and hangs it once the Yakuza are gone, and you can't safely invent a destination for a player who does visit. So don't let the cover run that long: ignite early (t12). If you denied acting earlier, that denial becomes the town's standing contradiction against you. A Sleepwalker trait claim beside a tokenless Village role explains random-looking visits, and no investigation sees traits; describe it exactly (one random visit every night, and you aren't told whom), so never name the players you 'sleepwalked' to. - [t92] (Jester) Build a hard, Village-sounding case. A fake investigative counterclaim against a credible info role (e.g. a fake Tracker result contradicting a Lookout's list) is a fast route to a hang. A passive counting claim (Cartographer, Fortune Teller) with a bold, falsifiable result posted before anyone speaks ('one visit all night') works well: the real visitors contradict you, and the town reads a duel, not a Jester. You needn't hang on Day 2. Escalate into a specific accusation. If a Villager hangs on it, the night's events break your claim and the town hangs the 'proven liar' the next Day. Being the obvious next hang also keeps the Katana off you, which your Basic Defense wouldn't stop. Commit to the claim and reject the Roleplayer excuse the town offers. On trial, fight: accuse your accusers and say you vote Innocent while voting Guilty. One small checkable lie (a secret Yes on your own nomination, stated as No) tips waverers, but careful players read it as a Jester tell, so don't overdo it. A self-nomination framed as a 'test' can also work, but repeated self-nominations or an open Jester claim make the town refuse to hang you. | support: 9 games, total weight 1.79 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.17: Judy and Niaj: repeated self-nominations and an open Jester claim made the town refuse to hang Judy, and she lost.
